= Ztt Intrinsic Design v0.6
:toc:
:toclevels: 4

<<<

== 1. Data Type Design

=== 1.1 Type Naming

Matrix types use the following form:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_<type>_<shape>_t
----

Examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t
__riscv_ztt_u8_4x1_t
__riscv_ztt_f32_accx1_t
__riscv_ztt_i32_accx4_t
----

`<type>` denotes the element data type, and `<shape>` denotes the logical square shape. For accumulator types, `accxK` denotes an explicit accumulator-register shape.

M/Acc C types have no default-construction semantics. An M or Acc value must be produced by an intrinsic result, for example as a return value, or by a pseudo intrinsic with pointer output/in-out semantics. Any M/Acc value obtained by default construction is undefined and must not be used as a valid operand; for example, directly using the `a` obtained from `__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t a;` is undefined behavior.

=== 1.2 Basic Data Types

Integer datatype tokens include signedness, bit width, an optional integer rounding-mode suffix, and an optional saturation suffix:

[source,latex]
----
i4(_<irm>)(_sat), u4(_<irm>)(_sat),
i8(_<irm>)(_sat), u8(_<irm>)(_sat),
i16(_<irm>)(_sat), u16(_<irm>)(_sat),
i32(_<irm>)(_sat), u32(_<irm>)(_sat),
i64(_<irm>)(_sat), u64(_<irm>)(_sat),
i128(_<irm>)(_sat), u128(_<irm>)(_sat)
----

`<irm>` is one of `rnu`, `rne`, `rdn`, or `rod`, corresponding to the integer rounding modes `RNU`, `RNE`, `RDN`, and `ROD`. If the rounding suffix is omitted, the datatype uses integer rounding-mode encoding 0, namely `rnu`; for example, `i8` is equivalent to `i8_rnu`, and `i8_sat` is equivalent to `i8_rnu_sat`. If `_sat` is omitted, the datatype uses the non-saturating integer encoding, namely natural overflow mode.

Floating-point datatype tokens include an optional floating-point rounding-mode suffix:

[source,latex]
----
f16(_<frm>), bf16(_<frm>), f32(_<frm>), f64(_<frm>)
----

`<frm>` is one of `rne`, `rtz`, `rdn`, `rup`, `rmm`, or `rno`, corresponding to the floating-point rounding modes `RNE`, `RTZ`, `RDN`, `RUP`, `RMM`, and `RNO`. If the suffix is omitted, the datatype uses floating-point rounding-mode encoding 0, namely `rne`; for example, `f32` is equivalent to `f32_rne`.

=== 1.3 Shapes

M register types support:

[source,latex]
----
1x1
1x2, 1x4, 1x8, 1x16, 1x32
2x1, 4x1, 8x1, 16x1, 32x1
----

Acc register types support:

[source,latex]
----
accx1, accx2, accx4, accx8, accx16
----

`1xK` denotes K squares stacked horizontally, and is normally used as the left-hand operand of matrix multiplication. `Kx1` denotes K squares stacked vertically, and is normally used as the right-hand operand of matrix multiplication.

Each physical ACC register always stores one complete accumulator square, independent of the M-register UDS value and the accumulator element datatype. Therefore, `accxK` means exactly K consecutive ACC registers and K logical accumulator squares.

Because the current datatype sizes, M shapes, and UDS values are all powers of two, an M logical shape always maps to an integer number of physical M registers. There is no case where an M shape occupies, for example, two and a half registers.

=== 1.4 Hardware Profile

The compiler-visible Ztt hardware profile describes structural machine parameters that affect C type validity, register allocation, and lowering. `UDS`, the number of M registers, and the number of ACC registers are compile-time constants. `N` is a runtime constant read from the machine state. Supported instruction datatype tuples are not part of the compiler-visible profile.

==== 1.4.1 N

`N` is the runtime square dimension. Each logical square is `N x N`. C intrinsic type names do not encode `N`; the same source type denotes the same logical shape across machines with different runtime `N` values.

==== 1.4.2 UDS

`UDS = AME_UNIT_DATATYPE_SIZE` is part of the compiler profile and must be a compile-time constant. The recommended representation is a target feature that specifies the exact UDS value, such as:

[source,latex]
----
zttuds8b, zttuds16b, zttuds32b, zttuds64b
----

`zttuds32b` means exactly `UDS=32`, not a minimum value. The frontend must use UDS to classify each C type as a unit type, packed type, wide type, half-register shape, or logical concatenation shape.

==== 1.4.3 Number of M Registers

The number of architectural M registers is part of the compiler profile and must be a compile-time constant. The compiler uses it for register allocation, legality checks for concatenation shapes, and decisions about whether pseudo intrinsics require lowering into smaller physical units.

==== 1.4.4 Number of ACC Registers

The number of architectural ACC registers is part of the compiler profile and must be a compile-time constant. It determines which `accxK` accumulator shapes are legal for the target profile.

==== 1.4.5 Datatype Tuples

Supported instruction datatype tuples are hardware capabilities provided by the hardware vendor to software developers. They are not part of the compiler profile described above. The spec explicitly does not guarantee binary compatibility across implementations: a binary compiled for one vendor's supported tuple set is not required to run successfully on another vendor's machine.

=== 1.5 Half-Register Shapes and Concatenation Shapes

Half-register shape: the logical shape covers only part of a physical M register. For example, on a `UDS=16` machine, `i8_1x1` is a half-register shape. The current rule is to reject all such shapes. The frontend should diagnose them directly.

Concatenation shape: the logical shape consists of multiple squares and is physically formed by concatenating multiple registers. For example, on a `UDS=16` machine, `i16_1x2` is a concatenation shape. Note that such shapes may require the compiler to split the operation into multiple operations over smaller physical units to achieve the same logical effect. Currently, permutation intrinsics other than zip/unzip and the matrix-construction intrinsics `mrowid.ew` and `mcolid.ew` disallow concatenation shapes by instruction design (those intrinsics only accept `1x1` square shapes). Zip/unzip accept only the ``1x2`` shape. All other categories allow concatenation shapes. This rule may be extended as the public intrinsic set evolves.

=== 1.6 Notation

[source,latex]
----
T        Any supported basic data type token
Tint     Integer data type
Tsint    Signed integer data type
Tfloat   Floating-point data type
Tnum     Numeric data type, either integer or floating-point
Tord     Ordered numeric datatype, currently including all standard integer, saturating integer, and floating-point datatypes for which the operation tuple is supported. Custom datatypes may not be ordered numeric datatypes; they are Tord only when a comparison order is explicitly defined.
S        M shape: 1x1/1x2/1x4/1x8/1x16/1x32/2x1/4x1/8x1/16x1/32x1
Sb       Basic shape: neither a half-register shape nor a concatenation shape
K        1/2/4/8/16/32
SA       Acc shape: accx1/accx2/accx4/accx8/accx16
Scalar<T> Scalar C type carried by an X register
C<T>     C element type corresponding to datatype token T
----

=== 1.7 Scalar Types

4-bit datatypes have no directly corresponding C type, so no 4-bit ``Scalar<T>`` type is provided; all data-scalar interfaces (including ``mbcast.m.x``) reject 4-bit data scalars. This restriction does not constrain matrix operand datatypes or change the type rules for control scalars. Scalar intrinsics accept only dedicated scalar typedefs, for example:

[source,c]
----
typedef int32_t  __riscv_ztt_i32_scalar_t;
typedef uint32_t __riscv_ztt_u32_scalar_t;
typedef int16_t  __riscv_ztt_i16_scalar_t;
typedef double   __riscv_ztt_f64_scalar_t;
----

`.ew.x` data-scalar instructions require the compiler to set `amestype` from `Scalar<T>`. Control scalars, such as shift amounts, indices, offsets, strides, and packed slot selectors, do not read `amestype`; for real Ztt instructions they are ordinary X-register numeric operands unless the instruction description explicitly says the value is encoded as an immediate.

=== 1.8 C Source Compatibility Across Hardware Profiles

Ztt C source may not be compatible across all profile configurations, but the compiler can provide compatibility across some machines.

On one side, some instruction datatype tuples, such as `madd.ew` with `f16` and `u32` inputs producing a `bf16` result, may be supported on some machines and unsupported on others. Running code that uses an unsupported tuple may produce an incorrect result or trap.

On the other side, the compiler can lower concatenation types into multiple operations over smaller physical units. Therefore, C code that is valid and executes correctly for one UDS value may also compile and run on a smaller-UDS machine, although the generated assembly may differ. The same code is not necessarily compatible with a larger-UDS machine, mainly because the source-level types may become half-register types.

This compatibility rule is an intrinsic design policy and may be extended as the public intrinsic set evolves.

=== 1.9 Calling Convention

Provisionally, all M/ACC registers, their associated Md/Ad datatype state, and Ztt-related CSRs are treated as caller-saved. The calling convention does not require the callee to preserve this state, and the caller must not assume that it remains unchanged across a function call; this does not alter architectural CSR access permissions.

If the caller needs existing M/ACC values or Ztt state after a function call, the caller and compiler are responsible for saving, restoring, or reconstructing the required state, rather than relying directly on the pre-call register contents.

No argument or return-value registers are currently defined for passing M/ACC values, so passing M/ACC values between C functions through parameters or return values is not currently supported.

As the instruction set and compiler support evolve, a dedicated calling convention variant may be defined following the RVV approach, specifying how M/ACC arguments and return values are passed and which registers and CSRs must be preserved by each party.

== 2. General Naming Rules

Intrinsic names use the `__riscv_ztt` prefix:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_<instruction_name_with_underscores>_<return_type>_<return_shape>(...)
----

Examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_madd_ew_i32_1x1(...)
__riscv_ztt_mmulacc_2d_bf16_accx1(...)
__riscv_ztt_mls_rm_i16_1x2(...)
----

Store intrinsics that return `void` do not encode a return type or shape in the intrinsic name; the C type of the source operand determines the stored datatype and shape.

The compiler is responsible for inserting `msettyp/asettyp` according to C types, and for setting `amestype` before `.ew.x` data-scalar instructions. Note that both `msettyp` and `asettyp` clear the corresponding data state.

<<<

== 3. Pseudo Intrinsics

Pseudo intrinsics provide additional utility functions to assist users in manipulating across intrinsic types. They do not map to any specific Ztt instruction. The specific mapping to actual instructions is given in the description of each pseudo intrinsic.

=== 3.1 Compiler Utility Interfaces

[cols=",,,",options="header",]
|=======================================================================
|Interface name |Pseudo intrinsic |Input/output types |Description
|xref:ztt-detail-mextract[`mextract`] |+__riscv_ztt_<T>_<Sdst>_t __riscv_ztt_mextract_<T>_<Sdst>(__riscv_ztt_<T>_<Ssrc>_t a, size_t index)+ |`M<T,Sdst> <- M<T,Ssrc>, size_t index` |Extracts the left/right half of a larger same-datatype concatenation shape.

|xref:ztt-detail-mconcat[`mconcat`] |+__riscv_ztt_<T>_<Sdst>_t __riscv_ztt_mconcat_m_<T>_<Sdst>(__riscv_ztt_<T>_<Ssrc>_t a, __riscv_ztt_<T>_<Ssrc>_t b)+ |`M<T,Sdst> <- M<T,Ssrc>, M<T,Ssrc>` |Concatenates two M values with exactly identical shapes and datatypes into a same-datatype M value with twice the size.

|xref:ztt-detail-mcopy_m2m[`mcopy_m2m`] |+__riscv_ztt_<T>_<S>_t __riscv_ztt_mcopy_m2m_<T>_<S>(__riscv_ztt_<T>_<S>_t a)+ |`M<T,S> <- M<T,S>` |Copies an M value.

|xref:ztt-detail-mcopy_a2m[`mcopy_a2m`] |+__riscv_ztt_<T>_<S>_t __riscv_ztt_mcopy_a2m_<T>_<S>(__riscv_ztt_<T>_<SA>_t a)+ |`M<T,S> <- Acc<T,SA>` |Copies an Acc value into an M value.

|xref:ztt-detail-mcopy_m2a[`mcopy_m2a`] |+__riscv_ztt_<T>_<SA>_t __riscv_ztt_mcopy_m2a_<T>_<SA>(__riscv_ztt_<T>_<S>_t a)+ |`Acc<T,SA> <- M<T,S>` |Copies an M value into an Acc value.

|xref:ztt-detail-mclear_m[`mclear_m`] |+__riscv_ztt_<T>_<S>_t __riscv_ztt_mclear_m_<T>_<S>(void)+ |`M<T,S>` |Constructs a cleared M value.

|xref:ztt-detail-mclear_acc[`mclear_acc`] |+__riscv_ztt_<T>_<SA>_t __riscv_ztt_mclear_acc_<T>_<SA>(void)+ |`Acc<T,SA>` |Constructs a cleared Acc value.

|xref:ztt-detail-mzero_m[`mzero_m`] |+__riscv_ztt_<T>_<S>_t __riscv_ztt_mzero_m_<T>_<S>(void)+ |`M<T,S>` |Constructs an M value with every element set to the target datatype's numeric zero.

|xref:ztt-detail-mzero_acc[`mzero_acc`] |+__riscv_ztt_<T>_<SA>_t __riscv_ztt_mzero_acc_<T>_<SA>(void)+ |`Acc<T,SA>` |Constructs an Acc value with every element set to the target datatype's numeric zero.
|=======================================================================

[[ztt-detail-mextract]]
==== `mextract`

Extracts one `M<T,Sdst>` half from an `M<T,Ssrc>` source, where the source is a same-datatype concatenation shape that is exactly twice the destination shape. `index == 0` selects the left half for `1xK` shapes, or the upper half for `Kx1` shapes. `index == 1` selects the right half for `1xK` shapes, or the lower half for `Kx1` shapes. This is a compiler utility pseudo intrinsic and is lowered by the compiler to `mmov`-class copies.

C intrinsic data types: Source operand `src` is `__riscv_ztt_<T>_<Ssrc>_t`, and result `dst` is `__riscv_ztt_<T>_<Sdst>_t`, and `index` is a `size_t` unsigned immediate or compile-time constant selecting the first or second half.

Type constraints: The source `src` and result `dst` element datatypes must be [.red]##identical##. `Ssrc` must be exactly twice `Sdst` along the same concatenation direction, such as `1x4 -> 1x2` or `4x1 -> 2x1`. `index` must be `0` or `1`.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
src1 = mextract([src1; src2], 0)
src2 = mextract([src1; src2], 1)
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mextract_i16_1x2(src_i16_1x4, 0);
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mextract_i16_1x2(src_i16_1x4, 1);
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mextract_i32_1x1(src_i32_1x2, 1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_2x1_t __riscv_ztt_mextract_bf16_2x1(src_bf16_4x1, 0);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mextract_i16_1x2(src_i32_1x4, 0); // Source and result element types differ
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mextract_i16_1x2(src_i16_1x8, 0); // Result is not half the source
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mextract_i16_1x2(src_i16_4x1, 0); // Shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mextract_i16_1x2(src_i16_1x4, 2); // index must be 0 or 1
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mextract_i16_1x2(src_i16_1x4, runtime_index); // index must be a compile-time constant
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x4_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mextract_i16_1x2(src, 1);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m2, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mmov.m.m m2, m1      # m2 <- m1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m4, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m5, a0
mmov.m.m m4, m2      # m4 <- m2
mmov.m.m m5, m3      # m5 <- m3

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m7
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m8, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m10, a0
mmov.m.m m8, m4      # m8-m9 <- m4-m5
mmov.m.m m10, m6     # m10-m11 <- m6-m7
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mconcat]]
==== `mconcat`

Concatenates two `M<T,Ssrc>` source operands into one `M<T,Sdst>` result. It is the reverse operation of `mextract`. For `1xK` shapes, `src1` becomes the left half and `src2` becomes the right half. For `Kx1` shapes, `src1` becomes the upper half and `src2` becomes the lower half. `1x1` can be concatenated in either direction, with the result direction determined by `Sdst`, such as `1x1 + 1x1 -> 1x2` or `1x1 + 1x1 -> 2x1`. This is a compiler utility pseudo intrinsic and is lowered by the compiler to `mmov`-class copies. To guarantee that the concatenation shape uses consecutive M registers, the compiler must copy the two inputs into consecutive M-register space.

C intrinsic data types: Source operands `src1` and `src2` are `__riscv_ztt_<T>_<Ssrc>_t`, and result `dst` is `__riscv_ztt_<T>_<Sdst>_t`.

Type constraints: Source `src1`, source `src2`, and result `dst` must have [.red]##identical## element datatypes; `src1` and `src2` must have [.red]##identical## shapes. `Sdst` must be exactly twice `Ssrc` along the same concatenation direction, such as `1x2 + 1x2 -> 1x4` or `2x1 + 2x1 -> 4x1`; shapes from different directions cannot be mixed, such as `1x2` with `2x1`. When `Ssrc` is `1x1`, `Sdst` may be either `1x2` or `2x1`.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
[src1; src2] = mconcat(src1, src2)
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x4_t __riscv_ztt_mconcat_m_i16_1x4(src1_i16_1x2, src2_i16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mconcat_m_bf16_4x1(src1_bf16_2x1, src2_bf16_2x1);
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mconcat_m_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x1, src2_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_i32_2x1_t __riscv_ztt_mconcat_m_i32_2x1(src1_i32_1x1, src2_i32_1x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x4_t __riscv_ztt_mconcat_m_i16_1x4(src1_i16_1x2, src2_i32_1x2); // Input element types differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x4_t __riscv_ztt_mconcat_m_i32_1x4(src1_i16_1x2, src2_i16_1x2); // Input and result element types differ
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x4_t __riscv_ztt_mconcat_m_i16_1x4(src1_i16_1x2, src2_i16_1x4); // Input shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x4_t __riscv_ztt_mconcat_m_i16_1x4(src1_i16_1x2, src2_i16_2x1); // Input shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x8_t __riscv_ztt_mconcat_m_i16_1x8(src1_i16_1x2, src2_i16_1x2); // Result shape is not twice the input
__riscv_ztt_i16_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mconcat_m_i16_4x1(src1_i16_1x2, src2_i16_1x2); // Result shape orientation differs
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x4_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mconcat_m_i16_1x4(src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1
# result: m2-m3
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m2, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m3, a0
mmov.m.m m2, m0      # m2 <- m0
mmov.m.m m3, m1      # m3 <- m1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m3
# result: m4-m7
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m4, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m5, a0
msettyp m6, a0
msettyp m7, a0
mmov.m.m m4, m0      # m4 <- m0
mmov.m.m m5, m1      # m5 <- m1
mmov.m.m m6, m2      # m6 <- m2
mmov.m.m m7, m3      # m7 <- m3

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m7
# result: m8-m15
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m8, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m10, a0
msettyp m12, a0
msettyp m14, a0
mmov.m.m m8, m0      # m8-m9 <- m0-m1
mmov.m.m m10, m2     # m10-m11 <- m2-m3
mmov.m.m m12, m4     # m12-m13 <- m4-m5
mmov.m.m m14, m6     # m14-m15 <- m6-m7
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mcopy_m2m]]
==== `mcopy_m2m`

Copies one M C value, corresponding to `msettyp` and `mmov.m.m`.

C intrinsic data types: Source `src` and result `dst` are both `M<T,S>`.

Type constraints: Source `src` and destination `dst` datatypes must be [.red]##identical##, and shapes must be [.red]##identical##. Concatenation shapes are allowed; half-register shapes are rejected by the general type rules.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst = src
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcopy_m2m_i32_1x1(src_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcopy_m2m_bf16_1x2(src_bf16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_f32_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcopy_m2m_f32_4x1(src_f32_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcopy_m2m_i16_1x2(src_i32_1x2); // Source and result element types differ
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcopy_m2m_i16_1x2(src_i16_1x4); // Source and result shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcopy_m2m_i16_1x2(acc_i16_accx2); // Source must be an M value
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mcopy_m2m_i16_1x2(src);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0
# result: m1
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m1, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mmov.m.m m1, m0      # m1 <- m0

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# result: m2-m3
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m2, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m3, a0
mmov.m.m m2, m0      # m2 <- m0
mmov.m.m m3, m1      # m3 <- m1

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# result: m4-m7
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m4, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m6, a0
mmov.m.m m4, m0      # m4-m5 <- m0-m1
mmov.m.m m6, m2      # m6-m7 <- m2-m3
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mcopy_a2m]]
==== `mcopy_a2m`

Copies an Acc C value into an M C value, corresponding to `msettyp` and `mmov.m.a`.

C intrinsic data types: Result `dst` is `M<T,S>` and source `acc` is `Acc<T,SA>`.

Type constraints: Source `acc` and destination `dst` datatypes must be [.red]##identical##. The Acc shape must contain the [.red]##same## number of squares as the M shape. Concatenation shapes are allowed; half-register shapes are rejected by the general type rules.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst = acc
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcopy_a2m_i32_1x1(acc_i32_accx1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcopy_a2m_bf16_1x2(acc_bf16_accx2);
__riscv_ztt_f32_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcopy_a2m_f32_4x1(acc_f32_accx4);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcopy_a2m_i16_1x2(acc_i32_accx2); // Source and result element types differ
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcopy_a2m_i16_1x2(acc_i16_accx4); // Source and result square counts differ
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcopy_a2m_i16_1x2(src_i16_1x2); // Source must be an ACC value
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_accx2_t acc = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mcopy_a2m_i16_1x2(acc);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# acc: acc0-acc1
# result: m0
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m0, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mmov.m.a m0, acc0    # m0 <- acc0-acc1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# acc: acc0-acc1
# result: m0-m1
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m0, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m1, a0
mmov.m.a m0, acc0    # m0 <- acc0
mmov.m.a m1, acc1    # m1 <- acc1

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# acc: acc0-acc1
# result: m0-m3
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m0, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m2, a0
mmov.m.a m0, acc0    # m0-m1 <- acc0
mmov.m.a m2, acc1    # m2-m3 <- acc1
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mcopy_m2a]]
==== `mcopy_m2a`

Copies an M C value into an Acc C value, corresponding to `asettyp` and `mmov.a.m`.

C intrinsic data types: Result `acc` is `Acc<T,SA>` and source `src` is `M<T,S>`.

Type constraints: Source `src` and destination `acc` datatypes must be [.red]##identical##. The Acc shape must contain the [.red]##same## number of squares as the M shape. Concatenation shapes are allowed; half-register shapes are rejected by the general type rules.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
acc = src
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_accx1_t __riscv_ztt_mcopy_m2a_i32_accx1(src_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_accx2_t __riscv_ztt_mcopy_m2a_bf16_accx2(src_bf16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_f32_accx4_t __riscv_ztt_mcopy_m2a_f32_accx4(src_f32_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i16_accx2_t __riscv_ztt_mcopy_m2a_i16_accx2(src_i32_1x2); // Source and result element types differ
__riscv_ztt_i16_accx2_t __riscv_ztt_mcopy_m2a_i16_accx2(src_i16_1x4); // Source and result square counts differ
__riscv_ztt_i16_accx2_t __riscv_ztt_mcopy_m2a_i16_accx2(acc_i16_accx2); // Source must be an M value
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_accx2_t acc = __riscv_ztt_mcopy_m2a_i16_accx2(src);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0
# result: acc0-acc1
# Assembly mapping:

asettyp acc0, a0     # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
asettyp acc1, a0
mmov.a.m acc0, m0    # acc0-acc1 <- m0

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# result: acc0-acc1
# Assembly mapping:

asettyp acc0, a0     # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
asettyp acc1, a0
mmov.a.m acc0, m0    # acc0 <- m0
mmov.a.m acc1, m1    # acc1 <- m1

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# result: acc0-acc1
# Assembly mapping:

asettyp acc0, a0     # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
asettyp acc1, a0
mmov.a.m acc0, m0    # acc0 <- m0-m1
mmov.a.m acc1, m2    # acc1 <- m2-m3
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mclear_m]]
==== `mclear_m`

Clears every bit of the M registers corresponding to the C value. This differs from the `mzero_m` interface, which constructs a value whose elements are all numeric zero: it directly sets every physical storage bit to 0 rather than setting each unit element to the target datatype's numeric zero (because numeric zero in some datatypes is not necessarily an all-zero bit pattern). In the logical operation below, `0` denotes an all-zero bit pattern.

C intrinsic data types: Result `dst` is `M<T,S>`.

Type constraints: The destination `dst` may have any supported datatype. Concatenation shapes are allowed; half-register shapes are rejected by the general type rules.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst = 0
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mclear_m_i32_1x1(void);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mclear_m_bf16_1x2(void);
__riscv_ztt_f32_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mclear_m_f32_4x1(void);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i16_2x2_t __riscv_ztt_mclear_m_i16_2x2(); // Unsupported 2x2 shape
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mclear_m_i16_1x2(src_i16_1x2); // Interface takes no arguments
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mclear_m_i16_1x2();

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# result: m0
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m0, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding; m0 is cleared

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# result: m0-m1
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m0, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding; m0 is cleared
msettyp m1, a0       # m1 is cleared

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# result: m0-m3
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m0, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding; m0-m1 are cleared
msettyp m2, a0       # m2-m3 are cleared
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mclear_acc]]
==== `mclear_acc`

Clears every bit of the ACC registers corresponding to the C value. This differs from the `mzero_acc` interface, which constructs a value whose elements are all numeric zero: it directly sets every physical storage bit to 0 rather than setting each unit element to the target datatype's numeric zero (because numeric zero in some datatypes is not necessarily an all-zero bit pattern). In the logical operation below, `0` denotes an all-zero bit pattern.

C intrinsic data types: Result `acc` is `Acc<T,SA>`.

Type constraints: The destination `acc` may have any supported datatype and any supported `accxK` shape.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
acc = 0
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_accx1_t __riscv_ztt_mclear_acc_i32_accx1(void);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_accx2_t __riscv_ztt_mclear_acc_bf16_accx2(void);
__riscv_ztt_f32_accx4_t __riscv_ztt_mclear_acc_f32_accx4(void);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i16_accx3_t __riscv_ztt_mclear_acc_i16_accx3(); // Unsupported accx3 shape
__riscv_ztt_i16_accx2_t __riscv_ztt_mclear_acc_i16_accx2(acc_i16_accx2); // Interface takes no arguments
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_accx2_t acc = __riscv_ztt_mclear_acc_i16_accx2();

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# result: acc0-acc1
# Assembly mapping:

asettyp acc0, a0     # a0 holds the i16 type encoding; acc0 is cleared
asettyp acc1, a0     # acc1 is cleared

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# result: acc0-acc1
# Assembly mapping:

asettyp acc0, a0     # a0 holds the i16 type encoding; acc0 is cleared
asettyp acc1, a0     # acc1 is cleared

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# result: acc0-acc1
# Assembly mapping:

asettyp acc0, a0     # a0 holds the i16 type encoding; acc0 is cleared
asettyp acc1, a0     # acc1 is cleared
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mzero_m]]
==== `mzero_m`

Constructs an M C value with every unit element set to the target datatype's numeric zero, unlike `mclear_m`, which clears physical storage bits. The compiler first uses `msettyp` to set the destination datatype, then uses `mzero.2d.m` to set every element to that datatype's numeric zero.

C intrinsic data types: Result `dst` is `M<T,S>`.

Type constraints: The destination `dst` may have any datatype supported by the corresponding `mzero.2d.m` operation. Concatenation shapes are allowed; half-register shapes are rejected by the general type rules.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst = zero(dst_type)
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mzero_m_i32_1x1(void);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mzero_m_bf16_1x2(void);
__riscv_ztt_f32_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mzero_m_f32_4x1(void);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i16_2x2_t __riscv_ztt_mzero_m_i16_2x2(); // Unsupported 2x2 shape
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mzero_m_i16_1x2(src_i16_1x2); // Interface takes no arguments
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mzero_m_i16_1x2();

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# result: m0
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m0, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mzero.2d.m m0  # m0 <- zero(dst_type)

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# result: m0-m1
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m0, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m1, a0
mzero.2d.m m0  # m0 <- zero(dst_type)
mzero.2d.m m1  # m1 <- zero(dst_type)

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# result: m0-m3
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m0, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m2, a0
mzero.2d.m m0  # m0-m1 <- zero(dst_type)
mzero.2d.m m2  # m2-m3 <- zero(dst_type)
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mzero_acc]]
==== `mzero_acc`

Constructs an Acc C value with every unit element set to the target datatype's numeric zero, unlike `mclear_acc`, which clears physical storage bits. The compiler first uses `asettyp` to set the destination datatype, then uses `mzero.2d.acc` to set every element to that datatype's numeric zero.

C intrinsic data types: Result `acc` is `Acc<T,SA>`.

Type constraints: The destination `acc` may have any datatype supported by the corresponding `mzero.2d.acc` operation and any supported `accxK` shape.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
acc = zero(acc_type)
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_accx1_t __riscv_ztt_mzero_acc_i32_accx1(void);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_accx2_t __riscv_ztt_mzero_acc_bf16_accx2(void);
__riscv_ztt_f32_accx4_t __riscv_ztt_mzero_acc_f32_accx4(void);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i16_accx3_t __riscv_ztt_mzero_acc_i16_accx3(); // Unsupported accx3 shape
__riscv_ztt_i16_accx2_t __riscv_ztt_mzero_acc_i16_accx2(acc_i16_accx2); // Interface takes no arguments
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_accx2_t acc = __riscv_ztt_mzero_acc_i16_accx2();

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# result: acc0-acc1
# Assembly mapping:

asettyp acc0, a0     # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
asettyp acc1, a0
mzero.2d.acc acc0  # acc0 <- zero(acc_type)
mzero.2d.acc acc1  # acc1 <- zero(acc_type)

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# result: acc0-acc1
# Assembly mapping:

asettyp acc0, a0     # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
asettyp acc1, a0
mzero.2d.acc acc0  # acc0 <- zero(acc_type)
mzero.2d.acc acc1  # acc1 <- zero(acc_type)

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# result: acc0-acc1
# Assembly mapping:

asettyp acc0, a0     # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
asettyp acc1, a0
mzero.2d.acc acc0  # acc0 <- zero(acc_type)
mzero.2d.acc acc1  # acc1 <- zero(acc_type)
----

[[ztt-detail-ameown]]
==== `ameown`

Reads the `ameown` CSR. The compiler maps this interface to `csrr rd, ameown`.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic returns integer scalar `result` and has no matrix or accumulator operand.

Type constraints: The interface is a state helper and does not imply ownership changes or datatype changes.

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
size_t __riscv_ztt_get_ameown(void);
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
size_t result = __riscv_ztt_get_ameown();

----------------
# Operand register allocation:
# result: a0
# Assembly mapping:

csrr a0, ameown
----

<<<

== 4. Resource Management

=== 4.1 Resource Management

[cols=",,,",options="header",]
|=======================================================================
|Spec instruction |Intrinsic |Input/output types |Type constraints
|xref:ztt-detail-ame_acquire[`ame.acquire`] |`size_t __riscv_ztt_ame_acquire(size_t desc)`
|Input acquire descriptor; returns the acquire result word
|Non-matrix type; requires the current context to be allowed to acquire the AME backend

|xref:ztt-detail-ame_release[`ame.release`] |`void __riscv_ztt_ame_release(void)` |No input or output
|Releases the AME backend owned by the current context
|=======================================================================

<<<

[[ztt-detail-ame_acquire]]
==== `ame.acquire`

Requests ownership of an AME backend and returns the acquisition result word. It maps directly to the spec instruction `ame.acquire`.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic uses ordinary integer scalar types: input descriptor `desc` and return value `result` are both `size_t`.

Type constraints: The current context must have AME state enabled and must be allowed by the platform to acquire the backend.

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
size_t __riscv_ztt_ame_acquire((size_t)desc);
size_t __riscv_ztt_ame_acquire(0);
size_t __riscv_ztt_ame_acquire(0x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
size_t __riscv_ztt_ame_acquire(); // Missing desc argument
size_t __riscv_ztt_ame_acquire(src_i32_1x1); // desc cannot be an M value
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
size_t desc = ...;
size_t result = __riscv_ztt_ame_acquire(desc);

----------------
# Operand register allocation:
# desc: a0
# result: a1
# Assembly mapping:

ame.acquire a1, a0   # a1 <- a0
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-ame_release]]
==== `ame.release`

Releases the AME backend owned by the current context. It maps directly to the spec instruction `ame.release`.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic has no matrix, accumulator, or scalar data operand and returns `void`.

Type constraints: If the current context does not own the backend, the operation is a no-op as defined by the spec.

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
void __riscv_ztt_ame_release(void);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
void __riscv_ztt_ame_release(0); // Interface takes no arguments
size_t result = __riscv_ztt_ame_release(); // Interface returns no value
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_ame_release();

----------------
# Assembly mapping:

ame.release
----


<<<

== 5. 2D Matrix Multiplication

General forms:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_<TD>_<SA>_t
__riscv_ztt_<op>_<TD>_<SA>(__riscv_ztt_<TD>_<SA>_t acc,
                           M<TA,1xK> a, M<TB,Kx1> b);
----

Matrix multiplication accumulator operands use `Acc<TD,SA>`, where `SA` is an explicit `accxK` accumulator shape.

=== 5.1 2D Matrix Multiplication

[cols=",,,",options="header",]
|=======================================================================
|Spec instruction |Intrinsic |Input/output types |Type constraints

[[ztt-detail-mmulacc_2d]]
==== `mmulacc.2d`

Accumulates a matrix product into an existing accumulator. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mmulacc.2d`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `acc` is `Acc<TD,SA>`. The intrinsic takes input accumulator `old_acc=Acc<TD,SA>`, source `src1=M<TA,1xK>`, and source `src2=M<TB,Kx1>`.

Type constraints: `TA/TB/TD` must be a supported numeric operation tuple. The datatypes and shapes of `old_acc` and `acc` must be [.red]##identical##. `src1` and `src2` must have matching `K` values. Concatenation shapes are allowed and half-register shapes are rejected.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
acc = old_acc + src1 × src2
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_accx1_t __riscv_ztt_mmulacc_2d_i32_accx1(old_acc_i32_accx1, src1_i16_1x2, src2_i16_2x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_accx2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulacc_2d_bf16_accx2(old_acc_bf16_accx2, src1_bf16_1x4, src2_bf16_4x1);
__riscv_ztt_f64_accx4_t __riscv_ztt_mmulacc_2d_f64_accx4(old_acc_f64_accx4, src1_i8_1x4, src2_f32_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i16_accx2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulacc_2d_i16_accx2(old_acc_f32_accx2, src1_i16_1x2, src2_i32_2x1); // Accumulator datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_i16_accx2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulacc_2d_i16_accx2(old_acc_i16_accx1, src1_i16_1x2, src2_i32_2x1); // Accumulator shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i16_accx2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulacc_2d_i16_accx2(old_acc_i16_accx2, src1_i16_2x1, src2_i32_2x1); // src1 must be 1xK
__riscv_ztt_i16_accx2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulacc_2d_i16_accx2(old_acc_i16_accx2, src1_i16_1x2, src2_i32_1x2); // src2 must be Kx1
__riscv_ztt_i16_accx2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulacc_2d_i16_accx2(old_acc_i16_accx2, src1_i16_1x2, src2_i32_4x1); // Input K values differ
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_accx2_t old_acc = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_2x1_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_accx2_t acc = __riscv_ztt_mmulacc_2d_i16_accx2(old_acc, src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# old_acc/acc: acc0-acc1 (acc1 remains unchanged)
# src1: m0
# src2: m1-m2
# Assembly mapping:

mmulacc.2d acc0, m0, m1  # acc0 <- acc0, m0, m1-m2

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# old_acc/acc: acc0-acc1 (acc1 remains unchanged)
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m5
# Assembly mapping:

mmulacc.2d acc0, m0, m2  # acc0 <- acc0, m0, m2-m3
mmulacc.2d acc0, m1, m4  # acc0 <- acc0, m1, m4-m5

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# old_acc/acc: acc0-acc1 (acc1 remains unchanged)
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m11
# Assembly mapping:

mmulacc.2d acc0, m0, m4  # acc0 <- acc0, m0-m1, m4-m7
mmulacc.2d acc0, m2, m8  # acc0 <- acc0, m2-m3, m8-m11
----

[[ztt-detail-mmulatacc_2d]]
==== `mmulatacc.2d`

Accumulates a transpose-A matrix product into an existing accumulator. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mmulatacc.2d`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `acc` is `Acc<TD,SA>`. The intrinsic takes input accumulator `old_acc=Acc<TD,SA>` and M source operands `src1` and `src2` with the same-direction transpose shape required by this instruction.

Type constraints: `TA/TB/TD` must be a supported numeric operation tuple. The datatypes and shapes of `old_acc` and `acc` must be [.red]##identical##. Source operands `src1` and `src2` must follow this instruction's transpose shape rule and have matching `K` values. Concatenation shapes are allowed and half-register shapes are rejected.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
acc = old_acc + sum(transpose(src1[s]) × src2[s], s = 0 .. K-1)
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_accx1_t __riscv_ztt_mmulatacc_2d_i32_accx1(old_acc_i32_accx1, src1_i16_1x2, src2_i16_1x2);

----

[source,c]
----
acc = old_acc - sum(src1[s] × transpose(src2[s]), s = 0 .. K-1)
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_accx1_t __riscv_ztt_mmulbtaccneg_2d_i32_accx1(old_acc_i32_accx1, src1_i16_1x2, src2_i16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_accx2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulbtaccneg_2d_bf16_accx2(old_acc_bf16_accx2, src1_bf16_4x1, src2_bf16_4x1);
__riscv_ztt_f64_accx4_t __riscv_ztt_mmulbtaccneg_2d_f64_accx4(old_acc_f64_accx4, src1_i8_1x4, src2_f32_1x4);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i16_accx2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulbtaccneg_2d_i16_accx2(old_acc_f32_accx2, src1_i16_1x2, src2_i32_1x2); // Accumulator datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_i16_accx2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulbtaccneg_2d_i16_accx2(old_acc_i16_accx1, src1_i16_1x2, src2_i32_1x2); // Accumulator shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i16_accx2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulbtaccneg_2d_i16_accx2(old_acc_i16_accx2, src1_i16_1x2, src2_i32_2x1); // Inputs must have the same orientation
__riscv_ztt_i16_accx2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulbtaccneg_2d_i16_accx2(old_acc_i16_accx2, src1_i16_1x2, src2_i32_1x4); // Input K values differ
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_accx2_t old_acc = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_accx2_t acc = __riscv_ztt_mmulbtaccneg_2d_i16_accx2(old_acc, src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# old_acc/acc: acc0-acc1 (acc1 remains unchanged)
# src1: m0
# src2: m1-m2
# Assembly mapping:

mmulbtaccneg.2d acc0, m0, m1  # acc0 <- acc0, m0, m1-m2

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# old_acc/acc: acc0-acc1 (acc1 remains unchanged)
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m5
# Assembly mapping:

mmulbtaccneg.2d acc0, m0, m2  # acc0 <- acc0, m0, m2-m3
mmulbtaccneg.2d acc0, m1, m4  # acc0 <- acc0, m1, m4-m5

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# old_acc/acc: acc0-acc1 (acc1 remains unchanged)
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m11
# Assembly mapping:

mmulbtaccneg.2d acc0, m0, m4  # acc0 <- acc0, m0-m1, m4-m7
mmulbtaccneg.2d acc0, m2, m8  # acc0 <- acc0, m2-m3, m8-m11
----


<<<

== 6. 1D Elementwise Operations

General rule: `S` must be identical for all M inputs and outputs. Concatenation shapes are allowed. Half-register shapes are forbidden.

=== 6.1 Arithmetic

[cols=",,,",options="header",]
|=======================================================================
|Spec instruction |Intrinsic |Input/output types |Type constraints
|xref:ztt-detail-mabs_ew[`mabs.ew`] |+__riscv_ztt_mabs_ew_<TD>_<S>(a)+ |M<TD,S> <- M<TA,S> |Data source `a` and destination `d` must be supported numeric datatypes.

|xref:ztt-detail-mabsdiff_ew[`mabsdiff.ew`] |+__riscv_ztt_mabsdiff_ew_<TD>_<S>(a,b)+ |M<TD,S> <- M<TA,S>, M<TB,S> |Data sources `a`/`b` and destination `d` must be supported numeric datatypes.

|xref:ztt-detail-mabsdiff_ew_x[`mabsdiff.ew.x`] |+__riscv_ztt_mabsdiff_ew_x_<TD>_<S>(b,c)+ |M<TD,S> <- M<TB,S>, Scalar<TC> |Data scalar `c` is converted under `amestype`; matrix source `b` and destination `d` are supported numeric datatypes. Data scalar ``c`` must not have a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

|xref:ztt-detail-madd_ew[`madd.ew`] |+__riscv_ztt_madd_ew_<TD>_<S>(a,b)+ |Same as `mabsdiff.ew` |Data sources `a`/`b` and destination `d` must be supported numeric datatypes.

|xref:ztt-detail-madd_ew_x[`madd.ew.x`] |+__riscv_ztt_madd_ew_x_<TD>_<S>(b,c)+ |Same as `mabsdiff.ew.x` |Data scalar `c` is converted under `amestype`; matrix source `b` and destination `d` are supported numeric datatypes. Data scalar ``c`` must not have a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

|xref:ztt-detail-mfrintm_ew[`mfrintm.ew`] |+__riscv_ztt_mfrintm_ew_<TD>_<S>(a)+ |M<TD,S> <- M<TA,S> |Source `a` and destination `d` datatypes must be [.red]##floating-point## datatypes.

|xref:ztt-detail-mfrintn_ew[`mfrintn.ew`] |+__riscv_ztt_mfrintn_ew_<TD>_<S>(a)+ |M<TD,S> <- M<TA,S> |Source `a` and destination `d` datatypes must be [.red]##floating-point## datatypes.

|xref:ztt-detail-mfrintp_ew[`mfrintp.ew`] |+__riscv_ztt_mfrintp_ew_<TD>_<S>(a)+ |M<TD,S> <- M<TA,S> |Source `a` and destination `d` datatypes must be [.red]##floating-point## datatypes.

|xref:ztt-detail-mfrintz_ew[`mfrintz.ew`] |+__riscv_ztt_mfrintz_ew_<TD>_<S>(a)+ |M<TD,S> <- M<TA,S> |Source `a` and destination `d` datatypes must be [.red]##floating-point## datatypes.

|xref:ztt-detail-mhdiff_ew[`mhdiff.ew`] |+__riscv_ztt_mhdiff_ew_<TD>_<S>(a,b)+ |Same as `mabsdiff.ew` |Data sources `a`/`b` and destination `d` must be supported numeric datatypes.

|xref:ztt-detail-mhdiff_ew_x[`mhdiff.ew.x`] |+__riscv_ztt_mhdiff_ew_x_<TD>_<S>(b,c)+ |Same as `mabsdiff.ew.x` |Data scalar `c` is converted under `amestype`; matrix source `b` and destination `d` are supported numeric datatypes. Data scalar ``c`` must not have a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

|xref:ztt-detail-mmax_ew[`mmax.ew`] |+__riscv_ztt_mmax_ew_<TD>_<S>(a,b)+ |M<TD,S> <- M<TD,S>, M<TD,S> |Sources `a`/`b` and destination `d` must have [.red]##identical## ordered numeric datatypes.

|xref:ztt-detail-mmax_ew_x[`mmax.ew.x`] |+__riscv_ztt_mmax_ew_x_<TD>_<S>(b,c)+ |M<TD,S> <- M<TD,S>, Scalar<TC> |Matrix source `b` and destination `d` datatypes must be [.red]##identical## ordered numeric datatypes; scalar `c` is converted to the datatype of matrix source `b`. Data scalar ``c`` must not have a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

|xref:ztt-detail-mmean_ew[`mmean.ew`] |+__riscv_ztt_mmean_ew_<TD>_<S>(a,b)+ |Same as `mabsdiff.ew` |Data sources `a`/`b` and destination `d` must be supported numeric datatypes.

|xref:ztt-detail-mmean_ew_x[`mmean.ew.x`] |+__riscv_ztt_mmean_ew_x_<TD>_<S>(b,c)+ |Same as `mabsdiff.ew.x` |Data scalar `c` is converted under `amestype`; matrix source `b` and destination `d` are supported numeric datatypes. Data scalar ``c`` must not have a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

|xref:ztt-detail-mmin_ew[`mmin.ew`] |+__riscv_ztt_mmin_ew_<TD>_<S>(a,b)+ |M<TD,S> <- M<TD,S>, M<TD,S> |Sources `a`/`b` and destination `d` must have [.red]##identical## ordered numeric datatypes.

|xref:ztt-detail-mmin_ew_x[`mmin.ew.x`] |+__riscv_ztt_mmin_ew_x_<TD>_<S>(b,c)+ |M<TD,S> <- M<TD,S>, Scalar<TC> |Matrix source `b` and destination `d` datatypes must be [.red]##identical## ordered numeric datatypes; scalar `c` is converted to the datatype of matrix source `b`. Data scalar ``c`` must not have a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

|xref:ztt-detail-mmul_ew[`mmul.ew`] |+__riscv_ztt_mmul_ew_<TD>_<S>(a,b)+ |Same as `mabsdiff.ew` |Data sources `a`/`b` and destination `d` must be supported numeric datatypes.

|xref:ztt-detail-mmul_ew_x[`mmul.ew.x`] |+__riscv_ztt_mmul_ew_x_<TD>_<S>(b,c)+ |Same as `mabsdiff.ew.x` |Data scalar `c` is converted under `amestype`; matrix source `b` and destination `d` are supported numeric datatypes. Data scalar ``c`` must not have a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

|xref:ztt-detail-mmulacc_ew[`mmulacc.ew`] |+__riscv_ztt_mmulacc_ew_<TD>_<S>(old_d,a,b)+ |M<TD,S> <- M<TD,S>, M<TA,S>, M<TB,S> |Explicit data sources `a`/`b` and destination `d` must be supported numeric datatypes; `old_d` has the [.red]##same## datatype and size as destination `d`.

|xref:ztt-detail-mmulacc_ew_x[`mmulacc.ew.x`] |+__riscv_ztt_mmulacc_ew_x_<TD>_<S>(old_d,b,c)+ |M<TD,S> <- M<TD,S>, M<TB,S>, Scalar<TC> |Explicit matrix source `b` and destination `d` must be supported numeric datatypes; `old_d` has the [.red]##same## datatype and size as destination `d`; scalar `c` uses `amestype`. Data scalar ``c`` must not have a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

|xref:ztt-detail-mmulaccneg_ew[`mmulaccneg.ew`] |+__riscv_ztt_mmulaccneg_ew_<TD>_<S>(old_d,a,b)+ |Same as `mmulacc.ew` |Explicit data sources `a`/`b` and destination `d` must be supported numeric datatypes; `old_d` has the [.red]##same## datatype and size as destination `d`.

Data scalar ``c`` must not have a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

|xref:ztt-detail-mmuladd_ew_x[`mmuladd.ew.x`] |+__riscv_ztt_mmuladd_ew_x_<TD>_<S>(old_d,b,c)+ |Same as `mmulacc.ew.x` |Explicit matrix source `b` and destination `d` must be supported numeric datatypes; `old_d` has the [.red]##same## datatype and size as destination `d`; scalar `c` uses `amestype`. [[ztt-detail-mabs_ew]]
==== `mabs.ew`

Computes the elementwise absolute value of one M source operand. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mabs.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes M source operand `src=M<TA,S>`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The data source `src` and destination `dst` must be supported numeric datatypes.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = abs(src[i])
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mabs_ew_i32_1x1(src_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mabs_ew_bf16_1x2(src_bf16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_f64_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mabs_ew_f64_4x1(src_i8_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mabs_ew_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x4); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mabs_ew_i32_1x2(src_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mabs_ew_i16_1x2(src);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m2, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mabs.ew m2, m0       # m2 <- m0-m1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m4, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m5, a0
mabs.ew m4, m0       # m4 <- m0-m1
mabs.ew m5, m2       # m5 <- m2-m3

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m7
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m8, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m10, a0
mabs.ew m8, m0       # m8-m9 <- m0-m3
mabs.ew m10, m4      # m10-m11 <- m4-m7
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mabsdiff_ew]]
==== `mabsdiff.ew`

Computes the elementwise absolute difference of two M source operands. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mabsdiff.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes M source operands `src1=M<TA,S>` and `src2=M<TB,S>`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The data sources `src1`/`src2` and destination `dst` must be supported numeric datatypes.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = abs(src1[i] - src2[i])
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mabsdiff_ew_i32_1x1(src1_i32_1x1, src2_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mabsdiff_ew_bf16_1x2(src1_bf16_1x2, src2_bf16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_f64_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mabsdiff_ew_f64_4x1(src1_i8_4x1, src2_f32_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mabsdiff_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x4, src2_i32_1x2); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mabsdiff_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mabsdiff_ew_i16_1x2(src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1-m2
# result: m3
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m3, a0             # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mabsdiff.ew m3, m0, m1    # m3 <- m0, m1-m2

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m5
# result: m6-m7
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m6, a0             # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m7, a0
mabsdiff.ew m6, m0, m2    # m6 <- m0, m2-m3
mabsdiff.ew m7, m1, m4    # m7 <- m1, m4-m5

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m11
# result: m12-m15
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m12, a0            # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m14, a0
mabsdiff.ew m12, m0, m4   # m12-m13 <- m0-m1, m4-m7
mabsdiff.ew m14, m2, m8   # m14-m15 <- m2-m3, m8-m11
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mabsdiff_ew_x]]
==== `mabsdiff.ew.x`

Computes the elementwise absolute difference between a scalar and one M source operand. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mabsdiff.ew.x`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes M source operand `src=M<TB,S>` and data scalar `scalar=Scalar<TC>`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The matrix source `src` and destination `dst` must be supported numeric datatypes. The data scalar `scalar` is interpreted through `amestype` and converted before the operation. Data scalar ``scalar`` must not have a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = abs(scalar - src[i])
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mabsdiff_ew_x_i32_1x1(src_i32_1x1, scalar_i32);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mabsdiff_ew_x_bf16_1x2(src_bf16_1x2, scalar_bf16);
__riscv_ztt_f64_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mabsdiff_ew_x_f64_4x1(src_f32_4x1, scalar_i8);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mabsdiff_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x4, scalar_i32); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mabsdiff_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_2x1, scalar_i32); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mabsdiff_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, (__riscv_ztt_i4_scalar_t)0); // 4-bit Scalar types are unsupported
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mabsdiff_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, 3); // Scalar requires an explicit dedicated Scalar type
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_scalar_t scalar = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mabsdiff_ew_x_i16_1x2(src, scalar);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# scalar: a1
# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2  # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m2, a0     # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mabsdiff.ew.x m2, a1, m0 # m2 <- m0-m1, a1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# scalar: a1
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2  # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m4, a0     # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m5, a0
mabsdiff.ew.x m4, a1, m0 # m4 <- m0-m1, a1
mabsdiff.ew.x m5, a1, m2 # m5 <- m2-m3, a1

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m7
# scalar: a1
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2   # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m8, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m10, a0
mabsdiff.ew.x m8, a1, m0  # m8-m9 <- m0-m3, a1
mabsdiff.ew.x m10, a1, m4 # m10-m11 <- m4-m7, a1
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-madd_ew]]
==== `madd.ew`

Computes the elementwise sum of two M source operands. It maps directly to the spec instruction `madd.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes M source operands `src1=M<TA,S>` and `src2=M<TB,S>`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The data sources `src1`/`src2` and destination `dst` must be supported numeric datatypes.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = src1[i] + src2[i]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_madd_ew_i32_1x1(src1_i32_1x1, src2_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_madd_ew_bf16_1x2(src1_bf16_1x2, src2_bf16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_f64_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_madd_ew_f64_4x1(src1_i8_4x1, src2_f32_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_madd_ew_i16_1x2(src1_i16_1x2, src2_i32_1x4); // Input shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_madd_ew_i16_1x2(src1_i16_1x2, src2_i32_2x1); // Input shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_madd_ew_i16_1x2(src1_i16_1x1, src2_i32_1x1); // Input and output shapes differ
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_madd_ew_i16_1x2(src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1-m2
# result: m3
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m3, a0        # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
madd.ew m3, m0, m1    # m3 <- m0, m1-m2

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m5
# result: m6-m7
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m6, a0        # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m7, a0
madd.ew m6, m0, m2    # m6 <- m0, m2-m3
madd.ew m7, m1, m4    # m7 <- m1, m4-m5

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m11
# result: m12-m15
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m12, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m14, a0
madd.ew m12, m0, m4   # m12-m13 <- m0-m1, m4-m7
madd.ew m14, m2, m8   # m14-m15 <- m2-m3, m8-m11
----

[[ztt-detail-madd_ew_x]]
==== `madd.ew.x`

Computes the elementwise sum of a scalar and one M source operand. It maps directly to the spec instruction `madd.ew.x`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes M source operand `src=M<TB,S>` and data scalar `scalar=Scalar<TC>`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The matrix source `src` and destination `dst` must be supported numeric datatypes. The data scalar `scalar` is interpreted through `amestype` and converted before the operation. Data scalar ``scalar`` must not have a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = scalar + src[i]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_madd_ew_x_i32_1x1(src_i32_1x1, scalar_i32);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_madd_ew_x_bf16_1x2(src_bf16_1x2, scalar_bf16);
__riscv_ztt_f64_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_madd_ew_x_f64_4x1(src_f32_4x1, scalar_i8);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_madd_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x4, scalar_i32); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_madd_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_2x1, scalar_i32); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_madd_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, (__riscv_ztt_i4_scalar_t)0); // 4-bit Scalar types are unsupported
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_madd_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, 3); // Scalar requires an explicit dedicated Scalar type
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_scalar_t scalar = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_madd_ew_x_i16_1x2(src, scalar);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# scalar: a1
# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2  # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m2, a0     # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
madd.ew.x m2, a1, m0 # m2 <- m0-m1, a1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# scalar: a1
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2  # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m4, a0     # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m5, a0
madd.ew.x m4, a1, m0 # m4 <- m0-m1, a1
madd.ew.x m5, a1, m2 # m5 <- m2-m3, a1

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m7
# scalar: a1
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2   # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m8, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m10, a0
madd.ew.x m8, a1, m0  # m8-m9 <- m0-m3, a1
madd.ew.x m10, a1, m4 # m10-m11 <- m4-m7, a1
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mfrintm_ew]]
==== `mfrintm.ew`

Rounds each floating-point element toward minus infinity. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mfrintm.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes floating-point M source operand `src` with shape `S`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. source `src` and destination `dst` datatypes must be [.red]##floating-point## datatypes, and the exact `src`-to-`dst` tuple must be supported by hardware.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = floor(src[i])
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----

__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mfrintm_ew_bf16_1x2(src_bf16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_f32_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mfrintm_ew_f32_4x1(src_bf16_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----

__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mfrintm_ew_f32_1x2(src_i32_1x2); // Data source must be floating-point
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mfrintm_ew_i32_1x2(src_f32_1x2); // dst must be floating-point
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mfrintm_ew_bf16_1x2(src);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m2, a0          # a0 holds the bf16 type encoding
mfrintm.ew m2, m0      # m2 <- m0-m1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m5, a0
mfrintm.ew m4, m0      # m4 <- m0-m1
mfrintm.ew m5, m2      # m5 <- m2-m3

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m7
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m8, a0          # a0 holds the bf16 type encoding
msettyp m10, a0
mfrintm.ew m8, m0      # m8-m9 <- m0-m3
mfrintm.ew m10, m4     # m10-m11 <- m4-m7

[[ztt-detail-mhdiff_ew]]
==== `mhdiff.ew`

Computes the elementwise half-difference of two M source operands. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mhdiff.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes M source operands `src1=M<TA,S>` and `src2=M<TB,S>`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The data sources `src1`/`src2` and destination `dst` must be supported numeric datatypes.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = (src2[i] - src1[i]) / 2
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mhdiff_ew_i32_1x1(src1_i32_1x1, src2_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mhdiff_ew_bf16_1x2(src1_bf16_1x2, src2_bf16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_f64_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mhdiff_ew_f64_4x1(src1_i8_4x1, src2_f32_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mhdiff_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x4, src2_i32_1x2); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mhdiff_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mhdiff_ew_i16_1x2(src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1-m2
# result: m3
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m3, a0        # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mhdiff.ew m3, m0, m1 # m3 <- m0, m1-m2

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m5
# result: m6-m7
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m6, a0        # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m7, a0
mhdiff.ew m6, m0, m2 # m6 <- m0, m2-m3
mhdiff.ew m7, m1, m4 # m7 <- m1, m4-m5

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m11
# result: m12-m15
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m12, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m14, a0
mhdiff.ew m12, m0, m4 # m12-m13 <- m0-m1, m4-m7
mhdiff.ew m14, m2, m8 # m14-m15 <- m2-m3, m8-m11
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mhdiff_ew_x]]
==== `mhdiff.ew.x`

Computes the elementwise half-difference between a scalar and one M source operand. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mhdiff.ew.x`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes M source operand `src=M<TB,S>` and data scalar `scalar=Scalar<TC>`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The matrix source `src` and destination `dst` must be supported numeric datatypes. The data scalar `scalar` is interpreted through `amestype` and converted before the operation. Data scalar ``scalar`` must not have a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = (src[i] - scalar) / 2
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]

__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mhdiff_ew_x_bf16_1x2(src_bf16_1x2, scalar_bf16);

__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mhdiff_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, (__riscv_ztt_i4_scalar_t)0); // 4-bit Scalar types are unsupported
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mhdiff_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, 3); // Scalar requires an explicit dedicated Scalar type
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]

/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_scalar_t scalar = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mhdiff_ew_x_i16_1x2(src, scalar);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# scalar: a1
# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2  # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m2, a0     # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mhdiff.ew.x m2, a1, m0 # m2 <- m0-m1, a1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# scalar: a1
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2  # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m4, a0     # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m5, a0
mhdiff.ew.x m4, a1, m0 # m4 <- m0-m1, a1
mhdiff.ew.x m5, a1, m2 # m5 <- m2-m3, a1

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m7
# scalar: a1
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2   # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m8, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m10, a0
mhdiff.ew.x m8, a1, m0  # m8-m9 <- m0-m3, a1
mhdiff.ew.x m10, a1, m4 # m10-m11 <- m4-m7, a1
----

[[ztt-detail-mmax_ew]]
==== `mmax.ew`

Computes the elementwise maximum of two M source operands. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mmax.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes M source operands `src1=M<TA,S>` and `src2=M<TB,S>`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. sources `src1`/`src2` and destination `dst` datatypes must be [.red]##identical## ordered numeric datatypes.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = max(src1[i], src2[i])
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mmax_ew_i32_1x1(src1_i32_1x1, src2_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmax_ew_bf16_1x2(src1_bf16_1x2, src2_bf16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_u8_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mmax_ew_u8_4x1(src1_u8_4x1, src2_u8_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmax_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x4, src2_i32_1x2); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmax_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmax_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i16_1x2, src2_i32_1x2); // Data source and dst datatypes differ
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mmax_ew_i16_1x2(src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1
# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m2, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mmax.ew m2, m0, m1  # m2 <- m0, m1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m3
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m4, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m5, a0
mmax.ew m4, m0, m2  # m4 <- m0, m2
mmax.ew m5, m1, m3  # m5 <- m1, m3

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m7
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m8, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m10, a0
mmax.ew m8, m0, m4  # m8-m9 <- m0-m1, m4-m5
mmax.ew m10, m2, m6 # m10-m11 <- m2-m3, m6-m7
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mmax_ew_x]]
==== `mmax.ew.x`

Computes the elementwise maximum of a scalar and one M source operand. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mmax.ew.x`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes M source operand `src=M<TD,S>` and data scalar `scalar=Scalar<TC>`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. matrix source `src` and destination `dst` datatypes must be [.red]##identical## ordered numeric datatypes. scalar `scalar` is converted to the datatype of matrix source `src`, and the scalar `scalar` datatype may differ. Data scalar ``scalar`` must not have a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = max(scalar, src[i])
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mmax_ew_x_i32_1x1(src_i32_1x1, scalar_i32);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmax_ew_x_bf16_1x2(src_bf16_1x2, scalar_bf16);
__riscv_ztt_u8_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mmax_ew_x_u8_4x1(src_u8_4x1, scalar_i32);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmax_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x4, scalar_i32); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmax_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_2x1, scalar_i32); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmax_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i16_1x2, scalar_i32); // Data source and dst datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmax_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, (__riscv_ztt_i4_scalar_t)0); // 4-bit Scalar types are unsupported
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmax_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, 3); // Scalar requires an explicit dedicated Scalar type
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_scalar_t scalar = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mmax_ew_x_i16_1x2(src, scalar);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0
# scalar: a1
# result: m1
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2   # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m1, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mmax.ew.x m1, a1, m0 # m1 <- m0, a1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# scalar: a1
# result: m2-m3
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2   # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m2, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m3, a0
mmax.ew.x m2, a1, m0 # m2 <- m0, a1
mmax.ew.x m3, a1, m1 # m3 <- m1, a1

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# scalar: a1
# result: m4-m7
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2   # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m4, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m6, a0
mmax.ew.x m4, a1, m0 # m4-m5 <- m0-m1, a1
mmax.ew.x m6, a1, m2 # m6-m7 <- m2-m3, a1
----

[[ztt-detail-mmean_ew]]
==== `mmean.ew`

Computes the elementwise mean of two M source operands. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mmean.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes M source operands `src1=M<TA,S>` and `src2=M<TB,S>`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The data sources `src1`/`src2` and destination `dst` must be supported numeric datatypes.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = (src1[i] + src2[i]) / 2
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mmean_ew_i32_1x1(src1_i32_1x1, src2_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmean_ew_bf16_1x2(src1_bf16_1x2, src2_bf16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_f64_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mmean_ew_f64_4x1(src1_i8_4x1, src2_f32_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmean_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x4, src2_i32_1x2); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmean_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mmean_ew_i16_1x2(src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1-m2
# result: m3
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m3, a0        # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mmean.ew m3, m0, m1  # m3 <- m0, m1-m2

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m5
# result: m6-m7
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m6, a0        # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m7, a0
mmean.ew m6, m0, m2  # m6 <- m0, m2-m3
mmean.ew m7, m1, m4  # m7 <- m1, m4-m5

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m11
# result: m12-m15
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m12, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m14, a0
mmean.ew m12, m0, m4 # m12-m13 <- m0-m1, m4-m7
mmean.ew m14, m2, m8 # m14-m15 <- m2-m3, m8-m11
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mmean_ew_x]]
==== `mmean.ew.x`

Computes the elementwise mean of a scalar and one M source operand. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mmean.ew.x`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes M source operand `src=M<TB,S>` and data scalar `scalar=Scalar<TC>`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The matrix source `src` and destination `dst` must be supported numeric datatypes. The data scalar `scalar` is interpreted through `amestype` and converted before the operation. Data scalar ``scalar`` must not have a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = (scalar + src[i]) / 2
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mmean_ew_x_i32_1x1(src_i32_1x1, scalar_i32);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmean_ew_x_bf16_1x2(src_bf16_1x2, scalar_bf16);
__riscv_ztt_f64_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mmean_ew_x_f64_4x1(src_f32_4x1, scalar_i8);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmean_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x4, scalar_i32); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmean_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_2x1, scalar_i32); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmean_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, (__riscv_ztt_i4_scalar_t)0); // 4-bit Scalar types are unsupported
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmean_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, 3); // Scalar requires an explicit dedicated Scalar type
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_scalar_t scalar = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mmean_ew_x_i16_1x2(src, scalar);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# scalar: a1
# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2  # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m2, a0     # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mmean.ew.x m2, a1, m0 # m2 <- m0-m1, a1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# scalar: a1
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2  # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m4, a0     # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m5, a0
mmean.ew.x m4, a1, m0 # m4 <- m0-m1, a1
mmean.ew.x m5, a1, m2 # m5 <- m2-m3, a1

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m7
# scalar: a1
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2   # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m8, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m10, a0
mmean.ew.x m8, a1, m0  # m8-m9 <- m0-m3, a1
mmean.ew.x m10, a1, m4 # m10-m11 <- m4-m7, a1
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mmin_ew]]
==== `mmin.ew`

Computes the elementwise minimum of two M source operands. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mmin.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes M source operands `src1=M<TA,S>` and `src2=M<TB,S>`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. sources `src1`/`src2` and destination `dst` datatypes must be [.red]##identical## ordered numeric datatypes.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = min(src1[i], src2[i])
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mmin_ew_i32_1x1(src1_i32_1x1, src2_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmin_ew_bf16_1x2(src1_bf16_1x2, src2_bf16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_u8_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mmin_ew_u8_4x1(src1_u8_4x1, src2_u8_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmin_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x4, src2_i32_1x2); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmin_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmin_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i16_1x2, src2_i32_1x2); // Data source and dst datatypes differ
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]

/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mmin_ew_i16_1x2(src1, src2);

# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1
# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m2, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mmin.ew m2, m0, m1  # m2 <- m0, m1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m3
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m4, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m5, a0
mmin.ew m4, m0, m2  # m4 <- m0, m2
mmin.ew m5, m1, m3  # m5 <- m1, m3

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m7
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m8, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m10, a0
mmin.ew m8, m0, m4  # m8-m9 <- m0-m1, m4-m5
mmin.ew m10, m2, m6 # m10-m11 <- m2-m3, m6-m7
