[[ztt-detail-mmul_ew]]
==== `mmul.ew`

Computes the elementwise product of two M source operands. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mmul.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes M source operands `src1=M<TA,S>` and `src2=M<TB,S>`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The data sources `src1`/`src2` and destination `dst` must be supported numeric datatypes.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = src1[i] * src2[i]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mmul_ew_i32_1x1(src1_i32_1x1, src2_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmul_ew_bf16_1x2(src1_bf16_1x2, src2_bf16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_f64_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mmul_ew_f64_4x1(src1_i8_4x1, src2_f32_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmul_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x4, src2_i32_1x2); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmul_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mmul_ew_i16_1x2(src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1-m2
# result: m3
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m3, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mmul.ew m3, m0, m1  # m3 <- m0, m1-m2

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m5
# result: m6-m7
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m6, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m7, a0
mmul.ew m6, m0, m2  # m6 <- m0, m2-m3
mmul.ew m7, m1, m4  # m7 <- m1, m4-m5

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m11
# result: m12-m15
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m12, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m14, a0
mmul.ew m12, m0, m4 # m12-m13 <- m0-m1, m4-m7
mmul.ew m14, m2, m8 # m14-m15 <- m2-m3, m8-m11
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mmul_ew_x]]
==== `mmul.ew.x`

Computes the elementwise product of a scalar and one M source operand. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mmul.ew.x`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes M source operand `src=M<TB,S>` and data scalar `scalar=Scalar<TC>`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The matrix source `src` and destination `dst` must be supported numeric datatypes. The data scalar `scalar` is interpreted through `amestype` and converted before the operation. Data scalar ``scalar`` must not have a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = scalar * src[i]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mmul_ew_x_i32_1x1(src_i32_1x1, scalar_i32);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmul_ew_x_bf16_1x2(src_bf16_1x2, scalar_bf16);
__riscv_ztt_f64_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mmul_ew_x_f64_4x1(src_f32_4x1, scalar_i8);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmul_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x4, scalar_i32); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmul_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_2x1, scalar_i32); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmul_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, (__riscv_ztt_i4_scalar_t)0); // 4-bit Scalar types are unsupported
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmul_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, 3); // Scalar requires an explicit dedicated Scalar type
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_scalar_t scalar = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mmul_ew_x_i16_1x2(src, scalar);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# scalar: a1
# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2  # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m2, a0     # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mmul.ew.x m2, a1, m0 # m2 <- m0-m1, a1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# scalar: a1
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2  # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m4, a0     # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m5, a0
mmul.ew.x m4, a1, m0 # m4 <- m0-m1, a1
mmul.ew.x m5, a1, m2 # m5 <- m2-m3, a1

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m7
# scalar: a1
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2   # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m8, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m10, a0
mmul.ew.x m8, a1, m0  # m8-m9 <- m0-m3, a1
mmul.ew.x m10, a1, m4 # m10-m11 <- m4-m7, a1
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mmulacc_ew]]
==== `mmulacc.ew`

Computes an elementwise multiply-accumulate using an old destination value and two M source operands. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mmulacc.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes `old_d=M<TD,S>`, `src1=M<TA,S>`, and `src2=M<TB,S>`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The explicit data sources `src1`/`src2` and destination `dst` must be supported numeric datatypes. The `old_d` operand has the [.red]##same## datatype and size as destination `dst`.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = old_d[i] + src1[i] * src2[i]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mmulacc_ew_i32_1x1(old_d_i32_1x1, src1_i32_1x1, src2_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulacc_ew_bf16_1x2(old_d_bf16_1x2, src1_bf16_1x2, src2_bf16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_f64_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mmulacc_ew_f64_4x1(old_d_f64_4x1, src1_i8_4x1, src2_f32_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulacc_ew_i32_1x2(old_d_i32_1x2, src1_i32_1x4, src2_i32_1x2); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulacc_ew_i32_1x2(old_d_i32_1x2, src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulacc_ew_i32_1x2(old_d_i16_1x2, src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_1x2); // old_d and dst datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulacc_ew_i32_1x2(old_d_i32_1x4, src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_1x2); // old_d and dst shapes differ
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t old_d = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mmulacc_ew_i16_1x2(old_d, src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1-m2
# old_d/result: m3
# Assembly mapping:

mmulacc.ew m3, m0, m1   # m3 <- m3, m0, m1-m2

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m5
# old_d/result: m6-m7
# Assembly mapping:

mmulacc.ew m6, m0, m2   # m6 <- m6, m0, m2-m3
mmulacc.ew m7, m1, m4   # m7 <- m7, m1, m4-m5

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m11
# old_d/result: m12-m15
# Assembly mapping:

mmulacc.ew m12, m0, m4  # m12-m13 <- m12-m13, m0-m1, m4-m7
mmulacc.ew m14, m2, m8  # m14-m15 <- m14-m15, m2-m3, m8-m11
----

==== `mmulacc.ew.x`

Computes an elementwise scalar multiply-accumulate using an old destination value, one M source operand, and a scalar. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mmulacc.ew.x`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes `old_d=M<TD,S>`, matrix source `src=M<TB,S>`, and data scalar `scalar=Scalar<TC>`.

The `old_d` operand has the [.red]##same## datatype and size as destination `dst`. The data scalar `scalar` is interpreted through `amestype`. Data scalar ``scalar`` must not have a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

[source,c]

----

__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulacc_ew_x_i32_1x2(old_d_i16_1x2, src_i32_1x2, scalar_i32); // old_d and dst datatypes differ

__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_scalar_t scalar = ...;

[[ztt-detail-mmulaccneg_ew]]
==== `mmulaccneg.ew`

Computes an elementwise negated multiply-accumulate using an old destination value and two M source operands. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mmulaccneg.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes `old_d=M<TD,S>`, `src1=M<TA,S>`, and `src2=M<TB,S>`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The explicit data sources `src1`/`src2` and destination `dst` must be supported numeric datatypes. The `old_d` operand has the [.red]##same## datatype and size as destination `dst`.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = old_d[i] - src1[i] * src2[i]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]

----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]

__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulaccneg_ew_i32_1x2(old_d_i32_1x2, src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulaccneg_ew_i32_1x2(old_d_i16_1x2, src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_1x2); // old_d and dst datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulaccneg_ew_i32_1x2(old_d_i32_1x4, src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_1x2); // old_d and dst shapes differ
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----

__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mmulaccneg_ew_i16_1x2(old_d, src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1-m2
# old_d/result: m3
# Assembly mapping:

mmulaccneg.ew m3, m0, m1   # m3 <- m3, m0, m1-m2

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m5
# old_d/result: m6-m7
# Assembly mapping:

mmulaccneg.ew m6, m0, m2   # m6 <- m6, m0, m2-m3
mmulaccneg.ew m7, m1, m4   # m7 <- m7, m1, m4-m5

----------------
# UDS=8

# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m11
# old_d/result: m12-m15
# Assembly mapping:

mmulaccneg.ew m14, m2, m8  # m14-m15 <- m14-m15, m2-m3, m8-m11

[[ztt-detail-mmuladd_ew]]
==== `mmuladd.ew`

Computes an elementwise multiply-add using an old destination value and two M source operands. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mmuladd.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes `old_d=M<TD,S>`, `src1=M<TA,S>`, and `src2=M<TB,S>`.

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = src1[i] + src2[i] * old_d[i]
----

__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src2 = ...;

mmuladd.ew m3, m0, m1   # m3 <- m3, m0, m1-m2

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m5
# old_d/result: m6-m7
# Assembly mapping:

mmuladd.ew m6, m0, m2   # m6 <- m6, m0, m2-m3
mmuladd.ew m7, m1, m4   # m7 <- m7, m1, m4-m5

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m11
# old_d/result: m12-m15
# Assembly mapping:

mmuladd.ew m12, m0, m4  # m12-m13 <- m12-m13, m0-m1, m4-m7
mmuladd.ew m14, m2, m8  # m14-m15 <- m14-m15, m2-m3, m8-m11
----

[[ztt-detail-mmuladd_ew_x]]
==== `mmuladd.ew.x`

Computes an elementwise scalar multiply-add using an old destination value, one M source operand, and a scalar. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mmuladd.ew.x`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes `old_d=M<TD,S>`, matrix source `src=M<TB,S>`, and data scalar `scalar=Scalar<TC>`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The explicit matrix source `src` and destination `dst` must be supported numeric datatypes. The `old_d` operand has the [.red]##same## datatype and size as destination `dst`. The data scalar `scalar` is interpreted through `amestype`. Data scalar ``scalar`` must not have a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = scalar + src[i] * old_d[i]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mmuladd_ew_x_i32_1x1(old_d_i32_1x1, src_i32_1x1, scalar_i32);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmuladd_ew_x_bf16_1x2(old_d_bf16_1x2, src_bf16_1x2, scalar_bf16);
__riscv_ztt_f64_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mmuladd_ew_x_f64_4x1(old_d_f64_4x1, src_f32_4x1, scalar_i8);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmuladd_ew_x_i32_1x2(old_d_i32_1x2, src_i32_1x4, scalar_i32); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmuladd_ew_x_i32_1x2(old_d_i32_1x2, src_i32_2x1, scalar_i32); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmuladd_ew_x_i32_1x2(old_d_i16_1x2, src_i32_1x2, scalar_i32); // old_d and dst datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmuladd_ew_x_i32_1x2(old_d_i32_1x2, src_i32_1x2, (__riscv_ztt_i4_scalar_t)0); // 4-bit Scalar types are unsupported
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmuladd_ew_x_i32_1x2(old_d_i32_1x4, src_i32_1x2, scalar_i32); // old_d and dst shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmuladd_ew_x_i32_1x2(old_d_i32_1x2, src_i32_1x2, 3); // Scalar requires an explicit dedicated Scalar type
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t old_d = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_scalar_t scalar = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mmuladd_ew_x_i16_1x2(old_d, src, scalar);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# scalar: a1
# old_d/result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2      # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
mmuladd.ew.x m2, a1, m0  # m2 <- m2, m0-m1, a1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# scalar: a1
# old_d/result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2      # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
mmuladd.ew.x m4, a1, m0  # m4 <- m4, m0-m1, a1
mmuladd.ew.x m5, a1, m2  # m5 <- m5, m2-m3, a1

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m7
# scalar: a1
# old_d/result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2       # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
mmuladd.ew.x m8, a1, m0   # m8-m9 <- m8-m9, m0-m3, a1
mmuladd.ew.x m10, a1, m4  # m10-m11 <- m10-m11, m4-m7, a1
----

[[ztt-detail-mmulneg_ew]]
==== `mmulneg.ew`

Computes the elementwise negated product of two M source operands. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mmulneg.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes M source operands `src1=M<TA,S>` and `src2=M<TB,S>`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The data sources `src1`/`src2` and destination `dst` must be supported numeric datatypes.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = -(src1[i] * src2[i])
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mmulneg_ew_i32_1x1(src1_i32_1x1, src2_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulneg_ew_bf16_1x2(src1_bf16_1x2, src2_bf16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_f64_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mmulneg_ew_f64_4x1(src1_i8_4x1, src2_f32_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulneg_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x4, src2_i32_1x2); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulneg_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mmulneg_ew_i16_1x2(src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1-m2
# result: m3
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m3, a0          # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mmulneg.ew m3, m0, m1  # m3 <- m0, m1-m2

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m5
# result: m6-m7
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m6, a0          # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m7, a0
mmulneg.ew m6, m0, m2  # m6 <- m0, m2-m3
mmulneg.ew m7, m1, m4  # m7 <- m1, m4-m5

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m11
# result: m12-m15
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m12, a0         # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m14, a0
mmulneg.ew m12, m0, m4 # m12-m13 <- m0-m1, m4-m7
mmulneg.ew m14, m2, m8 # m14-m15 <- m2-m3, m8-m11
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mmulneg_ew_x]]
==== `mmulneg.ew.x`

Computes the elementwise negated product of a scalar and one M source operand. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mmulneg.ew.x`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes M source operand `src=M<TB,S>` and data scalar `scalar=Scalar<TC>`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The matrix source `src` and destination `dst` must be supported numeric datatypes. The data scalar `scalar` is interpreted through `amestype` and converted before the operation. Data scalar ``scalar`` must not have a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = -(scalar * src[i])
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mmulneg_ew_x_i32_1x1(src_i32_1x1, scalar_i32);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulneg_ew_x_bf16_1x2(src_bf16_1x2, scalar_bf16);
__riscv_ztt_f64_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mmulneg_ew_x_f64_4x1(src_f32_4x1, scalar_i8);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulneg_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x4, scalar_i32); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulneg_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_2x1, scalar_i32); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulneg_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, (__riscv_ztt_i4_scalar_t)0); // 4-bit Scalar types are unsupported
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulneg_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, 3); // Scalar requires an explicit dedicated Scalar type
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_scalar_t scalar = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mmulneg_ew_x_i16_1x2(src, scalar);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# scalar: a1
# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2  # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m2, a0     # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mmulneg.ew.x m2, a1, m0 # m2 <- m0-m1, a1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# scalar: a1
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2  # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m4, a0     # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m5, a0
mmulneg.ew.x m4, a1, m0 # m4 <- m0-m1, a1
mmulneg.ew.x m5, a1, m2 # m5 <- m2-m3, a1

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m7
# scalar: a1
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2   # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m8, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m10, a0
mmulneg.ew.x m8, a1, m0  # m8-m9 <- m0-m3, a1
mmulneg.ew.x m10, a1, m4 # m10-m11 <- m4-m7, a1
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mmulsub_ew]]
==== `mmulsub.ew`

Computes an elementwise multiply-subtract using an old destination value and two M source operands. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mmulsub.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes `old_d=M<TD,S>`, `src1=M<TA,S>`, and `src2=M<TB,S>`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The explicit data sources `src1`/`src2` and destination `dst` must be supported numeric datatypes. The `old_d` operand has the [.red]##same## datatype and size as destination `dst`.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = src1[i] - src2[i] * old_d[i]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mmulsub_ew_i32_1x1(old_d_i32_1x1, src1_i32_1x1, src2_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulsub_ew_bf16_1x2(old_d_bf16_1x2, src1_bf16_1x2, src2_bf16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_f64_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mmulsub_ew_f64_4x1(old_d_f64_4x1, src1_i8_4x1, src2_f32_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulsub_ew_i32_1x2(old_d_i32_1x2, src1_i32_1x4, src2_i32_1x2); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulsub_ew_i32_1x2(old_d_i32_1x2, src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulsub_ew_i32_1x2(old_d_i16_1x2, src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_1x2); // old_d and dst datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulsub_ew_i32_1x2(old_d_i32_1x4, src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_1x2); // old_d and dst shapes differ
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t old_d = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mmulsub_ew_i16_1x2(old_d, src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1-m2
# old_d/result: m3
# Assembly mapping:

mmulsub.ew m3, m0, m1   # m3 <- m3, m0, m1-m2

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m5
# old_d/result: m6-m7
# Assembly mapping:

mmulsub.ew m6, m0, m2   # m6 <- m6, m0, m2-m3
mmulsub.ew m7, m1, m4   # m7 <- m7, m1, m4-m5

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m11
# old_d/result: m12-m15
# Assembly mapping:

mmulsub.ew m12, m0, m4  # m12-m13 <- m12-m13, m0-m1, m4-m7
mmulsub.ew m14, m2, m8  # m14-m15 <- m14-m15, m2-m3, m8-m11
----

[[ztt-detail-mmulsub_ew_x]]
==== `mmulsub.ew.x`

Computes an elementwise scalar multiply-subtract using an old destination value, one M source operand, and a scalar. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mmulsub.ew.x`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes `old_d=M<TD,S>`, matrix source `src=M<TB,S>`, and data scalar `scalar=Scalar<TC>`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The explicit matrix source `src` and destination `dst` must be supported numeric datatypes. The `old_d` operand has the [.red]##same## datatype and size as destination `dst`. The data scalar `scalar` is interpreted through `amestype`. Data scalar ``scalar`` must not have a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = scalar - src[i] * old_d[i]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mmulsub_ew_x_i32_1x1(old_d_i32_1x1, src_i32_1x1, scalar_i32);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulsub_ew_x_bf16_1x2(old_d_bf16_1x2, src_bf16_1x2, scalar_bf16);
__riscv_ztt_f64_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mmulsub_ew_x_f64_4x1(old_d_f64_4x1, src_f32_4x1, scalar_i8);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulsub_ew_x_i32_1x2(old_d_i32_1x2, src_i32_1x4, scalar_i32); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulsub_ew_x_i32_1x2(old_d_i32_1x2, src_i32_2x1, scalar_i32); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulsub_ew_x_i32_1x2(old_d_i16_1x2, src_i32_1x2, scalar_i32); // old_d and dst datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulsub_ew_x_i32_1x2(old_d_i32_1x2, src_i32_1x2, (__riscv_ztt_i4_scalar_t)0); // 4-bit Scalar types are unsupported
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulsub_ew_x_i32_1x2(old_d_i32_1x4, src_i32_1x2, scalar_i32); // old_d and dst shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mmulsub_ew_x_i32_1x2(old_d_i32_1x2, src_i32_1x2, 3); // Scalar requires an explicit dedicated Scalar type
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t old_d = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_scalar_t scalar = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mmulsub_ew_x_i16_1x2(old_d, src, scalar);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# scalar: a1
# old_d/result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2      # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
mmulsub.ew.x m2, a1, m0  # m2 <- m2, m0-m1, a1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# scalar: a1
# old_d/result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2      # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
mmulsub.ew.x m4, a1, m0  # m4 <- m4, m0-m1, a1
mmulsub.ew.x m5, a1, m2  # m5 <- m5, m2-m3, a1

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m7
# scalar: a1
# old_d/result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2       # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
mmulsub.ew.x m8, a1, m0   # m8-m9 <- m8-m9, m0-m3, a1
mmulsub.ew.x m10, a1, m4  # m10-m11 <- m10-m11, m4-m7, a1

[[ztt-detail-msub_ew]]
==== `msub.ew`

Computes the elementwise difference of two M source operands. It maps directly to the spec instruction `msub.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes M source operands `src1=M<TA,S>` and `src2=M<TB,S>`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The data sources `src1`/`src2` and destination `dst` must be supported numeric datatypes.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = src2[i] - src1[i]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_msub_ew_i32_1x1(src1_i32_1x1, src2_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msub_ew_bf16_1x2(src1_bf16_1x2, src2_bf16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_f64_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_msub_ew_f64_4x1(src1_i8_4x1, src2_f32_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msub_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x4, src2_i32_1x2); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msub_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_msub_ew_i16_1x2(src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1-m2
# result: m3
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m3, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msub.ew m3, m0, m1  # m3 <- m0, m1-m2

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m5
# result: m6-m7
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m6, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m7, a0
msub.ew m6, m0, m2  # m6 <- m0, m2-m3
msub.ew m7, m1, m4  # m7 <- m1, m4-m5

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m11
# result: m12-m15
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m12, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m14, a0
msub.ew m12, m0, m4 # m12-m13 <- m0-m1, m4-m7
msub.ew m14, m2, m8 # m14-m15 <- m2-m3, m8-m11
----

[[ztt-detail-msub_ew_x]]
==== `msub.ew.x`

Computes the elementwise difference between a scalar and one M source operand. It maps directly to the spec instruction `msub.ew.x`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes M source operand `src=M<TB,S>` and data scalar `scalar=Scalar<TC>`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The matrix source `src` and destination `dst` must be supported numeric datatypes. The data scalar `scalar` is interpreted through `amestype` and converted before the operation. Data scalar ``scalar`` must not have a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = src[i] - scalar
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_msub_ew_x_i32_1x1(src_i32_1x1, scalar_i32);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msub_ew_x_bf16_1x2(src_bf16_1x2, scalar_bf16);
__riscv_ztt_f64_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_msub_ew_x_f64_4x1(src_f32_4x1, scalar_i8);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msub_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x4, scalar_i32); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msub_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_2x1, scalar_i32); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msub_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, (__riscv_ztt_i4_scalar_t)0); // 4-bit Scalar types are unsupported
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msub_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, 3); // Scalar requires an explicit dedicated Scalar type
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_scalar_t scalar = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_msub_ew_x_i16_1x2(src, scalar);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# scalar: a1
# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2  # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m2, a0     # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msub.ew.x m2, a1, m0 # m2 <- m0-m1, a1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# scalar: a1
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2  # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m4, a0     # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m5, a0
msub.ew.x m4, a1, m0 # m4 <- m0-m1, a1
msub.ew.x m5, a1, m2 # m5 <- m2-m3, a1

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m7
# scalar: a1
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2   # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m8, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m10, a0
msub.ew.x m8, a1, m0  # m8-m9 <- m0-m3, a1
msub.ew.x m10, a1, m4 # m10-m11 <- m4-m7, a1
----

[[ztt-detail-mand_ew]]
==== `mand.ew`

Computes the elementwise bitwise AND of two integer M operands. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mand.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes M source operands `src1` and `src2` with the same integer datatype and shape `S`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. sources `src1`/`src2` and destination `dst` datatypes must be [.red]##identical## [.red]##integer## datatypes.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = src1[i] & src2[i]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mand_ew_i32_1x1(src1_i32_1x1, src2_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_u16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mand_ew_u16_1x2(src1_u16_1x2, src2_u16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_u8_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mand_ew_u8_4x1(src1_u8_4x1, src2_u8_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mand_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x4, src2_i32_1x2); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mand_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mand_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i16_1x2, src2_i32_1x2); // Data source and dst datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mand_ew_f32_1x2(src1_f32_1x2, src2_f32_1x2); // Matrix datatypes must be integer
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mand_ew_i16_1x2(src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1
# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m2, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mand.ew m2, m0, m1  # m2 <- m0, m1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m3
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m4, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m5, a0
mand.ew m4, m0, m2  # m4 <- m0, m2
mand.ew m5, m1, m3  # m5 <- m1, m3

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m7
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m8, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m10, a0
mand.ew m8, m0, m4  # m8-m9 <- m0-m1, m4-m5
mand.ew m10, m2, m6 # m10-m11 <- m2-m3, m6-m7
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mand_ew_x]]
==== `mand.ew.x`

Computes the elementwise bitwise AND of a scalar and one integer M operand. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mand.ew.x`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes M source operand `src` with shape `S` and data scalar `scalar`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. matrix source `src` and destination `dst` datatypes must be [.red]##identical## [.red]##integer## datatypes. scalar `scalar` is converted to the datatype of matrix source `src`, and the scalar `scalar` datatype may differ. Data scalar ``scalar`` must not have a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = scalar & src[i]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mand_ew_x_i32_1x1(src_i32_1x1, scalar_i32);
__riscv_ztt_u16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mand_ew_x_u16_1x2(src_u16_1x2, scalar_u16);
__riscv_ztt_u8_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mand_ew_x_u8_4x1(src_u8_4x1, scalar_i32);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mand_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x4, scalar_i32); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mand_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_2x1, scalar_i32); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mand_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i16_1x2, scalar_i32); // Data source and dst datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mand_ew_x_f32_1x2(src_f32_1x2, scalar_i32); // Matrix datatypes must be integer
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mand_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, (__riscv_ztt_i4_scalar_t)0); // 4-bit Scalar types are unsupported
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mand_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, 3); // Scalar requires an explicit dedicated Scalar type
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_scalar_t scalar = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mand_ew_x_i16_1x2(src, scalar);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0
# scalar: a1
# result: m1
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2   # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m1, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mand.ew.x m1, a1, m0 # m1 <- m0, a1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# scalar: a1
# result: m2-m3
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2   # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m2, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m3, a0
mand.ew.x m2, a1, m0 # m2 <- m0, a1
mand.ew.x m3, a1, m1 # m3 <- m1, a1

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# scalar: a1
# result: m4-m7
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2   # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m4, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m6, a0
mand.ew.x m4, a1, m0 # m4-m5 <- m0-m1, a1
mand.ew.x m6, a1, m2 # m6-m7 <- m2-m3, a1
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mandnot_ew]]
==== `mandnot.ew`

Computes the elementwise bitwise AND-NOT of two integer M operands. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mandnot.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes M source operands `src1` and `src2` with the same integer datatype and shape `S`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. sources `src1`/`src2` and destination `dst` datatypes must be [.red]##identical## [.red]##integer## datatypes.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = src1[i] & ~src2[i]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mandnot_ew_i32_1x1(src1_i32_1x1, src2_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_u16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mandnot_ew_u16_1x2(src1_u16_1x2, src2_u16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_u8_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mandnot_ew_u8_4x1(src1_u8_4x1, src2_u8_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mandnot_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x4, src2_i32_1x2); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mandnot_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mandnot_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i16_1x2, src2_i32_1x2); // Data source and dst datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mandnot_ew_f32_1x2(src1_f32_1x2, src2_f32_1x2); // Matrix datatypes must be integer
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mandnot_ew_i16_1x2(src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1
# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m2, a0          # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mandnot.ew m2, m0, m1  # m2 <- m0, m1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m3
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m4, a0          # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m5, a0
mandnot.ew m4, m0, m2  # m4 <- m0, m2
mandnot.ew m5, m1, m3  # m5 <- m1, m3

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m7
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m8, a0          # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m10, a0
mandnot.ew m8, m0, m4  # m8-m9 <- m0-m1, m4-m5
mandnot.ew m10, m2, m6 # m10-m11 <- m2-m3, m6-m7
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mandnot_ew_x]]
==== `mandnot.ew.x`

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes M source operand `src` with shape `S` and data scalar `scalar`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. matrix source `src` and destination `dst` datatypes must be [.red]##identical## [.red]##integer## datatypes. scalar `scalar` is converted to the datatype of matrix source `src`, and the scalar `scalar` datatype may differ. Data scalar ``scalar`` must not have a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mandnot_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, 3); // Scalar requires an explicit dedicated Scalar type

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]

/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_scalar_t scalar = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mandnot_ew_x_i16_1x2(src, scalar);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0
# scalar: a1
# result: m1

csrw amestype, a2   # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m1, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mandnot.ew.x m1, a1, m0 # m1 <- m0, a1

# Operand register allocation:

[[ztt-detail-mor_ew]]
==== `mor.ew`

Computes the elementwise bitwise OR of two integer M operands. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mor.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes M source operands `src1` and `src2` with the same integer datatype and shape `S`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. sources `src1`/`src2` and destination `dst` datatypes must be [.red]##identical## [.red]##integer## datatypes.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = src1[i] | src2[i]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mor_ew_i32_1x1(src1_i32_1x1, src2_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_u16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mor_ew_u16_1x2(src1_u16_1x2, src2_u16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_u8_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mor_ew_u8_4x1(src1_u8_4x1, src2_u8_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mor_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x4, src2_i32_1x2); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mor_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mor_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i16_1x2, src2_i32_1x2); // Data source and dst datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mor_ew_f32_1x2(src1_f32_1x2, src2_f32_1x2); // Matrix datatypes must be integer
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mor_ew_i16_1x2(src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1
# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m2, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mor.ew m2, m0, m1  # m2 <- m0, m1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m3
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m4, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m5, a0
mor.ew m4, m0, m2  # m4 <- m0, m2
mor.ew m5, m1, m3  # m5 <- m1, m3

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m7
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m8, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m10, a0
mor.ew m8, m0, m4  # m8-m9 <- m0-m1, m4-m5
mor.ew m10, m2, m6 # m10-m11 <- m2-m3, m6-m7
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mor_ew_x]]
==== `mor.ew.x`

Computes the elementwise bitwise OR of a scalar and one integer M operand. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mor.ew.x`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes M source operand `src` with shape `S` and data scalar `scalar`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. matrix source `src` and destination `dst` datatypes must be [.red]##identical## [.red]##integer## datatypes. scalar `scalar` is converted to the datatype of matrix source `src`, and the scalar `scalar` datatype may differ. Data scalar ``scalar`` must not have a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = scalar | src[i]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mor_ew_x_i32_1x1(src_i32_1x1, scalar_i32);
__riscv_ztt_u16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mor_ew_x_u16_1x2(src_u16_1x2, scalar_u16);
__riscv_ztt_u8_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mor_ew_x_u8_4x1(src_u8_4x1, scalar_i32);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mor_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x4, scalar_i32); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mor_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_2x1, scalar_i32); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mor_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i16_1x2, scalar_i32); // Data source and dst datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mor_ew_x_f32_1x2(src_f32_1x2, scalar_i32); // Matrix datatypes must be integer
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mor_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, (__riscv_ztt_i4_scalar_t)0); // 4-bit Scalar types are unsupported
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mor_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, 3); // Scalar requires an explicit dedicated Scalar type
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_scalar_t scalar = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mor_ew_x_i16_1x2(src, scalar);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0
# scalar: a1
# result: m1
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2   # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m1, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mor.ew.x m1, a1, m0 # m1 <- m0, a1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# scalar: a1
# result: m2-m3
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2   # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m2, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m3, a0
mor.ew.x m2, a1, m0 # m2 <- m0, a1
mor.ew.x m3, a1, m1 # m3 <- m1, a1

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# scalar: a1
# result: m4-m7
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2   # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m4, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m6, a0
mor.ew.x m4, a1, m0 # m4-m5 <- m0-m1, a1
mor.ew.x m6, a1, m2 # m6-m7 <- m2-m3, a1
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mornot_ew]]
==== `mornot.ew`

Computes the elementwise bitwise OR-NOT of two integer M operands. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mornot.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes M source operands `src1` and `src2` with the same integer datatype and shape `S`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. sources `src1`/`src2` and destination `dst` datatypes must be [.red]##identical## [.red]##integer## datatypes.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = src1[i] | ~src2[i]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mornot_ew_i32_1x1(src1_i32_1x1, src2_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_u16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mornot_ew_u16_1x2(src1_u16_1x2, src2_u16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_u8_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mornot_ew_u8_4x1(src1_u8_4x1, src2_u8_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mornot_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x4, src2_i32_1x2); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mornot_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mornot_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i16_1x2, src2_i32_1x2); // Data source and dst datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mornot_ew_f32_1x2(src1_f32_1x2, src2_f32_1x2); // Matrix datatypes must be integer
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mornot_ew_i16_1x2(src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1
# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m2, a0         # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mornot.ew m2, m0, m1  # m2 <- m0, m1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m3
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m4, a0         # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m5, a0
mornot.ew m4, m0, m2  # m4 <- m0, m2
mornot.ew m5, m1, m3  # m5 <- m1, m3

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m7
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m8, a0         # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m10, a0
mornot.ew m8, m0, m4  # m8-m9 <- m0-m1, m4-m5
mornot.ew m10, m2, m6 # m10-m11 <- m2-m3, m6-m7
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mornot_ew_x]]
==== `mornot.ew.x`

Computes the elementwise bitwise OR-NOT of a scalar and one integer M operand. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mornot.ew.x`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes M source operand `src` with shape `S` and data scalar `scalar`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. matrix source `src` and destination `dst` datatypes must be [.red]##identical## [.red]##integer## datatypes. scalar `scalar` is converted to the datatype of matrix source `src`, and the scalar `scalar` datatype may differ. Data scalar ``scalar`` must not have a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = scalar | ~src[i]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mornot_ew_x_i32_1x1(src_i32_1x1, scalar_i32);
__riscv_ztt_u16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mornot_ew_x_u16_1x2(src_u16_1x2, scalar_u16);
__riscv_ztt_u8_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mornot_ew_x_u8_4x1(src_u8_4x1, scalar_i32);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mornot_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x4, scalar_i32); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mornot_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_2x1, scalar_i32); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mornot_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i16_1x2, scalar_i32); // Data source and dst datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mornot_ew_x_f32_1x2(src_f32_1x2, scalar_i32); // Matrix datatypes must be integer
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mornot_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, (__riscv_ztt_i4_scalar_t)0); // 4-bit Scalar types are unsupported
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mornot_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, 3); // Scalar requires an explicit dedicated Scalar type
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_scalar_t scalar = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mornot_ew_x_i16_1x2(src, scalar);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0
# scalar: a1
# result: m1
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2   # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m1, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mornot.ew.x m1, a1, m0 # m1 <- m0, a1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# scalar: a1
# result: m2-m3
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2   # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m2, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m3, a0
mornot.ew.x m2, a1, m0 # m2 <- m0, a1
mornot.ew.x m3, a1, m1 # m3 <- m1, a1

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# scalar: a1
# result: m4-m7
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2   # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m4, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m6, a0
mornot.ew.x m4, a1, m0 # m4-m5 <- m0-m1, a1
mornot.ew.x m6, a1, m2 # m6-m7 <- m2-m3, a1
----

[[ztt-detail-mxor_ew]]
==== `mxor.ew`

Computes the elementwise bitwise XOR of two integer M operands. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mxor.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes M source operands `src1` and `src2` with the same integer datatype and shape `S`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. sources `src1`/`src2` and destination `dst` datatypes must be [.red]##identical## [.red]##integer## datatypes.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = src1[i] ^ src2[i]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mxor_ew_i32_1x1(src1_i32_1x1, src2_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_u16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mxor_ew_u16_1x2(src1_u16_1x2, src2_u16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_u8_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mxor_ew_u8_4x1(src1_u8_4x1, src2_u8_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mxor_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x4, src2_i32_1x2); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mxor_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mxor_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i16_1x2, src2_i32_1x2); // Data source and dst datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mxor_ew_f32_1x2(src1_f32_1x2, src2_f32_1x2); // Matrix datatypes must be integer
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mxor_ew_i16_1x2(src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1
# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m2, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mxor.ew m2, m0, m1  # m2 <- m0, m1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m3
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m4, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m5, a0
mxor.ew m4, m0, m2  # m4 <- m0, m2
mxor.ew m5, m1, m3  # m5 <- m1, m3

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m7
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m8, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m10, a0
mxor.ew m8, m0, m4  # m8-m9 <- m0-m1, m4-m5
mxor.ew m10, m2, m6 # m10-m11 <- m2-m3, m6-m7
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mxor_ew_x]]
==== `mxor.ew.x`

Computes the elementwise bitwise XOR of a scalar and one integer M operand. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mxor.ew.x`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes M source operand `src` with shape `S` and data scalar `scalar`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. matrix source `src` and destination `dst` datatypes must be [.red]##identical## [.red]##integer## datatypes. scalar `scalar` is converted to the datatype of matrix source `src`, and the scalar `scalar` datatype may differ. Data scalar ``scalar`` must not have a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = scalar ^ src[i]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mxor_ew_x_i32_1x1(src_i32_1x1, scalar_i32);
__riscv_ztt_u16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mxor_ew_x_u16_1x2(src_u16_1x2, scalar_u16);
__riscv_ztt_u8_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mxor_ew_x_u8_4x1(src_u8_4x1, scalar_i32);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mxor_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x4, scalar_i32); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mxor_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_2x1, scalar_i32); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mxor_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i16_1x2, scalar_i32); // Data source and dst datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mxor_ew_x_f32_1x2(src_f32_1x2, scalar_i32); // Matrix datatypes must be integer
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mxor_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, (__riscv_ztt_i4_scalar_t)0); // 4-bit Scalar types are unsupported
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mxor_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, 3); // Scalar requires an explicit dedicated Scalar type
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_scalar_t scalar = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mxor_ew_x_i16_1x2(src, scalar);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0
# scalar: a1
# result: m1
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2   # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m1, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mxor.ew.x m1, a1, m0 # m1 <- m0, a1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# scalar: a1
# result: m2-m3
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2   # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m2, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m3, a0
mxor.ew.x m2, a1, m0 # m2 <- m0, a1
mxor.ew.x m3, a1, m1 # m3 <- m1, a1

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# scalar: a1
# result: m4-m7
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2   # a2 holds the i32 scalar type encoding
msettyp m4, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m6, a0
mxor.ew.x m4, a1, m0 # m4-m5 <- m0-m1, a1
mxor.ew.x m6, a1, m2 # m6-m7 <- m2-m3, a1
----

[[ztt-detail-msll_ew]]
==== `msll.ew`

Computes the elementwise logical left shift using per-element matrix shift amounts. It maps directly to the spec instruction `msll.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes data M operand `src1` and matrix shift-amount operand `src2` with shape `S`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. Data source `src1`, destination `dst`, and matrix shift-amount `src2` datatypes must be [.red]##integer## datatypes, and the source element width must be a power of two; each `src2` element is interpreted as an [.red]##unsigned## raw bit pattern and reduced modulo the `src1` element width; data source `src1` and destination `dst` datatypes need not be identical.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = src1[i] << (src2[i] mod EW)
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_msll_ew_i32_1x1(src1_i32_1x1, src2_u32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_u16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msll_ew_u16_1x2(src1_u16_1x2, src2_u32_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msll_ew_i16_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_u16_1x2);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msll_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x4, src2_u32_1x2); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msll_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_u32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msll_ew_i32_1x2(src1_f32_1x2, src2_u32_1x2); // Data source must be integer
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msll_ew_f32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_u32_1x2); // dst must be integer
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msll_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_f32_1x2); // Shift-count matrix must be integer
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_u32_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_msll_ew_i16_1x2(src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1-m2
# result: m3
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m3, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msll.ew m3, m0, m1  # m3 <- m0, m1-m2

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m5
# result: m6-m7
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m6, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m7, a0
msll.ew m6, m0, m2  # m6 <- m0, m2-m3
msll.ew m7, m1, m4  # m7 <- m1, m4-m5

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m11
# result: m12-m15
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m12, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m14, a0
msll.ew m12, m0, m4 # m12-m13 <- m0-m1, m4-m7
msll.ew m14, m2, m8 # m14-m15 <- m2-m3, m8-m11
----

[[ztt-detail-msll_ew_x]]
==== `msll.ew.x`

Computes the elementwise logical left shift using one scalar shift amount. It maps directly to the spec instruction `msll.ew.x`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes data M operand `src` and `size_t` unsigned integer shift control scalar `sh`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The data source `src` and destination `dst` must be [.red]##integer## datatypes, and the source element width must be a power of two; source `src` and destination `dst` datatypes need not be identical. The shift amount `sh` is an [.red]##unsigned integer## control scalar and does not use `amestype`.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = src[i] << (sh mod EW)
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_msll_ew_x_i32_1x1(src_i32_1x1, (size_t)sh);
__riscv_ztt_u16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msll_ew_x_u16_1x2(src_u16_1x2, (size_t)sh);
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msll_ew_x_i16_1x2(src_i32_1x2, (size_t)sh);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msll_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x4, (size_t)sh); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msll_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_2x1, (size_t)sh); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msll_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_f32_1x2, (size_t)sh); // Data source must be integer
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msll_ew_x_f32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, (size_t)sh); // dst must be integer
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_msll_ew_x_i16_1x2(src, 3);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

li a1, 3
msettyp m2, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msll.ew.x m2, a1, m0 # m2 <- m0-m1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

li a1, 3
msettyp m4, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m5, a0
msll.ew.x m4, a1, m0 # m4 <- m0-m1
msll.ew.x m5, a1, m2 # m5 <- m2-m3

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m7
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

li a1, 3
msettyp m8, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m10, a0
msll.ew.x m8, a1, m0  # m8-m9 <- m0-m3
msll.ew.x m10, a1, m4 # m10-m11 <- m4-m7
----

[[ztt-detail-msra_ew]]
==== `msra.ew`

Computes the elementwise arithmetic right shift using per-element matrix shift amounts. It maps directly to the spec instruction `msra.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes signed-integer data M source operand `src1` and matrix shift-amount operand `src2` with shape `S`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The data source `src1` must be a [.red]##signed integer## datatype with a power-of-two element width; destination `dst` and matrix shift amount `src2` must have [.red]##integer## datatypes. Each `src2` element is interpreted as an [.red]##unsigned## raw bit pattern and reduced modulo the `src1` element width. Data source `src1` and destination `dst` datatypes need not be identical.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = src1[i] >>s (src2[i] mod EW)
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_msra_ew_i32_1x1(src1_i32_1x1, src2_u32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msra_ew_i16_1x2(src1_i16_1x2, src2_u32_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msra_ew_i16_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_u16_1x2);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msra_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x4, src2_u32_1x2); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msra_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_u32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msra_ew_i32_1x2(src1_f32_1x2, src2_u32_1x2); // Data source must be integer
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msra_ew_f32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_u32_1x2); // dst must be integer
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msra_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_f32_1x2); // Shift-count matrix must be integer
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msra_ew_i32_1x2(src1_u32_1x2, src2_u32_1x2); // Data source must be signed integer
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_u32_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_msra_ew_i16_1x2(src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1-m2
# result: m3
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m3, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msra.ew m3, m0, m1  # m3 <- m0, m1-m2

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m5
# result: m6-m7
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m6, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m7, a0
msra.ew m6, m0, m2  # m6 <- m0, m2-m3
msra.ew m7, m1, m4  # m7 <- m1, m4-m5

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m11
# result: m12-m15
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m12, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m14, a0
msra.ew m12, m0, m4 # m12-m13 <- m0-m1, m4-m7
msra.ew m14, m2, m8 # m14-m15 <- m2-m3, m8-m11

[[ztt-detail-msrl_ew]]
==== `msrl.ew`

Computes the elementwise logical right shift using per-element matrix shift amounts. It maps directly to the spec instruction `msrl.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes data M operand `src1` and matrix shift-amount operand `src2` with shape `S`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. Data source `src1`, destination `dst`, and matrix shift-amount `src2` datatypes must be [.red]##integer## datatypes, and the source element width must be a power of two; each `src2` element is interpreted as an [.red]##unsigned## raw bit pattern and reduced modulo the `src1` element width; data source `src1` and destination `dst` datatypes need not be identical.

[source,c]

[[ztt-detail-mcmovge_ew]]
==== `mcmovge.ew`

Preserves `old_d` or selects `src2` according to whether each `src1` element is greater than or equal to zero. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mcmovge.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes `old_d=M<TD,S>`, predicate M operand `src1=M<TP,S>`, and selected-data M operand `src2=M<TD,S>`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. Predicate operand `src1` must have an ordered numeric datatype. Old destination `old_d`, selected data source `src2`, and destination `dst` must have [.red]##identical## datatypes.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = (src1[i] >= 0) ? src2[i] : old_d[i]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcmovge_ew_i32_1x1(old_d_i32_1x1, src1_i32_1x1, src2_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcmovge_ew_bf16_1x2(old_d_bf16_1x2, src1_bf16_1x2, src2_bf16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_i32_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcmovge_ew_i32_4x1(old_d_i32_4x1, src1_i16_4x1, src2_i32_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcmovge_ew_i32_1x2(old_d_i32_1x2, src1_i32_1x4, src2_i32_1x2); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcmovge_ew_i32_1x2(old_d_i32_1x2, src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcmovge_ew_i32_1x2(old_d_i16_1x2, src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_1x2); // old_d and dst datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcmovge_ew_i32_1x2(old_d_i32_1x2, src1_i32_1x2, src2_i16_1x2); // src2 and dst datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcmovge_ew_i32_1x2(old_d_i32_1x4, src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_1x2); // old_d and dst shapes differ
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t old_d = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mcmovge_ew_i32_1x2(old_d, src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1-m2
# old_d/result: m3-m4
# Assembly mapping:

mcmovge.ew m3, m0, m1   # m3-m4 <- m3-m4, m0, m1-m2

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m5
# old_d/result: m6-m9
# Assembly mapping:

mcmovge.ew m6, m0, m2   # m6-m7 <- m6-m7, m0, m2-m3
mcmovge.ew m8, m1, m4   # m8-m9 <- m8-m9, m1, m4-m5

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m11
# old_d/result: m12-m19
# Assembly mapping:

mcmovge.ew m12, m0, m4  # m12-m15 <- m12-m15, m0-m1, m4-m7
mcmovge.ew m16, m2, m8  # m16-m19 <- m16-m19, m2-m3, m8-m11
----

[[ztt-detail-mcmovlt_ew]]
==== `mcmovlt.ew`

Preserves `old_d` or selects `src2` according to whether each `src1` element is less than zero. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mcmovlt.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes `old_d=M<TD,S>`, predicate M operand `src1=M<TP,S>`, and selected-data M operand `src2=M<TD,S>`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. Predicate operand `src1` must have an ordered numeric datatype. Old destination `old_d`, selected data source `src2`, and destination `dst` must have [.red]##identical## datatypes.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = (src1[i] < 0) ? src2[i] : old_d[i]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcmovlt_ew_i32_1x1(old_d_i32_1x1, src1_i32_1x1, src2_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcmovlt_ew_bf16_1x2(old_d_bf16_1x2, src1_bf16_1x2, src2_bf16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_i32_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcmovlt_ew_i32_4x1(old_d_i32_4x1, src1_i16_4x1, src2_i32_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcmovlt_ew_i32_1x2(old_d_i32_1x2, src1_i32_1x4, src2_i32_1x2); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcmovlt_ew_i32_1x2(old_d_i32_1x2, src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcmovlt_ew_i32_1x2(old_d_i16_1x2, src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_1x2); // old_d and dst datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcmovlt_ew_i32_1x2(old_d_i32_1x2, src1_i32_1x2, src2_i16_1x2); // src2 and dst datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcmovlt_ew_i32_1x2(old_d_i32_1x4, src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_1x2); // old_d and dst shapes differ
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t old_d = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mcmovlt_ew_i32_1x2(old_d, src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1-m2
# old_d/result: m3-m4
# Assembly mapping:

mcmovlt.ew m3, m0, m1   # m3-m4 <- m3-m4, m0, m1-m2

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m5
# old_d/result: m6-m9
# Assembly mapping:

mcmovlt.ew m6, m0, m2   # m6-m7 <- m6-m7, m0, m2-m3
mcmovlt.ew m8, m1, m4   # m8-m9 <- m8-m9, m1, m4-m5

----------------

mcmovlt.ew m12, m0, m4  # m12-m15 <- m12-m15, m0-m1, m4-m7

[[ztt-detail-mcmpge_ew]]
==== `mcmpge.ew`

Compares two M source operands elementwise with the greater-or-equal relation and writes an integer predicate result. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mcmpge.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes ordered numeric M source operands `src1` and `src2` with shape `S`; the result is an integer predicate matrix.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The comparison sources `src1`/`src2` must be ordered numeric datatypes, and the destination `dst` datatype must be an [.red]##integer## datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = (src1[i] >= src2[i]) ? -1 : 0
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcmpge_ew_i32_1x1(src1_i32_1x1, src2_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcmpge_ew_i32_1x2(src1_bf16_1x2, src2_bf16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_i32_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcmpge_ew_i32_4x1(src1_i8_4x1, src2_f32_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcmpge_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x4, src2_i32_1x2); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcmpge_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcmpge_ew_f32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_1x2); // dst must be integer
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mcmpge_ew_i16_1x2(src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1-m2
# result: m3
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m3, a0         # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mcmpge.ew m3, m0, m1  # m3 <- m0, m1-m2

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m5
# result: m6-m7
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m6, a0         # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m7, a0
mcmpge.ew m6, m0, m2  # m6 <- m0, m2-m3
mcmpge.ew m7, m1, m4  # m7 <- m1, m4-m5

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m11
# result: m12-m15
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m12, a0        # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m14, a0
mcmpge.ew m12, m0, m4 # m12-m13 <- m0-m1, m4-m7
mcmpge.ew m14, m2, m8 # m14-m15 <- m2-m3, m8-m11
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mcmpge_ew_x]]
==== `mcmpge.ew.x`

Compares a scalar and one M source operand elementwise with the greater-or-equal relation and writes an integer predicate result. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mcmpge.ew.x`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes ordered numeric M source operand `src` and data scalar `scalar`; the result is an integer predicate matrix.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The matrix source `src` and converted scalar `scalar` must be ordered numeric, and the destination `dst` datatype must be an [.red]##integer## datatype. The data scalar `scalar` is converted through `amestype`. Data scalar ``scalar`` must not have a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = (scalar >= src[i]) ? -1 : 0
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcmpge_ew_x_i32_1x1(src_i32_1x1, scalar_i32);
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcmpge_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_bf16_1x2, scalar_bf16);
__riscv_ztt_i32_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcmpge_ew_x_i32_4x1(src_f32_4x1, scalar_i8);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcmpge_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x4, scalar_i32); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcmpge_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_2x1, scalar_i32); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcmpge_ew_x_f32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, scalar_i32); // dst must be integer
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcmpge_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, (__riscv_ztt_i4_scalar_t)0); // 4-bit Scalar types are unsupported
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcmpge_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, 3); // Scalar requires an explicit dedicated Scalar type
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_scalar_t scalar = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mcmpge_ew_x_i16_1x2(src, scalar);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# scalar: a1
# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2   # a2 holds the i16 scalar type encoding
msettyp m2, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mcmpge.ew.x m2, a1, m0 # m2 <- m0-m1, a1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# scalar: a1
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2   # a2 holds the i16 scalar type encoding
msettyp m4, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m5, a0
mcmpge.ew.x m4, a1, m0 # m4 <- m0-m1, a1
mcmpge.ew.x m5, a1, m2 # m5 <- m2-m3, a1

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m7
# scalar: a1
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2   # a2 holds the i16 scalar type encoding
msettyp m8, a0      # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m10, a0
mcmpge.ew.x m8, a1, m0  # m8-m9 <- m0-m3, a1
mcmpge.ew.x m10, a1, m4 # m10-m11 <- m4-m7, a1
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mcmplt_ew]]
==== `mcmplt.ew`

Compares two M source operands elementwise with the less-than relation and writes an integer predicate result. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mcmplt.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes ordered numeric M source operands `src1` and `src2` with shape `S`; the result is an integer predicate matrix.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The comparison sources `src1`/`src2` must be ordered numeric datatypes, and the destination `dst` datatype must be an [.red]##integer## datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = (src1[i] < src2[i]) ? -1 : 0
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcmplt_ew_i32_1x1(src1_i32_1x1, src2_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcmplt_ew_i32_1x2(src1_bf16_1x2, src2_bf16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_i32_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcmplt_ew_i32_4x1(src1_i8_4x1, src2_f32_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----

__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcmplt_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcmplt_ew_f32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_1x2); // dst must be integer
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mcmplt_ew_i16_1x2(src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1-m2
# result: m3
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m3, a0         # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mcmplt.ew m3, m0, m1  # m3 <- m0, m1-m2

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m5
# result: m6-m7
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m6, a0         # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m7, a0
mcmplt.ew m6, m0, m2  # m6 <- m0, m2-m3
mcmplt.ew m7, m1, m4  # m7 <- m1, m4-m5

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m11
# result: m12-m15
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m12, a0        # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m14, a0
mcmplt.ew m12, m0, m4 # m12-m13 <- m0-m1, m4-m7
mcmplt.ew m14, m2, m8 # m14-m15 <- m2-m3, m8-m11
