[[ztt-detail-mselge_ew]]
==== `mselge.ew`

Selects `src2` or semantic zero according to whether each `src1` element is greater than or equal to zero. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mselge.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes predicate M operand `src1=M<TP,S>` and selected-data M operand `src2=M<TD,S>`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. Predicate operand `src1` must have an ordered numeric datatype. Selected data source `src2` and destination `dst` must have [.red]##identical## datatypes.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = (src1[i] >= 0) ? src2[i] : 0
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mselge_ew_i32_1x1(src1_i32_1x1, src2_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mselge_ew_bf16_1x2(src1_bf16_1x2, src2_bf16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_i32_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mselge_ew_i32_4x1(src1_i16_4x1, src2_i32_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mselge_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x4, src2_i32_1x2); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mselge_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mselge_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_i16_1x2); // src2 and dst datatypes differ
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mselge_ew_i32_1x2(src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1-m2
# result: m3-m4
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m3, a0         # a0 stores the i32 type encoding
msettyp m4, a0
mselge.ew m3, m0, m1  # m3-m4 <- m0, m1-m2

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m5
# result: m6-m9
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m6, a0         # a0 stores the i32 type encoding
msettyp m8, a0
mselge.ew m6, m0, m2  # m6-m7 <- m0, m2-m3
mselge.ew m8, m1, m4  # m8-m9 <- m1, m4-m5

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m11
# result: m12-m19
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m12, a0        # a0 stores the i32 type encoding
msettyp m16, a0
mselge.ew m12, m0, m4 # m12-m15 <- m0-m1, m4-m7
mselge.ew m16, m2, m8 # m16-m19 <- m2-m3, m8-m11
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-msellt_ew]]
==== `msellt.ew`

Selects `src2` or semantic zero according to whether each `src1` element is less than zero. It maps directly to the spec instruction `msellt.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes predicate M operand `src1=M<TP,S>` and selected-data M operand `src2=M<TD,S>`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. Predicate operand `src1` must have an ordered numeric datatype. Selected data source `src2` and destination `dst` must have [.red]##identical## datatypes.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = (src1[i] < 0) ? src2[i] : 0
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_msellt_ew_i32_1x1(src1_i32_1x1, src2_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msellt_ew_bf16_1x2(src1_bf16_1x2, src2_bf16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_i32_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_msellt_ew_i32_4x1(src1_i16_4x1, src2_i32_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msellt_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x4, src2_i32_1x2); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msellt_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msellt_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_i16_1x2); // src2 and dst datatypes differ
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_msellt_ew_i32_1x2(src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1-m2
# result: m3-m4
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m3, a0         # a0 stores the i32 type encoding
msettyp m4, a0
msellt.ew m3, m0, m1  # m3-m4 <- m0, m1-m2

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m5
# result: m6-m9
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m6, a0         # a0 stores the i32 type encoding
msettyp m8, a0
msellt.ew m6, m0, m2  # m6-m7 <- m0, m2-m3
msellt.ew m8, m1, m4  # m8-m9 <- m1, m4-m5

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m11
# result: m12-m19
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m12, a0        # a0 stores the i32 type encoding
msettyp m16, a0
msellt.ew m12, m0, m4 # m12-m15 <- m0-m1, m4-m7
msellt.ew m16, m2, m8 # m16-m19 <- m2-m3, m8-m11
----

[[ztt-detail-mexp2_ew]]
==== `mexp2.ew`

Computes the elementwise base-2 exponential. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mexp2.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes floating-point M source operand `src` with shape `S`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. source `src` and destination `dst` datatypes must be [.red]##floating-point## datatypes, and the exact `src`-to-`dst` tuple must be supported by hardware.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = pow2(src[i])
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mexp2_ew_f32_1x1(src_f32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mexp2_ew_bf16_1x2(src_bf16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_f32_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mexp2_ew_f32_4x1(src_bf16_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mexp2_ew_f32_1x2(src_f32_1x4); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mexp2_ew_f32_1x2(src_f32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mexp2_ew_f32_1x2(src_i32_1x2); // Data source must be floating-point
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mexp2_ew_i32_1x2(src_f32_1x2); // dst must be floating-point
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mexp2_ew_bf16_1x2(src);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m2, a0       # a0 holds the bf16 type encoding
mexp2.ew m2, m0     # m2 <- m0-m1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m4, a0       # a0 holds the bf16 type encoding
msettyp m5, a0
mexp2.ew m4, m0     # m4 <- m0-m1
mexp2.ew m5, m2      # m5 <- m2-m3

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m7
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m8, a0       # a0 holds the bf16 type encoding
msettyp m10, a0
mexp2.ew m8, m0     # m8-m9 <- m0-m3
mexp2.ew m10, m4     # m10-m11 <- m4-m7
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mldexp_ew]]
==== `mldexp.ew`

Scales each data element by a power of two provided by a matrix exponent operand. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mldexp.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes numeric data M source `src1` and integer exponent M operand `src2` with shape `S`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The data source `src1` and destination `dst` must be numeric datatypes. The exponent operand `src2` must have an [.red]##integer## datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = src1[i] * pow2(src2[i])
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mldexp_ew_f32_1x1(src1_f32_1x1, src2_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mldexp_ew_bf16_1x2(src1_bf16_1x2, src2_i32_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_f64_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mldexp_ew_f64_4x1(src1_i8_4x1, src2_i32_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mldexp_ew_f32_1x2(src1_f32_1x4, src2_i32_1x2); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mldexp_ew_f32_1x2(src1_f32_1x2, src2_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mldexp_ew_f32_1x2(src1_f32_1x2, src2_f32_1x2); // Exponent matrix must be integer
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mldexp_ew_i16_1x2(src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1-m2
# result: m3
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m3, a0          # a0 stores the i16 type encoding
mldexp.ew m3, m0, m1   # m3 <- m0, m1-m2

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m5
# result: m6-m7
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m6, a0          # a0 stores the i16 type encoding
msettyp m7, a0
mldexp.ew m6, m0, m2   # m6 <- m0, m2-m3
mldexp.ew m7, m1, m4   # m7 <- m1, m4-m5

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m11
# result: m12-m15
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m12, a0         # a0 stores the i16 type encoding
msettyp m14, a0
mldexp.ew m12, m0, m4  # m12-m13 <- m0-m1, m4-m7
mldexp.ew m14, m2, m8  # m14-m15 <- m2-m3, m8-m11
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mldexp_ew_x]]
==== `mldexp.ew.x`

Scales each data element by a power of two provided by a scalar exponent. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mldexp.ew.x`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes numeric data M source operand `src` and signed exponent control scalar `exp`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The data source `src` and destination `dst` must be numeric datatypes. exponent `exp` is the [.red]##signed integer## control scalar defined by the `.ew.x` form. A `.ew.x` tuple is unsupported if `AME_MAX_INT_DTYPE` is absent.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = src[i] * pow2(exp)
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mldexp_ew_x_f32_1x1(src_f32_1x1, (long)exp);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mldexp_ew_x_bf16_1x2(src_bf16_1x2, (long)exp);
__riscv_ztt_f64_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mldexp_ew_x_f64_4x1(src_i8_4x1, (long)exp);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mldexp_ew_x_f32_1x2(src_f32_1x4, (long)exp); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mldexp_ew_x_f32_1x2(src_f32_2x1, (long)exp); // Input and output shape orientations differ
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mldexp_ew_x_i16_1x2(src, -2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

li a1, -2
msettyp m2, a0          # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mldexp.ew.x m2, a1, m0  # m2 <- m0-m1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

li a1, -2
msettyp m4, a0          # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m5, a0
mldexp.ew.x m4, a1, m0  # m4 <- m0-m1
mldexp.ew.x m5, a1, m2  # m5 <- m2-m3

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m7
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

li a1, -2
msettyp m8, a0          # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m10, a0
mldexp.ew.x m8, a1, m0  # m8-m9 <- m0-m3
mldexp.ew.x m10, a1, m4 # m10-m11 <- m4-m7
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mldexpacc_ew]]
==== `mldexpacc.ew`

Accumulates a matrix-exponent ldexp result into an old destination value. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mldexpacc.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes `old_d=M<TD,S>`, numeric data M source operand `src1`, and integer exponent M operand `src2` with shape `S`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The data source `src1` and destination `dst` must be numeric datatypes. The exponent operand `src2` must have an [.red]##integer## datatype. `old_d` and destination `dst` must have the [.red]##same## type and size.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = old_d[i] + src1[i] * pow2(src2[i])
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mldexpacc_ew_f32_1x1(old_d_f32_1x1, src1_f32_1x1, src2_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mldexpacc_ew_bf16_1x2(old_d_bf16_1x2, src1_bf16_1x2, src2_i32_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_f64_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mldexpacc_ew_f64_4x1(old_d_f64_4x1, src1_i8_4x1, src2_i32_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mldexpacc_ew_f32_1x2(old_d_f32_1x2, src1_f32_1x4, src2_i32_1x2); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mldexpacc_ew_f32_1x2(old_d_f32_1x2, src1_f32_1x2, src2_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mldexpacc_ew_f32_1x2(old_d_bf16_1x2, src1_f32_1x2, src2_i32_1x2); // old_d and dst datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mldexpacc_ew_f32_1x2(old_d_f32_1x2, src1_f32_1x2, src2_f32_1x2); // Exponent matrix must be integer
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mldexpacc_ew_f32_1x2(old_d_f32_1x4, src1_f32_1x2, src2_i32_1x2); // old_d and dst shapes differ
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t old_d = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mldexpacc_ew_i16_1x2(old_d, src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1-m2
# old_d/result: m3
# Assembly mapping:

mldexpacc.ew m3, m0, m1   # m3 <- m3, m0, m1-m2

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m5
# old_d/result: m6-m7
# Assembly mapping:

mldexpacc.ew m6, m0, m2   # m6 <- m6, m0, m2-m3
mldexpacc.ew m7, m1, m4   # m7 <- m7, m1, m4-m5

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m11
# old_d/result: m12-m15
# Assembly mapping:

mldexpacc.ew m12, m0, m4  # m12-m13 <- m12-m13, m0-m1, m4-m7
mldexpacc.ew m14, m2, m8  # m14-m15 <- m14-m15, m2-m3, m8-m11
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mldexpacc_ew_x]]
==== `mldexpacc.ew.x`

Accumulates a scalar-exponent ldexp result into an old destination value. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mldexpacc.ew.x`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes `old_d=M<TD,S>`, numeric data M source operand `src`, and signed exponent control scalar `exp`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The data source `src` and destination `dst` must be numeric datatypes. exponent `exp` is the [.red]##signed integer## control scalar defined by the `.ew.x` form. `old_d` and destination `dst` must have the [.red]##same## type and size. A `.ew.x` tuple is unsupported if `AME_MAX_INT_DTYPE` is absent.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = old_d[i] + src[i] * pow2(exp)
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mldexpacc_ew_x_f32_1x1(old_d_f32_1x1, src_f32_1x1, (long)exp);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mldexpacc_ew_x_bf16_1x2(old_d_bf16_1x2, src_bf16_1x2, (long)exp);
__riscv_ztt_f64_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mldexpacc_ew_x_f64_4x1(old_d_f64_4x1, src_i8_4x1, (long)exp);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mldexpacc_ew_x_f32_1x2(old_d_f32_1x2, src_f32_1x4, (long)exp); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mldexpacc_ew_x_f32_1x2(old_d_f32_1x2, src_f32_2x1, (long)exp); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mldexpacc_ew_x_f32_1x2(old_d_bf16_1x2, src_f32_1x2, (long)exp); // old_d and dst datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mldexpacc_ew_x_f32_1x2(old_d_f32_1x4, src_f32_1x2, (long)exp); // old_d and dst shapes differ
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t old_d = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mldexpacc_ew_x_i16_1x2(old_d, src, -2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# old_d/result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

li a1, -2
mldexpacc.ew.x m2, a1, m0  # m2 <- m2, m0-m1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# old_d/result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

li a1, -2
mldexpacc.ew.x m4, a1, m0  # m4 <- m4, m0-m1
mldexpacc.ew.x m5, a1, m2  # m5 <- m5, m2-m3

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m7
# old_d/result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

li a1, -2
mldexpacc.ew.x m8, a1, m0  # m8-m9 <- m8-m9, m0-m3
mldexpacc.ew.x m10, a1, m4 # m10-m11 <- m10-m11, m4-m7
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mlog2_ew]]
==== `mlog2.ew`

Computes the elementwise base-2 logarithm. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mlog2.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes floating-point M source operand `src` with shape `S`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. source `src` and destination `dst` datatypes must be [.red]##floating-point## datatypes, and the exact `src`-to-`dst` tuple must be supported by hardware.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = log2(src[i])
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mlog2_ew_f32_1x1(src_f32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mlog2_ew_bf16_1x2(src_bf16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_f32_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mlog2_ew_f32_4x1(src_bf16_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mlog2_ew_f32_1x2(src_f32_1x4); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mlog2_ew_f32_1x2(src_f32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mlog2_ew_f32_1x2(src_i32_1x2); // Data source must be floating-point
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mlog2_ew_i32_1x2(src_f32_1x2); // dst must be floating-point
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mlog2_ew_bf16_1x2(src);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m2, a0       # a0 holds the bf16 type encoding
mlog2.ew m2, m0     # m2 <- m0-m1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m4, a0       # a0 holds the bf16 type encoding
msettyp m5, a0
mlog2.ew m4, m0     # m4 <- m0-m1
mlog2.ew m5, m2      # m5 <- m2-m3

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m7
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m8, a0       # a0 holds the bf16 type encoding
msettyp m10, a0
mlog2.ew m8, m0     # m8-m9 <- m0-m3
mlog2.ew m10, m4     # m10-m11 <- m4-m7
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mlog2sub_ew]]
==== `mlog2sub.ew`

Computes elementwise log2 minus a matrix bias operand. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mlog2sub.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes floating-point logarithm M source `src1` and numeric bias M operand `src2` with shape `S`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The logarithm data source `src1` and destination `dst` must be [.red]##floating-point## datatypes, while bias `src2` is a numeric datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = log2(abs(src1[i])) - src2[i]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mlog2sub_ew_f32_1x1(src1_f32_1x1, src2_f32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mlog2sub_ew_bf16_1x2(src1_bf16_1x2, src2_bf16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_f32_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mlog2sub_ew_f32_4x1(src1_bf16_4x1, src2_i16_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mlog2sub_ew_f32_1x2(src1_f32_1x4, src2_f32_1x2); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mlog2sub_ew_f32_1x2(src1_f32_1x2, src2_f32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mlog2sub_ew_f32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_f32_1x2); // Data source must be floating-point
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mlog2sub_ew_i32_1x2(src1_f32_1x2, src2_f32_1x2); // dst must be floating-point
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mlog2sub_ew_f32_1x2(src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m3
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m4, a0            # a0 stores the f32 type encoding
msettyp m5, a0
mlog2sub.ew m4, m0, m2   # m4 <- m0, m2
mlog2sub.ew m5, m1, m3   # m5 <- m1, m3

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m7
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m8, a0            # a0 stores the f32 type encoding
msettyp m10, a0
mlog2sub.ew m8, m0, m4   # m8-m9 <- m0-m1, m4-m5
mlog2sub.ew m10, m2, m6  # m10-m11 <- m2-m3, m6-m7

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m7
# src2: m8-m15
# result: m16-m23
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m16, a0           # a0 stores the f32 type encoding
msettyp m20, a0
mlog2sub.ew m16, m0, m8  # m16-m19 <- m0-m3, m8-m11
mlog2sub.ew m20, m4, m12 # m20-m23 <- m4-m7, m12-m15
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mlog2sub_ew_x]]
==== `mlog2sub.ew.x`

Computes elementwise log2 minus a scalar bias. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mlog2sub.ew.x`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes floating-point logarithm M source operand `src` and scalar bias `scalar`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The logarithm data source `src` and destination `dst` must be [.red]##floating-point## datatypes. scalar bias `scalar` is converted under the common scalar datatype rules. Data scalar ``scalar`` must not have a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = log2(abs(src[i])) - scalar
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mlog2sub_ew_x_f32_1x1(src_f32_1x1, scalar_f32);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mlog2sub_ew_x_bf16_1x2(src_bf16_1x2, scalar_bf16);
__riscv_ztt_f32_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mlog2sub_ew_x_f32_4x1(src_bf16_4x1, scalar_i16);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mlog2sub_ew_x_f32_1x2(src_f32_1x4, scalar_f32); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mlog2sub_ew_x_f32_1x2(src_f32_2x1, scalar_f32); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mlog2sub_ew_x_f32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, scalar_f32); // Data source must be floating-point
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mlog2sub_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_f32_1x2, scalar_f32); // dst must be floating-point
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mlog2sub_ew_x_f32_1x2(src_f32_1x2, (__riscv_ztt_i4_scalar_t)0); // 4-bit Scalar types are unsupported
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mlog2sub_ew_x_f32_1x2(src_f32_1x2, 3); // Scalar requires an explicit dedicated Scalar type
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_scalar_t scalar = ...;
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mlog2sub_ew_x_f32_1x2(src, scalar);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# scalar: a1
# result: m2-m3
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2      # a2 holds the i16 scalar type encoding
msettyp m2, a0         # a0 holds the f32 type encoding
msettyp m3, a0
mlog2sub.ew.x m2, a1, m0  # m2 <- m0, a1
mlog2sub.ew.x m3, a1, m1  # m3 <- m1, a1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# scalar: a1
# result: m4-m7
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2      # a2 holds the i16 scalar type encoding
msettyp m4, a0         # a0 holds the f32 type encoding
msettyp m6, a0
mlog2sub.ew.x m4, a1, m0  # m4-m5 <- m0-m1, a1
mlog2sub.ew.x m6, a1, m2  # m6-m7 <- m2-m3, a1

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m7
# scalar: a1
# result: m8-m15
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2       # a2 holds the i16 scalar type encoding
msettyp m8, a0          # a0 holds the f32 type encoding
msettyp m12, a0
mlog2sub.ew.x m8, a1, m0   # m8-m11 <- m0-m3, a1
mlog2sub.ew.x m12, a1, m4  # m12-m15 <- m4-m7, a1
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mrdexp_ew]]
==== `mrdexp.ew`

Applies the reverse exponent-scaling operation using a matrix exponent operand. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mrdexp.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes numeric data M source `src1` and integer exponent M operand `src2` with shape `S`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The data source `src1` and destination `dst` must be numeric datatypes. The exponent operand `src2` must have an [.red]##integer## datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = src1[i] * pow2(-src2[i])
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mrdexp_ew_f32_1x1(src1_f32_1x1, src2_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mrdexp_ew_bf16_1x2(src1_bf16_1x2, src2_i32_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_f64_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mrdexp_ew_f64_4x1(src1_i8_4x1, src2_i32_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mrdexp_ew_f32_1x2(src1_f32_1x4, src2_i32_1x2); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mrdexp_ew_f32_1x2(src1_f32_1x2, src2_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mrdexp_ew_f32_1x2(src1_f32_1x2, src2_f32_1x2); // Exponent matrix must be integer
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mrdexp_ew_i16_1x2(src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1-m2
# result: m3
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m3, a0          # a0 stores the i16 type encoding
mrdexp.ew m3, m0, m1   # m3 <- m0, m1-m2

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m5
# result: m6-m7
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m6, a0          # a0 stores the i16 type encoding
msettyp m7, a0
mrdexp.ew m6, m0, m2   # m6 <- m0, m2-m3
mrdexp.ew m7, m1, m4   # m7 <- m1, m4-m5

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m11
# result: m12-m15
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m12, a0         # a0 stores the i16 type encoding
msettyp m14, a0
mrdexp.ew m12, m0, m4  # m12-m13 <- m0-m1, m4-m7
mrdexp.ew m14, m2, m8  # m14-m15 <- m2-m3, m8-m11
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mrdexpacc_ew]]
==== `mrdexpacc.ew`

Accumulates the reverse exponent-scaling result into an old destination value. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mrdexpacc.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes `old_d=M<TD,S>`, numeric data M source operand `src1`, and integer exponent M operand `src2` with shape `S`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The data source `src1` and destination `dst` must be numeric datatypes. The exponent operand `src2` must have an [.red]##integer## datatype. `old_d` and destination `dst` must have the [.red]##same## type and size.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = old_d[i] + src1[i] * pow2(-src2[i])
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mrdexpacc_ew_f32_1x1(old_d_f32_1x1, src1_f32_1x1, src2_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mrdexpacc_ew_bf16_1x2(old_d_bf16_1x2, src1_bf16_1x2, src2_i32_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_f64_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mrdexpacc_ew_f64_4x1(old_d_f64_4x1, src1_i8_4x1, src2_i32_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mrdexpacc_ew_f32_1x2(old_d_f32_1x2, src1_f32_1x4, src2_i32_1x2); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mrdexpacc_ew_f32_1x2(old_d_f32_1x2, src1_f32_1x2, src2_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mrdexpacc_ew_f32_1x2(old_d_bf16_1x2, src1_f32_1x2, src2_i32_1x2); // old_d and dst datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mrdexpacc_ew_f32_1x2(old_d_f32_1x2, src1_f32_1x2, src2_f32_1x2); // Exponent matrix must be integer
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mrdexpacc_ew_f32_1x2(old_d_f32_1x4, src1_f32_1x2, src2_i32_1x2); // old_d and dst shapes differ
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t old_d = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mrdexpacc_ew_i16_1x2(old_d, src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1-m2
# old_d/result: m3
# Assembly mapping:

mrdexpacc.ew m3, m0, m1   # m3 <- m3, m0, m1-m2

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m5
# old_d/result: m6-m7
# Assembly mapping:

mrdexpacc.ew m6, m0, m2   # m6 <- m6, m0, m2-m3
mrdexpacc.ew m7, m1, m4   # m7 <- m7, m1, m4-m5

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m11
# old_d/result: m12-m15
# Assembly mapping:

mrdexpacc.ew m12, m0, m4  # m12-m13 <- m12-m13, m0-m1, m4-m7
mrdexpacc.ew m14, m2, m8  # m14-m15 <- m14-m15, m2-m3, m8-m11
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-msublog2_ew]]
==== `msublog2.ew`

Computes a matrix bias minus elementwise log2. It maps directly to the spec instruction `msublog2.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes floating-point logarithm M source `src1` and numeric bias M operand `src2` with shape `S`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The logarithm data source `src1` and destination `dst` must be [.red]##floating-point## datatypes, while bias `src2` is a numeric datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = src2[i] - log2(abs(src1[i]))
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_msublog2_ew_f32_1x1(src1_f32_1x1, src2_f32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msublog2_ew_bf16_1x2(src1_bf16_1x2, src2_bf16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_f32_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_msublog2_ew_f32_4x1(src1_bf16_4x1, src2_i16_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msublog2_ew_f32_1x2(src1_f32_1x4, src2_f32_1x2); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msublog2_ew_f32_1x2(src1_f32_1x2, src2_f32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msublog2_ew_f32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_f32_1x2); // Data source must be floating-point
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msublog2_ew_i32_1x2(src1_f32_1x2, src2_f32_1x2); // dst must be floating-point
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_msublog2_ew_f32_1x2(src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m3
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m4, a0            # a0 stores the f32 type encoding
msettyp m5, a0
msublog2.ew m4, m0, m2   # m4 <- m0, m2
msublog2.ew m5, m1, m3   # m5 <- m1, m3

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m7
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m8, a0            # a0 stores the f32 type encoding
msettyp m10, a0
msublog2.ew m8, m0, m4   # m8-m9 <- m0-m1, m4-m5
msublog2.ew m10, m2, m6  # m10-m11 <- m2-m3, m6-m7

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m7
# src2: m8-m15
# result: m16-m23
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m16, a0           # a0 stores the f32 type encoding
msettyp m20, a0
msublog2.ew m16, m0, m8  # m16-m19 <- m0-m3, m8-m11
msublog2.ew m20, m4, m12 # m20-m23 <- m4-m7, m12-m15
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-msublog2_ew_x]]
==== `msublog2.ew.x`

Computes a scalar bias minus elementwise log2. It maps directly to the spec instruction `msublog2.ew.x`.

C intrinsic data types: The result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic takes floating-point logarithm M source operand `src` and scalar bias `scalar`.

Type constraints: All M input and output shapes must be identical. The logarithm data source `src` and destination `dst` must be [.red]##floating-point## datatypes. scalar bias `scalar` is converted under the common scalar datatype rules. Data scalar ``scalar`` must not have a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = scalar - log2(abs(src[i]))
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_msublog2_ew_x_f32_1x1(src_f32_1x1, scalar_f32);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msublog2_ew_x_bf16_1x2(src_bf16_1x2, scalar_bf16);
__riscv_ztt_f32_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_msublog2_ew_x_f32_4x1(src_bf16_4x1, scalar_i16);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msublog2_ew_x_f32_1x2(src_f32_1x4, scalar_f32); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msublog2_ew_x_f32_1x2(src_f32_2x1, scalar_f32); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msublog2_ew_x_f32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, scalar_f32); // Data source must be floating-point
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msublog2_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_f32_1x2, scalar_f32); // dst must be floating-point
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msublog2_ew_x_f32_1x2(src_f32_1x2, (__riscv_ztt_i4_scalar_t)0); // 4-bit Scalar types are unsupported
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msublog2_ew_x_f32_1x2(src_f32_1x2, 3); // Scalar requires an explicit dedicated Scalar type
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_scalar_t scalar = ...;
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_msublog2_ew_x_f32_1x2(src, scalar);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# scalar: a1
# result: m2-m3
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2      # a2 holds the i16 scalar type encoding
msettyp m2, a0         # a0 holds the f32 type encoding
msettyp m3, a0
msublog2.ew.x m2, a1, m0  # m2 <- m0, a1
msublog2.ew.x m3, a1, m1  # m3 <- m1, a1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# scalar: a1
# result: m4-m7
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2      # a2 holds the i16 scalar type encoding
msettyp m4, a0         # a0 holds the f32 type encoding
msettyp m6, a0
msublog2.ew.x m4, a1, m0  # m4-m5 <- m0-m1, a1
msublog2.ew.x m6, a1, m2  # m6-m7 <- m2-m3, a1

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m7
# scalar: a1
# result: m8-m15
# Assembly mapping:

csrw amestype, a2       # a2 holds the i16 scalar type encoding
msettyp m8, a0          # a0 holds the f32 type encoding
msettyp m12, a0
msublog2.ew.x m8, a1, m0   # m8-m11 <- m0-m3, a1
msublog2.ew.x m12, a1, m4  # m12-m15 <- m4-m7, a1
----

<<<

__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msqrt_ew_f32_1x2(src_f32_1x4); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msqrt_ew_f32_1x2(src_f32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msqrt_ew_f32_1x2(src_i32_1x2); // Data source must be floating-point
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_msqrt_ew_i32_1x2(src_f32_1x2); // dst must be floating-point
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_msqrt_ew_bf16_1x2(src);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m2, a0        # a0 holds the bf16 type encoding
msqrt.ew m2, m0      # m2 <- m0-m1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m4, a0        # a0 holds the bf16 type encoding
msettyp m5, a0
msqrt.ew m4, m0      # m4 <- m0-m1
msqrt.ew m5, m2      # m5 <- m2-m3

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m7
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m8, a0        # a0 holds the bf16 type encoding
msettyp m10, a0
msqrt.ew m8, m0      # m8-m9 <- m0-m3
msqrt.ew m10, m4     # m10-m11 <- m4-m7
----

<<<

== 7. Reduction Operations

Design rule: concatenation shapes are allowed, and half-register shapes are rejected by the general type rules. For shapes containing multiple logical squares, the documented prefix or reduction transformation is applied independently to each logical square; row and column indices are local to that square.

=== 7.1 Reduction Operations

[cols=",,,",options="header",]
|=======================================================================
|Spec instruction |Intrinsic |Input/output types |Type constraints
|xref:ztt-detail-mprefixadd_col[`mprefixadd.col`] |+__riscv_ztt_mprefixadd_col_<TD>_<S>(a)+ |M<TD,S> <- M<TA,S> |Source `a` and destination `d` must be numeric datatypes; `a`-to-`d` conversion and `d`-datatype-domain addition must be supported

|xref:ztt-detail-mprefixadd_row[`mprefixadd.row`] |+__riscv_ztt_mprefixadd_row_<TD>_<S>(a)+ |M<TD,S> <- M<TA,S> |Source `a` and destination `d` must be numeric datatypes; `a`-to-`d` conversion and `d`-datatype-domain addition must be supported

|xref:ztt-detail-mreduceadd_col[`mreduceadd.col`] |+__riscv_ztt_mreduceadd_col_<TD>_<S>(a)+ |M<TD,S> <- M<TA,S> |Source `a` and destination `d` must be numeric datatypes; `a`-to-`d` conversion and `d`-datatype-domain addition must be supported

|xref:ztt-detail-mreduceadd_row[`mreduceadd.row`] |+__riscv_ztt_mreduceadd_row_<TD>_<S>(a)+ |M<TD,S> <- M<TA,S> |Source `a` and destination `d` must be numeric datatypes; `a`-to-`d` conversion and `d`-datatype-domain addition must be supported

|xref:ztt-detail-mprefixmax_col[`mprefixmax.col`] |+__riscv_ztt_mprefixmax_col_<TD>_<S>(a)+ |M<TD,S> <- M<TA,S> |Source `a` and destination `d` must be ordered numeric datatypes; `a`-to-`d` conversion and `d`-datatype-domain maximum must be supported

|xref:ztt-detail-mprefixmax_row[`mprefixmax.row`] |+__riscv_ztt_mprefixmax_row_<TD>_<S>(a)+ |M<TD,S> <- M<TA,S> |Source `a` and destination `d` must be ordered numeric datatypes; `a`-to-`d` conversion and `d`-datatype-domain maximum must be supported

|xref:ztt-detail-mreducemax_col[`mreducemax.col`] |+__riscv_ztt_mreducemax_col_<TD>_<S>(a)+ |M<TD,S> <- M<TA,S> |Source `a` and destination `d` must be ordered numeric datatypes; `a`-to-`d` conversion and `d`-datatype-domain maximum must be supported

|xref:ztt-detail-mreducemax_row[`mreducemax.row`] |+__riscv_ztt_mreducemax_row_<TD>_<S>(a)+ |M<TD,S> <- M<TA,S> |Source `a` and destination `d` must be ordered numeric datatypes; `a`-to-`d` conversion and `d`-datatype-domain maximum must be supported

[[ztt-detail-mprefixadd_col]]
==== `mprefixadd.col`

Independently computes an inclusive prefix sum down each column of every logical square. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mprefixadd.col`.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic returns result `dst=M<TD,S>` and takes M source operand `src=M<TA,S>` with the same shape `S`.

Type constraints: Concatenation shapes are allowed, and half-register shapes are rejected by the general type rules. source `src` and destination `dst` must be numeric datatypes. The exact `src`-to-`dst` conversion and `dst`-datatype-domain addition must be supported by hardware.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i,j] = sum(convert(src[k,j]), k = 0 .. i)
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mprefixadd_col_i32_1x1(src_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mprefixadd_col_bf16_1x2(src_bf16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_i16_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mprefixadd_col_i16_4x1(src_i32_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mprefixadd_col_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x4); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mprefixadd_col_i32_1x2(src_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mprefixadd_col_i32_1x2(acc_i32_accx2); // Source must be an M value
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mprefixadd_col_i16_1x2(src);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m2, a0             # a0 contains the i16 datatype encoding
mprefixadd.col m2, m0   # m2 <- m0-m1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m4, a0             # a0 contains the i16 datatype encoding
msettyp m5, a0
mprefixadd.col m4, m0   # m4 <- m0-m1
mprefixadd.col m5, m2   # m5 <- m2-m3

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m7
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m8, a0             # a0 contains the i16 datatype encoding
msettyp m10, a0
mprefixadd.col m8, m0   # m8-m9 <- m0-m3
mprefixadd.col m10, m4   # m10-m11 <- m4-m7
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mprefixadd_row]]
==== `mprefixadd.row`

Independently computes an inclusive prefix sum across each row of every logical square.It maps directly to the spec instruction `mprefixadd.row`.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic returns result `dst=M<TD,S>` and takes M source operand `src=M<TA,S>` with the same shape `S`.

Type constraints: Concatenation shapes are allowed, and half-register shapes are rejected by the general type rules. source `src` and destination `dst` must be numeric datatypes. The exact `src`-to-`dst` conversion and `dst`-datatype-domain addition must be supported by hardware.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i,j] = sum(convert(src[i,k]), k = 0 .. j)
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mprefixadd_row_i32_1x1(src_i32_1x1);

__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mprefixadd_row_i32_1x2(acc_i32_accx2); // Source must be an M value
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mprefixadd_row_i16_1x2(src);

# src: m0-m1
# result: m2

# result: m4-m5

----------------

# src: m0-m7
# result: m8-m11

[[ztt-detail-mprefixmax_col]]
==== `mprefixmax.col`

Independently computes an inclusive prefix maximum down each column of every logical square.It maps directly to the spec instruction `mprefixmax.col`.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic returns result `dst=M<TD,S>` and takes M source operand `src=M<TA,S>` with the same shape `S`.

Type constraints: Concatenation shapes are allowed, and half-register shapes are rejected by the general type rules. source `src` and destination `dst` must be ordered numeric datatypes. The exact `src`-to-`dst` conversion and `dst`-datatype-domain maximum must be supported by hardware.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i,j] = max(convert(src[k,j]), k = 0 .. i)
----

[[ztt-detail-mreduceadd_col]]
==== `mreduceadd.col`

Independently reduces each column of every logical square by addition and broadcasts the result to that column.It maps directly to the spec instruction `mreduceadd.col`.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic returns result `dst=M<TD,S>` and takes M source operand `src=M<TA,S>` with the same shape `S`.

Type constraints: Concatenation shapes are allowed, and half-register shapes are rejected by the general type rules. source `src` and destination `dst` must be numeric datatypes. The exact `src`-to-`dst` conversion and `dst`-datatype-domain addition must be supported by hardware.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[:,j] = sum(convert(src[k,j]), k = 0 .. N-1)
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mreduceadd_col_i32_1x1(src_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mreduceadd_col_bf16_1x2(src_bf16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_i16_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mreduceadd_col_i16_4x1(src_i32_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mreduceadd_col_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x4); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mreduceadd_col_i32_1x2(src_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mreduceadd_col_i32_1x2(acc_i32_accx2); // Source must be an M value
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mreduceadd_col_i16_1x2(src);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m2, a0             # a0 contains the i16 datatype encoding
mreduceadd.col m2, m0   # m2 <- m0-m1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m5, a0
mreduceadd.col m4, m0   # m4 <- m0-m1
mreduceadd.col m5, m2   # m5 <- m2-m3

----------------

# src: m0-m7
# result: m8-m11

msettyp m10, a0

mreduceadd.col m10, m4   # m10-m11 <- m4-m7
----

----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mreducemax_col_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x4); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mreducemax_col_i32_1x2(src_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mreducemax_col_i32_1x2(acc_i32_accx2); // Source must be an M value
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mreducemax_col_i16_1x2(src);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m2, a0             # a0 contains the i16 datatype encoding
mreducemax.col m2, m0   # m2 <- m0-m1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m4, a0             # a0 contains the i16 datatype encoding
msettyp m5, a0
mreducemax.col m4, m0   # m4 <- m0-m1
mreducemax.col m5, m2   # m5 <- m2-m3

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m7
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m8, a0             # a0 contains the i16 datatype encoding
msettyp m10, a0
mreducemax.col m8, m0   # m8-m9 <- m0-m3
mreducemax.col m10, m4   # m10-m11 <- m4-m7
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mreducemax_row]]
==== `mreducemax.row`

Independently reduces each row of every logical square by maximum and broadcasts the result to that row.It maps directly to the spec instruction `mreducemax.row`.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic returns result `dst=M<TD,S>` and takes M source operand `src=M<TA,S>` with the same shape `S`.

Type constraints: Concatenation shapes are allowed, and half-register shapes are rejected by the general type rules. source `src` and destination `dst` must be ordered numeric datatypes. The exact `src`-to-`dst` conversion and `dst`-datatype-domain maximum must be supported by hardware.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i,:] = max(convert(src[i,k]), k = 0 .. N-1)
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mreducemax_row_i32_1x1(src_i32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mreducemax_row_bf16_1x2(src_bf16_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_i16_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mreducemax_row_i16_4x1(src_i32_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mreducemax_row_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x4); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mreducemax_row_i32_1x2(src_i32_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mreducemax_row_i32_1x2(acc_i32_accx2); // Source must be an M value
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mreducemax_row_i16_1x2(src);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m2, a0             # a0 contains the i16 datatype encoding
mreducemax.row m2, m0   # m2 <- m0-m1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m4, a0             # a0 contains the i16 datatype encoding
msettyp m5, a0
mreducemax.row m4, m0   # m4 <- m0-m1
mreducemax.row m5, m2   # m5 <- m2-m3

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m7
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m8, a0             # a0 contains the i16 datatype encoding
msettyp m10, a0
mreducemax.row m8, m0   # m8-m9 <- m0-m3
mreducemax.row m10, m4   # m10-m11 <- m4-m7
----

[[ztt-detail-mreducemin_col]]
==== `mreducemin.col`

Independently reduces each column of every logical square by minimum and broadcasts the result to that column.It maps directly to the spec instruction `mreducemin.col`.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic returns result `dst=M<TD,S>` and takes M source operand `src=M<TA,S>` with the same shape `S`.

Type constraints: Concatenation shapes are allowed, and half-register shapes are rejected by the general type rules. source `src` and destination `dst` must be ordered numeric datatypes. The exact `src`-to-`dst` conversion and `dst`-datatype-domain minimum must be supported by hardware.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[:,j] = min(convert(src[k,j]), k = 0 .. N-1)
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----

__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mreducemin_col_i32_1x2(acc_i32_accx2); // Source must be an M value
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mreducemin_col_i16_1x2(src);

# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

----------------

# result: m4-m5

== 8. Permutation Operations

Design rule: zip/unzip take one M value of shape ``1x2`` and return an M value of the same datatype and shape, require ``bits(T) >= UDS``, and do not support packed datatypes. All other permutation intrinsics accept only basic ``1x1`` shapes and reject half-register shapes, concatenation shapes, and packed datatypes.

=== 8.1 Permutation Operations

[cols=",,,",options="header",]
|=======================================================================
|Spec instruction |Intrinsic |Input/output types |Type constraints
|xref:ztt-detail-mcolbcast_ew_x[`mcolbcast.ew.x`] |+__riscv_ztt_mcolbcast_ew_x_<TD>_<Sb>(a, size_t col)+ |M<TD,Sb> <- M<TD,Sb>, unsigned control scalar |Data source `a` and destination `d` datatypes must be [.red]##identical##; the low 32 bits of `col` are interpreted as unsigned and must satisfy `0 <= col < N`; only [.red]##basic shapes## are accepted; shape must be `1x1`.

|xref:ztt-detail-mrowbcast_ew_x[`mrowbcast.ew.x`] |+__riscv_ztt_mrowbcast_ew_x_<TD>_<Sb>(a, size_t row)+ |M<TD,Sb> <- M<TD,Sb>, unsigned control scalar |Data source `a` and destination `d` datatypes must be [.red]##identical##; the low 32 bits of `row` are interpreted as unsigned and must satisfy `0 <= row < N`; only [.red]##basic shapes## are accepted; shape must be `1x1`.

|xref:ztt-detail-mcolshift_ew_x[`mcolshift.ew.x`] |+__riscv_ztt_mcolshift_ew_x_<TD>_<Sb>(a, int offset)+ |M<TD,Sb> <- M<TD,Sb>, signed control scalar |Data source `a` and destination `d` datatypes must be [.red]##identical##; `offset` uses the low 32 bits interpreted as a [.red]##signed integer##; only [.red]##basic shapes## are accepted; shape must be `1x1`.

|xref:ztt-detail-mrowshift_ew_x[`mrowshift.ew.x`] |+__riscv_ztt_mrowshift_ew_x_<TD>_<Sb>(a, int offset)+ |M<TD,Sb> <- M<TD,Sb>, signed control scalar |Data source `a` and destination `d` datatypes must be [.red]##identical##; `offset` uses the low 32 bits interpreted as a [.red]##signed integer##; only [.red]##basic shapes## are accepted; shape must be `1x1`.

|xref:ztt-detail-mcolgather_ew[`mcolgather.ew`] |+__riscv_ztt_mcolgather_ew_<TD>_<Sb>(a,b)+ |M<TD,Sb> <- M<TD,Sb>, M<Tint,Sb> |Data source `a` and destination `d` datatypes must be [.red]##identical##; index operand must be [.red]##integer##; only [.red]##basic shapes## are accepted; shape must be `1x1`.

|xref:ztt-detail-mrowgather_ew[`mrowgather.ew`] |+__riscv_ztt_mrowgather_ew_<TD>_<Sb>(a,b)+ |M<TD,Sb> <- M<TD,Sb>, M<Tint,Sb> |Data source `a` and destination `d` datatypes must be [.red]##identical##; index operand must be [.red]##integer##; only [.red]##basic shapes## are accepted; shape must be `1x1`.

|xref:ztt-detail-mcolscatadd_ew[`mcolscatadd.ew`] |+__riscv_ztt_mcolscatadd_ew_<TD>_<Sb>(old_d,a,b)+ |M<TD,Sb> <- M<TD,Sb>, M<TA,Sb>, M<Tint,Sb> |Contribution source `a` must be numeric; index source `b` must be [.red]##integer##; old destination `old_d` and result `d` use the [.red]##same## destination datatype; only [.red]##basic shapes## are accepted; shape must be `1x1`.

|xref:ztt-detail-mrowscatadd_ew[`mrowscatadd.ew`] |+__riscv_ztt_mrowscatadd_ew_<TD>_<Sb>(old_d,a,b)+ |M<TD,Sb> <- M<TD,Sb>, M<TA,Sb>, M<Tint,Sb> |Contribution source `a` must be numeric; index source `b` must be [.red]##integer##; old destination `old_d` and result `d` use the [.red]##same## destination datatype; only [.red]##basic shapes## are accepted; shape must be `1x1`.

|xref:ztt-detail-mcolscatmax_ew[`mcolscatmax.ew`] |+__riscv_ztt_mcolscatmax_ew_<TD>_<Sb>(old_d,a,b)+ |M<TD,Sb> <- M<TD,Sb>, M<TA,Sb>, M<Tint,Sb> |Contribution source `a` must be ordered numeric; index source `b` must be [.red]##integer##; old destination `old_d` and result `d` use the [.red]##same## destination datatype; only [.red]##basic shapes## are accepted; shape must be `1x1`.

|xref:ztt-detail-mrowscatmax_ew[`mrowscatmax.ew`] |+__riscv_ztt_mrowscatmax_ew_<TD>_<Sb>(old_d,a,b)+ |M<TD,Sb> <- M<TD,Sb>, M<TA,Sb>, M<Tint,Sb> |Contribution source `a` must be ordered numeric; index source `b` must be [.red]##integer##; old destination `old_d` and result `d` use the [.red]##same## destination datatype; only [.red]##basic shapes## are accepted; shape must be `1x1`.

|xref:ztt-detail-mcolunzip_ew[`mcolunzip.ew`] |+M<T,1x2> __riscv_ztt_mcolunzip_ew_<T>_1x2(M<T,1x2> a)+ |M<T,1x2> <- M<T,1x2> |Source ``a`` and result ``d`` must have the [.red]##same## datatype; shape must be ``1x2``; element bit width must be [.red]##at least UDS##; bit-exact copying; compiler register allocation must ensure ``ms1 != ms2``.

|xref:ztt-detail-mrowunzip_ew[`mrowunzip.ew`] |+M<T,1x2> __riscv_ztt_mrowunzip_ew_<T>_1x2(M<T,1x2> a)+ |M<T,1x2> <- M<T,1x2> |Source ``a`` and result ``d`` must have the [.red]##same## datatype; shape must be ``1x2``; element bit width must be [.red]##at least UDS##; bit-exact copying; compiler register allocation must ensure ``ms1 != ms2``.

|xref:ztt-detail-mcolzip_ew[`mcolzip.ew`] |+M<T,1x2> __riscv_ztt_mcolzip_ew_<T>_1x2(M<T,1x2> a)+ |M<T,1x2> <- M<T,1x2> |Source ``a`` and result ``d`` must have the [.red]##same## datatype; shape must be ``1x2``; element bit width must be [.red]##at least UDS##; bit-exact copying; compiler register allocation must ensure ``ms1 != ms2``.

|xref:ztt-detail-mrowzip_ew[`mrowzip.ew`] |+M<T,1x2> __riscv_ztt_mrowzip_ew_<T>_1x2(M<T,1x2> a)+ |M<T,1x2> <- M<T,1x2> |Source ``a`` and result ``d`` must have the [.red]##same## datatype; shape must be ``1x2``; element bit width must be [.red]##at least UDS##; bit-exact copying; compiler register allocation must ensure ``ms1 != ms2``.

|=======================================================================

[[ztt-detail-mcolbcast_ew_x]]
==== `mcolbcast.ew.x`

Broadcasts one selected column across the columns of the result. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mcolbcast.ew.x`.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic returns result `dst=M<TD,Sb>` and takes a `size_t` unsigned integer column control scalar `col` plus basic-shape M source operand `src`.

Type constraints: The data source `src` and destination `dst` datatypes must be [.red]##identical##. The low 32 bits of column selector `col` are interpreted as an [.red]##unsigned integer## and must satisfy `0 <= col < N`; an out-of-range value raises Illegal Instruction. Only [.red]##basic shapes## are accepted; shape must be `1x1`; half-register and concatenation shapes are rejected.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i,j] = src[i,col]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolbcast_ew_x_i32_1x1(src_i32_1x1, (size_t)col);
__riscv_ztt_u64_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolbcast_ew_x_u64_1x1(src_u64_1x1, (size_t)col);
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolbcast_ew_x_f32_1x1(src_f32_1x1, (size_t)col);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolbcast_ew_x_i32_1x1(src_f32_1x1, (size_t)col); // Source and result datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcolbcast_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, (size_t)col); // Only 1x1 shapes are supported
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mcolbcast_ew_x_i32_1x1(src, 1);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0
# result: m1
# Assembly mapping:

li a1, 1
msettyp m1, a0                 # a0 contains the i32 datatype encoding
mcolbcast.ew.x m1, a1, m0     # m1 <- a1, m0

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# result: m2-m3
# Assembly mapping:

li a1, 1
msettyp m2, a0                 # a0 contains the i32 datatype encoding
mcolbcast.ew.x m2, a1, m0     # m2-m3 <- a1, m0-m1

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# result: m4-m7
# Assembly mapping:

li a1, 1
msettyp m4, a0                 # a0 contains the i32 datatype encoding
mcolbcast.ew.x m4, a1, m0     # m4-m7 <- a1, m0-m3
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mrowbcast_ew_x]]
==== `mrowbcast.ew.x`

Broadcasts one selected row across the rows of the result. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mrowbcast.ew.x`.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic returns result `dst=M<TD,Sb>` and takes a `size_t` unsigned integer row control scalar `row` plus basic-shape M source operand `src`.

Type constraints: The data source `src` and destination `dst` datatypes must be [.red]##identical##. The low 32 bits of row selector `row` are interpreted as an [.red]##unsigned integer## and must satisfy `0 <= row < N`; an out-of-range value raises Illegal Instruction. Only [.red]##basic shapes## are accepted; shape must be `1x1`; half-register and concatenation shapes are rejected.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i,j] = src[row,j]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mrowbcast_ew_x_i32_1x1(src_i32_1x1, (size_t)row);
__riscv_ztt_u64_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mrowbcast_ew_x_u64_1x1(src_u64_1x1, (size_t)row);
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mrowbcast_ew_x_f32_1x1(src_f32_1x1, (size_t)row);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mrowbcast_ew_x_i32_1x1(src_f32_1x1, (size_t)row); // Source and result datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mrowbcast_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, (size_t)row); // Only 1x1 shapes are supported
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mrowbcast_ew_x_i32_1x1(src, 1);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0
# result: m1
# Assembly mapping:

li a1, 1
msettyp m1, a0   # a0 contains the i32 datatype encoding
mrowbcast.ew.x m1, a1, m0   # m1 <- a1, m0

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# result: m2-m3
# Assembly mapping:

li a1, 1
msettyp m2, a0   # a0 contains the i32 datatype encoding
mrowbcast.ew.x m2, a1, m0   # m2-m3 <- a1, m0-m1

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# result: m4-m7
# Assembly mapping:

li a1, 1
msettyp m4, a0   # a0 contains the i32 datatype encoding
mrowbcast.ew.x m4, a1, m0   # m4-m7 <- a1, m0-m3
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mcolgather_ew]]
==== `mcolgather.ew`

Gathers elements from source columns selected by an integer index matrix. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mcolgather.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic returns result `dst=M<TD,Sb>` and takes data M source `src1` and integer matrix index operand `src2` with the same basic shape `Sb`.

Type constraints: The data source `src1` and destination `dst` datatypes must be [.red]##identical##, and the matrix index operand `src2` must have an [.red]##integer## datatype. Only [.red]##basic shapes## are accepted; shape must be `1x1`; half-register and concatenation shapes are rejected.

The low 32 raw bits of each index element form an unsigned U32 value; narrower values are zero-extended and higher bits are ignored. The effective column index is the low `log2(N)` bits of that value, so no index value raises an exception.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
idx[i,j] = effective_column_index(src2[i,j])
dst[i,j] = src1[i,idx[i,j]]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolgather_ew_i32_1x1(src1_i32_1x1, src2_u32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_u64_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolgather_ew_u64_1x1(src1_u64_1x1, src2_u32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolgather_ew_f32_1x1(src1_f32_1x1, src2_u32_1x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolgather_ew_i32_1x1(src1_i32_1x1, src2_f32_1x1); // Index matrix must be integer
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcolgather_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_u32_1x2); // Only 1x1 shapes are supported
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolgather_ew_i32_1x1(src1_f32_1x1, src2_u32_1x1); // src1 and dst datatypes differ
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_u32_1x1_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mcolgather_ew_i32_1x1(src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1
# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m2, a0   # a0 contains the i32 datatype encoding
mcolgather.ew m2, m0, m1   # m2 <- m0, m1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m3
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m4, a0   # a0 contains the i32 datatype encoding
mcolgather.ew m4, m0, m2   # m4-m5 <- m0-m1, m2-m3

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m7
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m8, a0   # a0 contains the i32 datatype encoding
mcolgather.ew m8, m0, m4   # m8-m11 <- m0-m3, m4-m7
----

[[ztt-detail-mrowgather_ew]]
==== `mrowgather.ew`

Gathers elements from source rows selected by an integer index matrix. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mrowgather.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic returns result `dst=M<TD,Sb>` and takes data M source `src1` and integer matrix index operand `src2` with the same basic shape `Sb`.

Type constraints: The data source `src1` and destination `dst` datatypes must be [.red]##identical##, and the matrix index operand `src2` must have an [.red]##integer## datatype. Only [.red]##basic shapes## are accepted; shape must be `1x1`; half-register and concatenation shapes are rejected.

The low 32 raw bits of each index element form an unsigned U32 value; narrower values are zero-extended and higher bits are ignored. The effective row index is the low `log2(N)` bits of that value, so no index value raises an exception.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
idx[i,j] = effective_row_index(src2[i,j])
dst[i,j] = src1[idx[i,j],j]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mrowgather_ew_i32_1x1(src1_i32_1x1, src2_u32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_u64_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mrowgather_ew_u64_1x1(src1_u64_1x1, src2_u32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mrowgather_ew_f32_1x1(src1_f32_1x1, src2_u32_1x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mrowgather_ew_i32_1x1(src1_i32_1x1, src2_f32_1x1); // Index matrix must be integer
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mrowgather_ew_i32_1x2(src1_i32_1x2, src2_u32_1x2); // Only 1x1 shapes are supported
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mrowgather_ew_i32_1x1(src1_f32_1x1, src2_u32_1x1); // src1 and dst datatypes differ
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_u32_1x1_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mrowgather_ew_i32_1x1(src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0
# src2: m1
# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m2, a0   # a0 contains the i32 datatype encoding
mrowgather.ew m2, m0, m1   # m2 <- m0, m1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m1
# src2: m2-m3
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m4, a0   # a0 contains the i32 datatype encoding
mrowgather.ew m4, m0, m2   # m4-m5 <- m0-m1, m2-m3

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src1: m0-m3
# src2: m4-m7
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m8, a0   # a0 contains the i32 datatype encoding
mrowgather.ew m8, m0, m4   # m8-m11 <- m0-m3, m4-m7
----

[[ztt-detail-mcolscatadd_ew]]
==== `mcolscatadd.ew`

Scatters contribution elements to indexed destination columns and accumulates by addition. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mcolscatadd.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic returns result `dst=M<TD,Sb>` and takes `old_d=M<TD,Sb>`, contribution M source `src1`, and integer matrix index source `src2`.

Type constraints: The contribution source `src1` must be numeric, the matrix index source `src2` must be [.red]##integer##, and the old destination `old_d` and result `dst` use the [.red]##same## destination datatype. Only [.red]##basic shapes## are accepted; shape must be `1x1`; half-register and concatenation shapes are rejected.

Index elements produce effective column indices using the same rules as gather. Source elements are processed in ascending logical row-major order; a later contribution to the same destination coordinate reads the staged result of the previous contribution.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
idx[i,j] = effective_column_index(src2[i,j])
dst = old_d
dst[i,idx[i,j]] += src1[i,j]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolscatadd_ew_i32_1x1(old_d_i32_1x1, src1_i32_1x1, src2_u32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_u64_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolscatadd_ew_u64_1x1(old_d_u64_1x1, src1_u64_1x1, src2_u32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_f64_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolscatadd_ew_f64_1x1(old_d_f64_1x1, src1_i8_1x1, src2_u32_1x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolscatadd_ew_i32_1x1(old_d_i32_1x1, src1_i32_1x1, src2_f32_1x1); // Index matrix must be integer
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcolscatadd_ew_i32_1x2(old_d_i32_1x2, src1_i32_1x2, src2_u32_1x2); // Only 1x1 shapes are supported
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolscatadd_ew_i32_1x1(old_d_f32_1x1, src1_i32_1x1, src2_u32_1x1); // old_d and dst datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolscatadd_ew_i32_1x1(old_d_i32_1x2, src1_i32_1x1, src2_u32_1x1); // old_d and dst shapes differ
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t old_d = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_u32_1x1_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mcolscatadd_ew_i32_1x1(old_d, src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# old_d/result: m0
# src1: m1
# src2: m2
# Assembly mapping:

mcolscatadd.ew m0, m1, m2   # m0 <- m0, m1, m2

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# old_d/result: m0-m1
# src1: m2-m3
# src2: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

mcolscatadd.ew m0, m2, m4   # m0-m1 <- m0-m1, m2-m3, m4-m5

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# old_d/result: m0-m3
# src1: m4-m7
# src2: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

mcolscatadd.ew m0, m4, m8   # m0-m3 <- m0-m3, m4-m7, m8-m11
----

Only [.red]##basic shapes## are accepted; shape must be `1x1`; half-register and concatenation shapes are rejected.

Index elements produce effective row indices using the same rules as gather. Source elements are processed in ascending logical row-major order; a later contribution to the same destination coordinate reads the staged result of the previous contribution.

[[ztt-detail-mcolscatmax_ew]]
==== `mcolscatmax.ew`

Scatters contribution elements to indexed destination columns and accumulates by maximum. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mcolscatmax.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic returns result `dst=M<TD,Sb>` and takes `old_d=M<TD,Sb>`, contribution M source `src1`, and integer matrix index source `src2`.

Type constraints: The contribution source `src1` must be ordered numeric, the matrix index source `src2` must be [.red]##integer##, and the old destination `old_d` and result `dst` use the [.red]##same## destination datatype. Only [.red]##basic shapes## are accepted; shape must be `1x1`; half-register and concatenation shapes are rejected.

Index elements produce effective column indices using the same rules as gather. Source elements are processed in ascending logical row-major order; a later contribution to the same destination coordinate reads the staged result of the previous contribution.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
idx[i,j] = effective_column_index(src2[i,j])
dst = old_d
dst[i,idx[i,j]] = max(dst[i,idx[i,j]], src1[i,j])
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolscatmax_ew_i32_1x1(old_d_i32_1x1, src1_i32_1x1, src2_u32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_u64_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolscatmax_ew_u64_1x1(old_d_u64_1x1, src1_u64_1x1, src2_u32_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_f64_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolscatmax_ew_f64_1x1(old_d_f64_1x1, src1_i8_1x1, src2_u32_1x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolscatmax_ew_i32_1x1(old_d_i32_1x1, src1_i32_1x1, src2_f32_1x1); // Index matrix must be integer
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcolscatmax_ew_i32_1x2(old_d_i32_1x2, src1_i32_1x2, src2_u32_1x2); // Only 1x1 shapes are supported
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolscatmax_ew_i32_1x1(old_d_f32_1x1, src1_i32_1x1, src2_u32_1x1); // old_d and dst datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolscatmax_ew_i32_1x1(old_d_i32_1x2, src1_i32_1x1, src2_u32_1x1); // old_d and dst shapes differ
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t old_d = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t src1 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_u32_1x1_t src2 = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mcolscatmax_ew_i32_1x1(old_d, src1, src2);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# old_d/result: m0
# src1: m1
# src2: m2
# Assembly mapping:

mcolscatmax.ew m0, m1, m2   # m0 <- m0, m1, m2

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# old_d/result: m0-m1
# src1: m2-m3
# src2: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

----------------

# src2: m8-m11
