[[ztt-detail-mcolshift_ew_x]]
==== `mcolshift.ew.x`

Shifts columns by one signed offset. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mcolshift.ew.x`.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic returns result `dst=M<TD,Sb>` and takes an `int` signed offset control scalar `offset` plus basic-shape M source operand `src`.

Type constraints: The data source `src` and destination `dst` datatypes must be [.red]##identical##. The low 32 bits of offset `offset` are interpreted as a two's-complement [.red]##signed integer##. Only [.red]##basic shapes## are accepted; shape must be `1x1`; half-register and concatenation shapes are rejected.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i,j] = (0 <= j + offset < N) ? src[i,j + offset] : zero(dst_type)
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolshift_ew_x_i32_1x1(src_i32_1x1, (int)offset);
__riscv_ztt_u64_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolshift_ew_x_u64_1x1(src_u64_1x1, (int)offset);
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolshift_ew_x_f32_1x1(src_f32_1x1, (int)offset);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolshift_ew_x_i32_1x1(src_f32_1x1, (int)offset); // Source and result datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcolshift_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, (int)offset); // Only 1x1 shapes are supported
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolshift_ew_x_i32_1x1(src_i32_1x1, src2_i32_1x1); // offset cannot be an M value
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mcolshift_ew_x_i32_1x1(src, 1);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0
# result: m1
# Assembly mapping:

li a1, 1
msettyp m1, a0   # a0 contains the i32 datatype encoding
mcolshift.ew.x m1, a1, m0   # m1 <- a1, m0

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# result: m2-m3
# Assembly mapping:

li a1, 1
msettyp m2, a0   # a0 contains the i32 datatype encoding
mcolshift.ew.x m2, a1, m0   # m2-m3 <- a1, m0-m1

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# result: m4-m7
# Assembly mapping:

li a1, 1
msettyp m4, a0   # a0 contains the i32 datatype encoding
mcolshift.ew.x m4, a1, m0   # m4-m7 <- a1, m0-m3
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mrowshift_ew_x]]
==== `mrowshift.ew.x`

Shifts rows by one signed offset. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mrowshift.ew.x`.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic returns result `dst=M<TD,Sb>` and takes an `int` signed offset control scalar `offset` plus basic-shape M source operand `src`.

Type constraints: The data source `src` and destination `dst` datatypes must be [.red]##identical##. The low 32 bits of offset `offset` are interpreted as a two's-complement [.red]##signed integer##. Only [.red]##basic shapes## are accepted; shape must be `1x1`; half-register and concatenation shapes are rejected.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i,j] = (0 <= i + offset < N) ? src[i + offset,j] : zero(dst_type)
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mrowshift_ew_x_i32_1x1(src_i32_1x1, (int)offset);
__riscv_ztt_u64_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mrowshift_ew_x_u64_1x1(src_u64_1x1, (int)offset);
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mrowshift_ew_x_f32_1x1(src_f32_1x1, (int)offset);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mrowshift_ew_x_i32_1x1(src_f32_1x1, (int)offset); // Source and result datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mrowshift_ew_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2, (int)offset); // Only 1x1 shapes are supported
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mrowshift_ew_x_i32_1x1(src_i32_1x1, src2_i32_1x1); // offset cannot be an M value
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mrowshift_ew_x_i32_1x1(src, 1);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0
# result: m1
# Assembly mapping:

li a1, 1
msettyp m1, a0   # a0 contains the i32 datatype encoding
mrowshift.ew.x m1, a1, m0   # m1 <- a1, m0

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# result: m2-m3
# Assembly mapping:

li a1, 1
msettyp m2, a0   # a0 contains the i32 datatype encoding
mrowshift.ew.x m2, a1, m0   # m2-m3 <- a1, m0-m1

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# result: m4-m7
# Assembly mapping:

li a1, 1
msettyp m4, a0   # a0 contains the i32 datatype encoding
mrowshift.ew.x m4, a1, m0   # m4-m7 <- a1, m0-m3
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mcolunzip_ew]]
==== `mcolunzip.ew`

De-interleaves the columns of the two squares in source ``src`` and returns result ``dst``. It maps to the spec instruction ``mcolunzip.ew``.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic takes source ``src=M<T,1x2>`` and returns result ``dst=M<T,1x2>`` without modifying the C source value ``src``.

Type constraints: Source ``src`` and result ``dst`` must have the [.red]##same## datatype and shape ``1x2``. The element bit width must be [.red]##at least UDS##, i.e. ``bits(T) >= UDS``; packed datatypes are unsupported. For example, ``i16_1x2`` is supported only on machines with ``UDS <= 16``. Elements are copied bit-for-bit. The compiler must allocate distinct base registers for the two squares, aligned to the wide register-group size, ensuring ``ms1 != ms2``.

Copy the old value of ``src`` to other M register slots using ``mmov.m.m``, then perform zip/unzip in place on the original M registers, which hold the result. The compiler may optimize away the copy operations when the value of ``src`` has no subsequent uses.

Logical operation:

The first subscript below is the square index; 0 <= i < N, 0 <= j < N/2.

[source,c]
----
dst[0,i,j] = src[0,i,2*j]
dst[0,i,N/2+j] = src[1,i,2*j]
dst[1,i,j] = src[0,i,2*j+1]
dst[1,i,N/2+j] = src[1,i,2*j+1]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcolunzip_ew_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_u64_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcolunzip_ew_u64_1x2(src_u64_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcolunzip_ew_f32_1x2(src_f32_1x2);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcolunzip_ew_i32_1x2(src_f32_1x2); // Source and result datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcolunzip_ew_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x1); // Source shape must be 1x2
__riscv_ztt_i32_2x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolunzip_ew_i32_2x1(src_i32_2x1); // Only the 1x2 shape is supported
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x4_t __riscv_ztt_mcolunzip_ew_i32_1x4(src_i32_1x4); // Only the 1x2 shape is supported
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcolunzip_ew_i32_1x2(&src_i32_1x2); // Argument must be an M value, not a pointer
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcolunzip_ew_i16_1x2(src_i16_1x2); // Element bit width is below UDS when UDS=32
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mcolunzip_ew_i32_1x2(src);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1 (old value copied to m2-m3)
# result: m0-m1 (modified in place)
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m2, a0   # a0 holds the i32 datatype encoding
msettyp m3, a0
mmov.m.m m2, m0   # m2 <- m0
mmov.m.m m3, m1   # m3 <- m1
mcolunzip.ew m0, m1   # m0, m1 <- m0, m1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3 (old value copied to m4-m7)
# result: m0-m3 (modified in place)
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m4, a0   # a0 holds the i32 datatype encoding
msettyp m6, a0
mmov.m.m m4, m0   # m4-m5 <- m0-m1
mmov.m.m m6, m2   # m6-m7 <- m2-m3
mcolunzip.ew m0, m2   # m0-m1, m2-m3 <- m0-m1, m2-m3

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m7 (old value copied to m8-m15)
# result: m0-m7 (modified in place)
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m8, a0   # a0 holds the i32 datatype encoding
msettyp m12, a0
mmov.m.m m8, m0   # m8-m11 <- m0-m3
mmov.m.m m12, m4   # m12-m15 <- m4-m7
mcolunzip.ew m0, m4   # m0-m3, m4-m7 <- m0-m3, m4-m7
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mrowunzip_ew]]
==== `mrowunzip.ew`

De-interleaves the rows of the two squares in source ``src`` and returns result ``dst``. It maps to the spec instruction ``mrowunzip.ew``.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic takes source ``src=M<T,1x2>`` and returns result ``dst=M<T,1x2>`` without modifying the C source value ``src``.

Type constraints: Source ``src`` and result ``dst`` must have the [.red]##same## datatype and shape ``1x2``. The element bit width must be [.red]##at least UDS##, i.e. ``bits(T) >= UDS``; packed datatypes are unsupported. For example, ``i16_1x2`` is supported only on machines with ``UDS <= 16``. Elements are copied bit-for-bit. The compiler must allocate distinct base registers for the two squares, aligned to the wide register-group size, ensuring ``ms1 != ms2``.

Copy the old value of ``src`` to other M register slots using ``mmov.m.m``, then perform zip/unzip in place on the original M registers, which hold the result. The compiler may optimize away the copy operations when the value of ``src`` has no subsequent uses.

Logical operation:

The first subscript below is the square index; 0 <= k < N/2, 0 <= j < N.

[source,c]
----
dst[0,k,j] = src[0,2*k,j]
dst[0,k+N/2,j] = src[1,2*k,j]
dst[1,k,j] = src[0,2*k+1,j]
dst[1,k+N/2,j] = src[1,2*k+1,j]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mrowunzip_ew_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_u64_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mrowunzip_ew_u64_1x2(src_u64_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mrowunzip_ew_f32_1x2(src_f32_1x2);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mrowunzip_ew_i32_1x2(src_f32_1x2); // Source and result datatypes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mrowunzip_ew_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x1); // Source shape must be 1x2
__riscv_ztt_i32_2x1_t __riscv_ztt_mrowunzip_ew_i32_2x1(src_i32_2x1); // Only the 1x2 shape is supported
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x4_t __riscv_ztt_mrowunzip_ew_i32_1x4(src_i32_1x4); // Only the 1x2 shape is supported
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mrowunzip_ew_i32_1x2(&src_i32_1x2); // Argument must be an M value, not a pointer
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mrowunzip_ew_i16_1x2(src_i16_1x2); // Element bit width is below UDS when UDS=32
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mrowunzip_ew_i32_1x2(src);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1 (old value copied to m2-m3)
# result: m0-m1 (modified in place)
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m2, a0   # a0 holds the i32 datatype encoding
msettyp m3, a0
mmov.m.m m2, m0   # m2 <- m0
mmov.m.m m3, m1   # m3 <- m1
mrowunzip.ew m0, m1   # m0, m1 <- m0, m1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3 (old value copied to m4-m7)
# result: m0-m3 (modified in place)
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m4, a0   # a0 holds the i32 datatype encoding
msettyp m6, a0
mmov.m.m m4, m0   # m4-m5 <- m0-m1
mmov.m.m m6, m2   # m6-m7 <- m2-m3
mrowunzip.ew m0, m2   # m0-m1, m2-m3 <- m0-m1, m2-m3

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m7 (old value copied to m8-m15)
# result: m0-m7 (modified in place)
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m8, a0   # a0 holds the i32 datatype encoding
msettyp m12, a0
mmov.m.m m8, m0   # m8-m11 <- m0-m3
mmov.m.m m12, m4   # m12-m15 <- m4-m7
mrowunzip.ew m0, m4   # m0-m3, m4-m7 <- m0-m3, m4-m7

[[ztt-detail-mcolzip_ew]]
==== `mcolzip.ew`

Interleaves the columns of the two squares in source ``src`` and returns result ``dst``. It maps to the spec instruction ``mcolzip.ew``.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic takes source ``src=M<T,1x2>`` and returns result ``dst=M<T,1x2>`` without modifying the C source value ``src``.

Type constraints: Source ``src`` and result ``dst`` must have the [.red]##same## datatype and shape ``1x2``. The element bit width must be [.red]##at least UDS##, i.e. ``bits(T) >= UDS``; packed datatypes are unsupported. For example, ``i16_1x2`` is supported only on machines with ``UDS <= 16``. Elements are copied bit-for-bit. The compiler must allocate distinct base registers for the two squares, aligned to the wide register-group size, ensuring ``ms1 != ms2``.

Copy the old value of ``src`` to other M register slots using ``mmov.m.m``, then perform zip/unzip in place on the original M registers, which hold the result. The compiler may optimize away the copy operations when the value of ``src`` has no subsequent uses.

Logical operation:

The first subscript below is the square index; 0 <= i < N, 0 <= j < N/2.

[source,c]
----
dst[0,i,2*j] = src[0,i,j]
dst[0,i,2*j+1] = src[1,i,j]
dst[1,i,2*j] = src[0,i,N/2+j]
dst[1,i,2*j+1] = src[1,i,N/2+j]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----

__riscv_ztt_u64_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcolzip_ew_u64_1x2(src_u64_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcolzip_ew_f32_1x2(src_f32_1x2);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----

__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcolzip_ew_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x1); // Source shape must be 1x2
__riscv_ztt_i32_2x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolzip_ew_i32_2x1(src_i32_2x1); // Only the 1x2 shape is supported
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x4_t __riscv_ztt_mcolzip_ew_i32_1x4(src_i32_1x4); // Only the 1x2 shape is supported
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcolzip_ew_i32_1x2(&src_i32_1x2); // Argument must be an M value, not a pointer
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcolzip_ew_i16_1x2(src_i16_1x2); // Element bit width is below UDS when UDS=32
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mcolzip_ew_i32_1x2(src);

# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1 (old value copied to m2-m3)
# result: m0-m1 (modified in place)
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m2, a0   # a0 holds the i32 datatype encoding
msettyp m3, a0
mmov.m.m m2, m0   # m2 <- m0
mmov.m.m m3, m1   # m3 <- m1
mcolzip.ew m0, m1   # m0, m1 <- m0, m1

msettyp m6, a0

mmov.m.m m6, m2   # m6-m7 <- m2-m3
mcolzip.ew m0, m2   # m0-m1, m2-m3 <- m0-m1, m2-m3

mmov.m.m m12, m4   # m12-m15 <- m4-m7
mcolzip.ew m0, m4   # m0-m3, m4-m7 <- m0-m3, m4-m7

[[ztt-detail-mrowzip_ew]]
==== `mrowzip.ew`

Interleaves the rows of the two squares in source ``src`` and returns result ``dst``. It maps to the spec instruction ``mrowzip.ew``.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic takes source ``src=M<T,1x2>`` and returns result ``dst=M<T,1x2>`` without modifying the C source value ``src``.

Type constraints: Source ``src`` and result ``dst`` must have the [.red]##same## datatype and shape ``1x2``. The element bit width must be [.red]##at least UDS##, i.e. ``bits(T) >= UDS``; packed datatypes are unsupported. For example, ``i16_1x2`` is supported only on machines with ``UDS <= 16``. Elements are copied bit-for-bit. The compiler must allocate distinct base registers for the two squares, aligned to the wide register-group size, ensuring ``ms1 != ms2``.

Copy the old value of ``src`` to other M register slots using ``mmov.m.m``, then perform zip/unzip in place on the original M registers, which hold the result. The compiler may optimize away the copy operations when the value of ``src`` has no subsequent uses.

The first subscript below is the square index; 0 <= k < N/2, 0 <= j < N.

[source,c]

dst[0,2*k,j] = src[0,k,j]
dst[0,2*k+1,j] = src[1,k,j]

== 9. Register Move / Data Conversion

=== 9.1 Register Move / Data Conversion

[cols=",,,",options="header",]
|=======================================================================
|Spec instruction |Intrinsic |Input/output types |Type constraints
|xref:ztt-detail-mbcast_m_x[`mbcast.m.x`] |+M<TD,S> __riscv_ztt_mbcast_m_x_<TD>_<S>(Scalar<TC> c)+ |X c to M |Named source-to-destination conversion tuple must be supported; the intrinsic keeps only one source parameter, and the compiler encodes the c datatype from `Scalar<TC>` into `rs2`. Data scalar ``c`` must not have a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

|xref:ztt-detail-mconv_ew[`mconv.ew`] |+M<TD,S> __riscv_ztt_mconv_ew_<TD>_<S>(M<TA,S> a)+ |M-to-M conversion |Named source-to-destination conversion tuple must be supported; `S` must match; concatenation shapes are allowed.

|=======================================================================

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mbcast_m_x]]
==== `mbcast.m.x`

Broadcasts a scalar value from an X register into every element of an M operand. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mbcast.m.x`.

C intrinsic data types: Source `scalar` is `Scalar<TC>` and result `dst` is `M<TD,S>`. The intrinsic does not read `amestype` and has no explicit scalar-type immediate parameter; the compiler derives the scalar datatype from the `Scalar<TC>` typedef and encodes it into `rs2`.

Type constraints: The conversion tuple from scalar `scalar` to destination `dst` must be supported. Concatenation shapes are allowed, and half-register shapes are rejected by the general type rules. Data scalar ``scalar`` must not have a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = convert(scalar)
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mbcast_m_x_i32_1x1(scalar_i32);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mbcast_m_x_bf16_1x2(scalar_f32);
__riscv_ztt_f64_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mbcast_m_x_f64_4x1(scalar_i8);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mbcast_m_x_i32_1x2((__riscv_ztt_i4_scalar_t)0); // 4-bit Scalar types are unsupported
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mbcast_m_x_i32_1x2(src_i32_1x2); // Source must be a Scalar value
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mbcast_m_x_i32_1x2(3); // Scalar requires an explicit dedicated Scalar type
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_scalar_t scalar = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mbcast_m_x_i16_1x2(scalar);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# scalar: a0
# result: m0
# Assembly mapping:

lui a1, 0x40000
addi a1, a1, 0x20          # i32 datatype encoding
msettyp m0, a2             # a2 contains the i16 datatype encoding
mbcast.m.x m0, a0, a1      # m0 <- a0, a1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# scalar: a0
# result: m0-m1
# Assembly mapping:

lui a1, 0x40000
addi a1, a1, 0x20          # i32 datatype encoding
msettyp m0, a2             # a2 contains the i16 datatype encoding
msettyp m1, a2
mbcast.m.x m0, a0, a1      # m0 <- a0, a1
mbcast.m.x m1, a0, a1      # m1 <- a0, a1

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# scalar: a0
# result: m0-m3
# Assembly mapping:

lui a1, 0x40000
addi a1, a1, 0x20          # i32 datatype encoding
msettyp m0, a2             # a2 contains the i16 datatype encoding
msettyp m2, a2
mbcast.m.x m0, a0, a1      # m0-m1 <- a0, a1
mbcast.m.x m2, a0, a1      # m2-m3 <- a0, a1
----


<<<

[[ztt-detail-mconv_ew]]
==== `mconv.ew`

Converts an M value from one datatype to another supported datatype. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mconv.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic uses M C types; source `src` and destination `dst` datatypes may differ.

Type constraints: The named `src`-to-`dst` conversion tuple must be supported. Concatenation shapes are allowed by this intrinsic design; half-register shapes are rejected by the general type rules.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i] = convert(src[i])
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mconv_ew_i32_1x1(src_i16_1x1);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mconv_ew_bf16_1x2(src_f32_1x2);
__riscv_ztt_f64_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mconv_ew_f64_4x1(src_i8_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mconv_ew_i32_1x2(acc_i16_accx2); // Source must be an M value
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mconv_ew_i32_1x2(src_i16_1x4); // Input and output shapes differ
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mconv_ew_i32_1x2(src_i16_2x1); // Input and output shape orientations differ
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mconv_ew_i16_1x2(src);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m1
# result: m2
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m2, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
mconv.ew m2, m0      # m2 <- m0-m1

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m3
# result: m4-m5
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m4, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m5, a0
mconv.ew m4, m0      # m4 <- m0-m1
mconv.ew m5, m2      # m5 <- m2-m3

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# src: m0-m7
# result: m8-m11
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m8, a0       # a0 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m10, a0
mconv.ew m8, m0      # m8-m9 <- m0-m3
mconv.ew m10, m4     # m10-m11 <- m4-m7
----

Debug scalar element moves, `mpack.ew.x`, and `munpack.ew.x` are not exposed to ordinary users for now.

<<<

== 10. Matrix Construction

=== 10.1 Matrix Construction

[cols=",,,",options="header",]
|=======================================================================
|Spec instruction |Intrinsic |Input/output types |Type constraints
|xref:ztt-detail-mrowid_ew[`mrowid.ew`] |+M<Tint,1x1> __riscv_ztt_mrowid_ew_<Tint>_1x1(void)+ |Generates a row-index matrix |Destination must be an [.red]##integer## datatype that can exactly represent `sqrt(AME_NELEM) - 1`; only [.red]##basic shapes## are accepted; shape must be `1x1`.

|xref:ztt-detail-mcolid_ew[`mcolid.ew`] |+M<Tint,1x1> __riscv_ztt_mcolid_ew_<Tint>_1x1(void)+ |Generates a column-index matrix |Destination must be an [.red]##integer## datatype that can exactly represent `sqrt(AME_NELEM) - 1`; only [.red]##basic shapes## are accepted; shape must be `1x1`.

|=======================================================================

[[ztt-detail-mrowid_ew]]
==== `mrowid.ew`

Generates an M-register matrix whose elements contain their row indices. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mrowid.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic takes no input operand and returns destination `dst=M<Tint,1x1>`.

Type constraints: Destination `dst` datatype must be an [.red]##integer## type that can exactly represent `sqrt(AME_NELEM) - 1`. Only [.red]##basic shapes## are accepted, and the shape must be `1x1`.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i,j] = i
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mrowid_ew_i32_1x1(void);
__riscv_ztt_u16_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mrowid_ew_u16_1x1(void);
__riscv_ztt_u64_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mrowid_ew_u64_1x1(void);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mrowid_ew_f32_1x1(); // Result must be integer
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mrowid_ew_i32_1x2(); // Only 1x1 shapes are supported
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mrowid_ew_i32_1x1();

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# result: m0
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m0, a0       # a0 holds the i32 type encoding
mrowid.ew m0          # m0 <- row indices

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# result: m0-m1
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m0, a0       # a0 holds the i32 type encoding
mrowid.ew m0          # m0-m1 <- row indices

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# result: m0-m3
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m0, a0       # a0 holds the i32 type encoding
mrowid.ew m0          # m0-m3 <- row indices
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mcolid_ew]]
==== `mcolid.ew`

Generates an M-register matrix whose elements contain their column indices. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mcolid.ew`.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic takes no input operand and returns destination `dst=M<Tint,1x1>`.

Type constraints: Destination `dst` datatype must be an [.red]##integer## type that can exactly represent `sqrt(AME_NELEM) - 1`. Only [.red]##basic shapes## are accepted, and the shape must be `1x1`.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst[i,j] = j
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolid_ew_i32_1x1(void);
__riscv_ztt_u16_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolid_ew_u16_1x1(void);
__riscv_ztt_u64_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolid_ew_u64_1x1(void);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_f32_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mcolid_ew_f32_1x1(); // Result must be integer
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mcolid_ew_i32_1x2(); // Only 1x1 shapes are supported
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
__riscv_ztt_i32_1x1_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mcolid_ew_i32_1x1();

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# result: m0
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m0, a0       # a0 holds the i32 type encoding
mcolid.ew m0          # m0 <- column indices

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# result: m0-m1
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m0, a0       # a0 holds the i32 type encoding
mcolid.ew m0          # m0-m1 <- column indices

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# result: m0-m3
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m0, a0       # a0 holds the i32 type encoding
mcolid.ew m0          # m0-m3 <- column indices
----

<<<

== 11. Memory

This section includes row/column/strided load-store intrinsics. Whole-register transfers (`mls.1r` / `mss.1r`) are currently for compiler-internal use only.

For every public memory intrinsic in this section, the `base` pointer type must exactly match the C element type corresponding to the matrix element datatype `T`, or use a C-equivalent type. For example, an `i16` load requires `const int16_t *base` or an equivalent `const short *base`, and a `u32` store requires `uint32_t *base` or an equivalent `unsigned int *base`. In addition, `base` must be naturally aligned to `element_bytes = ceil(bits(T) / 8)`. A misaligned `base` raises an address-misaligned exception before any memory access. No additional architectural alignment is required for the transfer size, segment size, or `stride_bytes`. 4-bit datatypes have no directly corresponding C element type, so all public load/store intrinsics in this section reject 4-bit matrix element datatypes.

Acc cannot interact directly with memory. It must first be copied to M through `__riscv_ztt_mcopy_a2m`.

=== 11.1 Load Instructions

[cols=",,,",options="header",]
|=======================================================================
|Spec instruction |Intrinsic |Input/output types |Type constraints
|xref:ztt-detail-mls_rm[`mls.rm`] |+M<T,S> __riscv_ztt_mls_rm_<T>_<S>(const C<T> *base)+
|Memory to M, row-major |`base` has type and alignment requirements (see above); `T` must be a supported datatype; concatenation shapes are allowed; half-register shapes are rejected by the general rules; ``T`` must not be a [.red]##4-bit## datatype

|xref:ztt-detail-mls_cm[`mls.cm`] |+M<T,S> __riscv_ztt_mls_cm_<T>_<S>(const C<T> *base)+
|Memory to M, column-major |`base` has type and alignment requirements (see above); `T` must be a supported datatype; concatenation shapes are allowed; half-register shapes are rejected by the general rules; ``T`` must not be a [.red]##4-bit## datatype

|xref:ztt-detail-mls_st[`mls.st`]
|+M<T,S> __riscv_ztt_mls_st_<T>_<S>(const C<T> *base, size_t stride_bytes)+
|Strided memory to M |`base` has type and alignment requirements (see above); `stride_bytes` is an [.red]##unsigned integer## byte stride and imposes no additional base-address alignment; `T` must be a supported datatype; concatenation shapes are allowed; half-register shapes are rejected by the general rules; ``T`` must not be a [.red]##4-bit## datatype

|xref:ztt-detail-mls_tst[`mls.tst`]
|+M<T,S> __riscv_ztt_mls_tst_<T>_<S>(const C<T> *base, size_t stride_bytes)+
|Transposed strided memory to M |`base` has type and alignment requirements (see above); `stride_bytes` is an [.red]##unsigned integer## byte stride and imposes no additional base-address alignment; `T` must be a supported datatype; concatenation shapes are allowed; half-register shapes are rejected by the general rules; ``T`` must not be a [.red]##4-bit## datatype
|=======================================================================

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mls_rm]]
==== `mls.rm`

Transfers M-register data using the row-major memory layout. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mls.rm`.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic returns destination `dst=M<T,S>` and takes memory address `base` of type `const C<T> *`.

Type constraints: The pointee type of `base` must be [.red]##identical## to the matrix element datatype `T`, or be a C-equivalent type, and `base` must be naturally aligned to `element_bytes = ceil(bits(T) / 8)`. `T` must be a supported datatype. Concatenation shapes are allowed; half-register shapes are rejected by the general type rules. Matrix element datatype ``T`` must not be a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst = mem[base]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mls_rm_i16_1x1(base_i16);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mls_rm_bf16_1x2(base_bf16);
__riscv_ztt_u8_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mls_rm_u8_4x1(base_u8);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mls_rm_i16_1x2((const int32_t *)base_i32); // base element type does not match
__riscv_ztt_i4_1x8_t __riscv_ztt_mls_rm_i4_1x8((const int8_t *)base_i8); // 4-bit memory element types are unsupported
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
const int16_t *base = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mls_rm_i16_1x2(base);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# base: a0
# result: m0
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m0, a2       # a2 holds the i16 type encoding
mls.rm m0, a0        # m0 <- mem[a0]

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# base: a0
# result: m0-m1
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m0, a2       # a2 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m1, a2
mls.rm m0, a0        # m0 <- mem[a0]
csrr a1, amenlen    # a1 = N
mul a1, a1, a1      # a1 = N * N
slli a1, a1, 1      # a1 = N * N * sizeof(i16)
add a1, a0, a1      # a1 is the address of the second logical square
mls.rm m1, a1       # m1 <- mem[a1]

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# base: a0
# result: m0-m3
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m0, a2       # a2 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m2, a2
mls.rm m0, a0        # m0-m1 <- mem[a0]
csrr a1, amenlen    # a1 = N
mul a1, a1, a1      # a1 = N * N
slli a1, a1, 1      # a1 = N * N * sizeof(i16)
add a1, a0, a1      # a1 is the address of the second logical square
mls.rm m2, a1       # m2-m3 <- mem[a1]
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mls_cm]]
==== `mls.cm`

Transfers M-register data using the column-major memory layout. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mls.cm`.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic returns destination `dst=M<T,S>` and takes memory address `base` of type `const C<T> *`.

Type constraints: The pointee type of `base` must be [.red]##identical## to the matrix element datatype `T`, or be a C-equivalent type, and `base` must be naturally aligned to `element_bytes = ceil(bits(T) / 8)`. `T` must be a supported datatype. Concatenation shapes are allowed; half-register shapes are rejected by the general type rules. Matrix element datatype ``T`` must not be a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst = mem[base]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mls_cm_i16_1x1(base_i16);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mls_cm_bf16_1x2(base_bf16);
__riscv_ztt_u8_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mls_cm_u8_4x1(base_u8);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mls_cm_i16_1x2((const int32_t *)base_i32); // base element type does not match
__riscv_ztt_i4_1x8_t __riscv_ztt_mls_cm_i4_1x8((const int8_t *)base_i8); // 4-bit memory element types are unsupported
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
const int16_t *base = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mls_cm_i16_1x2(base);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# base: a0
# result: m0
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m0, a2       # a2 holds the i16 type encoding
mls.cm m0, a0        # m0 <- mem[a0]

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# base: a0
# result: m0-m1
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m0, a2       # a2 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m1, a2
mls.cm m0, a0        # m0 <- mem[a0]
csrr a1, amenlen    # a1 = N
mul a1, a1, a1      # a1 = N * N
slli a1, a1, 1      # a1 = N * N * sizeof(i16)
add a1, a0, a1      # a1 is the address of the second logical square
mls.cm m1, a1       # m1 <- mem[a1]

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# base: a0
# result: m0-m3
# Assembly mapping:

msettyp m0, a2       # a2 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m2, a2
mls.cm m0, a0        # m0-m1 <- mem[a0]
csrr a1, amenlen    # a1 = N
mul a1, a1, a1      # a1 = N * N
slli a1, a1, 1      # a1 = N * N * sizeof(i16)
add a1, a0, a1      # a1 is the address of the second logical square
mls.cm m2, a1       # m2-m3 <- mem[a1]
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mls_st]]
==== `mls.st`

Transfers M-register data using the strided memory layout. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mls.st`.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic returns destination `dst=M<T,S>` and takes memory address `base` of type `const C<T> *` plus an unsigned byte-stride control scalar `size_t stride_bytes`.

Type constraints: The pointee type of `base` must be [.red]##identical## to the matrix element datatype `T`, or be a C-equivalent type, and `base` must be naturally aligned to `element_bytes = ceil(bits(T) / 8)`. `stride_bytes` is an [.red]##unsigned integer## byte stride and imposes no additional base-address alignment. `T` must be a supported datatype. Concatenation shapes are allowed; half-register shapes are rejected by the general type rules. Matrix element datatype ``T`` must not be a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst = mem[base, stride = stride_bytes]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mls_st_i16_1x1(base_i16, (size_t)stride_bytes);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mls_st_bf16_1x2(base_bf16, (size_t)stride_bytes);
__riscv_ztt_u8_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mls_st_u8_4x1(base_u8, (size_t)stride_bytes);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mls_st_i16_1x2((const int32_t *)base_i32, (size_t)16); // base element type does not match
__riscv_ztt_i4_1x8_t __riscv_ztt_mls_st_i4_1x8((const int8_t *)base_i8, (size_t)16); // 4-bit memory element types are unsupported
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
const int16_t *base = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mls_st_i16_1x2(base, 16);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# base: a0
# stride_bytes: 16
# result: m0
# Assembly mapping:

li a2, 16
msettyp m0, a3       # a3 holds the i16 type encoding
mls.st m0, (a0), a2  # m0 <- mem[a0], stride_bytes=16

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# base: a0
# stride_bytes: 16
# result: m0-m1
# Assembly mapping:

li a2, 16
msettyp m0, a3       # a3 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m1, a3
mls.st m0, (a0), a2  # m0 <- mem[a0], stride_bytes=16
csrr a1, amenlen    # a1 = N
slli a1, a1, 1      # a1 = N * sizeof(i16)
add a1, a0, a1      # a1 is the address of the second logical square
mls.st m1, (a1), a2 # m1 <- mem[a1], stride_bytes=16

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# base: a0
# stride_bytes: 16
# result: m0-m3
# Assembly mapping:

li a2, 16
msettyp m0, a3       # a3 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m2, a3
mls.st m0, (a0), a2  # m0-m1 <- mem[a0], stride_bytes=16
csrr a1, amenlen    # a1 = N
slli a1, a1, 1      # a1 = N * sizeof(i16)
add a1, a0, a1      # a1 is the address of the second logical square
mls.st m2, (a1), a2 # m2-m3 <- mem[a1], stride_bytes=16
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mls_tst]]
==== `mls.tst`

Transfers M-register data using the transposed strided memory layout. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mls.tst`.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic returns destination `dst=M<T,S>` and takes memory address `base` of type `const C<T> *` plus an unsigned byte-stride control scalar `size_t stride_bytes`.

Type constraints: The pointee type of `base` must be [.red]##identical## to the matrix element datatype `T`, or be a C-equivalent type, and `base` must be naturally aligned to `element_bytes = ceil(bits(T) / 8)`. `stride_bytes` is an [.red]##unsigned integer## byte stride and imposes no additional base-address alignment. `T` must be a supported datatype. Concatenation shapes are allowed; half-register shapes are rejected by the general type rules. Matrix element datatype ``T`` must not be a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
dst = mem[base, stride = stride_bytes]
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x1_t __riscv_ztt_mls_tst_i16_1x1(base_i16, (size_t)stride_bytes);
__riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mls_tst_bf16_1x2(base_bf16, (size_t)stride_bytes);
__riscv_ztt_u8_4x1_t __riscv_ztt_mls_tst_u8_4x1(base_u8, (size_t)stride_bytes);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t __riscv_ztt_mls_tst_i16_1x2((const int32_t *)base_i32, (size_t)16); // base element type does not match
__riscv_ztt_i4_1x8_t __riscv_ztt_mls_tst_i4_1x8((const int8_t *)base_i8, (size_t)16); // 4-bit memory element types are unsupported
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
const int16_t *base = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t dst = __riscv_ztt_mls_tst_i16_1x2(base, 16);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# base: a0
# stride_bytes: 16
# result: m0
# Assembly mapping:

li a2, 16
msettyp m0, a3        # a3 holds the i16 type encoding
mls.tst m0, (a0), a2  # m0 <- mem[a0], stride_bytes=16

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# base: a0
# stride_bytes: 16
# result: m0-m1
# Assembly mapping:

li a2, 16
msettyp m0, a3        # a3 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m1, a3
mls.tst m0, (a0), a2  # m0 <- mem[a0], stride_bytes=16
csrr a1, amenlen    # a1 = N
mul a1, a1, a2      # a1 = N * stride_bytes
add a1, a0, a1      # a1 is the address of the second logical square
mls.tst m1, (a1), a2 # m1 <- mem[a1], stride_bytes=16

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# base: a0
# stride_bytes: 16
# result: m0-m3
# Assembly mapping:

li a2, 16
msettyp m0, a3        # a3 holds the i16 type encoding
msettyp m2, a3
mls.tst m0, (a0), a2  # m0-m1 <- mem[a0], stride_bytes=16
csrr a1, amenlen    # a1 = N
mul a1, a1, a2      # a1 = N * stride_bytes
add a1, a0, a1      # a1 is the address of the second logical square
mls.tst m2, (a1), a2 # m2-m3 <- mem[a1], stride_bytes=16
----

<<<

[[ztt-detail-mss_rm]]
==== `mss.rm`

Transfers M-register data using the row-major memory layout. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mss.rm`.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic takes memory address `base=C<T> *` and M source operand `src=M<T,S>`, and returns `void`.

Type constraints: The pointee type of `base` must be [.red]##identical## to the matrix element datatype `T`, or be a C-equivalent type, `base` must be non-`const`, and `base` must be naturally aligned to `element_bytes = ceil(bits(T) / 8)`. `T` must be a supported datatype. Concatenation shapes are allowed; half-register shapes are rejected. Matrix element datatype ``T`` must not be a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
mem[base] = src
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]
----
void __riscv_ztt_mss_rm(base_i16, src_i16_1x1);
void __riscv_ztt_mss_rm(base_bf16, src_bf16_1x2);
void __riscv_ztt_mss_rm(base_u8, src_u8_4x1);
----

Non-compliant interfaces:

[source,c]
----
void __riscv_ztt_mss_rm((int32_t *)base_i32, src_i16_1x2); // base element type does not match
void __riscv_ztt_mss_rm((const int16_t *)base_i16, src_i16_1x2); // Store base must not be const
void __riscv_ztt_mss_rm((int8_t *)base_i8, src_i4_1x8); // 4-bit memory element types are unsupported
----

Assembly instruction mapping:

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
int16_t *base = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_mss_rm(base, src);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# base: a0
# src: m0
# Assembly mapping:

mss.rm m0, a0        # mem[a0] <- m0

----------------
# UDS=16
# Operand register allocation:
# base: a0
# src: m0-m1
# Assembly mapping:

mss.rm m0, a0        # mem[a0] <- m0
csrr a1, amenlen    # a1 = N
mul a1, a1, a1      # a1 = N * N
slli a1, a1, 1      # a1 = N * N * sizeof(i16)
add a1, a0, a1      # a1 is the address of the second logical square
mss.rm m1, a1       # mem[a1] <- m1

----------------
# UDS=8
# Operand register allocation:
# base: a0
# src: m0-m3
# Assembly mapping:

mss.rm m0, a0        # mem[a0] <- m0-m1
csrr a1, amenlen    # a1 = N
mul a1, a1, a1      # a1 = N * N
slli a1, a1, 1      # a1 = N * N * sizeof(i16)
add a1, a0, a1      # a1 is the address of the second logical square
mss.rm m2, a1       # mem[a1] <- m2-m3
----

<<<

==== `mss.st`

Transfers M-register data using the strided memory layout. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mss.st`.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic takes memory address `base=C<T> *`, unsigned byte-stride control scalar `stride_bytes` of type `size_t`, and M source operand `src=M<T,S>`, and returns `void`.

Type constraints: The pointee type of `base` must be [.red]##identical## to the matrix element datatype `T`, or be a C-equivalent type, `base` must be non-`const`, and `base` must be naturally aligned to `element_bytes = ceil(bits(T) / 8)`. `stride_bytes` is an [.red]##unsigned integer## byte stride and imposes no additional base-address alignment. `T` must be a supported datatype. Concatenation shapes are allowed; half-register shapes are rejected. Matrix element datatype ``T`` must not be a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
mem[base, stride = stride_bytes] = src
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]

void __riscv_ztt_mss_st(base_bf16, (size_t)stride_bytes, src_bf16_1x2);

/* C intrinsic */

__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_mss_st(base, 16, src);

# Operand register allocation:
# base: a0
# stride_bytes: 16
# src: m0
# Assembly mapping:

add a1, a0, a1      # a1 is the address of the second logical square

==== `mss.tst`

Transfers M-register data using the transposed strided memory layout. It maps directly to the spec instruction `mss.tst`.

C intrinsic data types: The intrinsic takes memory address `base=C<T> *`, unsigned byte-stride control scalar `stride_bytes` of type `size_t`, and M source operand `src=M<T,S>`, and returns `void`.

Type constraints: The pointee type of `base` must be [.red]##identical## to the matrix element datatype `T`, or be a C-equivalent type, `base` must be non-`const`, and `base` must be naturally aligned to `element_bytes = ceil(bits(T) / 8)`. `stride_bytes` is an [.red]##unsigned integer## byte stride and imposes no additional base-address alignment. `T` must be a supported datatype. Concatenation shapes are allowed; half-register shapes are rejected. Matrix element datatype ``T`` must not be a [.red]##4-bit## datatype.

Logical operation:

[source,c]
----
mem[base, stride = stride_bytes] = src
----

User interface examples:

[source,c]

void __riscv_ztt_mss_tst((int8_t *)base_i8, (size_t)16, src_i4_1x8); // 4-bit memory element types are unsupported

[source,c]
----
/* C intrinsic */
int16_t *base = ...;
__riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t src = ...;
__riscv_ztt_mss_tst(base, 16, src);

----------------
# UDS=32
# Operand register allocation:
# base: a0
# stride_bytes: 16
# src: m0
# Assembly mapping:

li a2, 16
mss.tst m0, (a0), a2  # mem[a0] <- m0, stride_bytes=16

# Operand register allocation:

mul a1, a1, a2      # a1 = N * stride_bytes

csrr a1, amenlen    # a1 = N
mul a1, a1, a2      # a1 = N * stride_bytes
add a1, a0, a1      # a1 is the address of the second logical square

== 12. Complete Function Example

The following function loads `A` and `B`, constructs `C` with every element equal to 3, computes `B + C`, performs matrix multiplication, moves the result back to an M value, and stores it to `d`.

=== 12.1 C Source

[source,c]
----
void test(__bf16 *a, int32_t *b, int16_t *d) {
  __riscv_ztt_bf16_1x2_t A = __riscv_ztt_mls_rm_bf16_1x2(a);
  __riscv_ztt_i32_2x1_t B = __riscv_ztt_mls_cm_i32_2x1(b);
  __riscv_ztt_i32_scalar_t x = 3;
  __riscv_ztt_i32_2x1_t C = __riscv_ztt_mbcast_m_x_i32_2x1(x);
  __riscv_ztt_i32_2x1_t BC = __riscv_ztt_madd_ew_i32_2x1(B, C);
  __riscv_ztt_i16_accx2_t acc = __riscv_ztt_mzero_acc_i16_accx2();
  acc = __riscv_ztt_mmulacc_2d_i16_accx2(acc, A, BC);
  __riscv_ztt_i16_1x2_t D = __riscv_ztt_mcopy_a2m_i16_1x2(acc);
  __riscv_ztt_mss_rm(d, D);
}
----

=== 12.2 UDS=32 Assembly Mapping

[source,asm]
----
# a0: a
# a1: b
# a2: d

lui t3, 0x20300
addi t3, t3, 0x110      # bf16 datatype encoding
lui t4, 0x40000
addi t4, t4, 0x20       # i32 datatype encoding
lui t5, 0x40000
addi t5, t5, 0x10       # i16 datatype encoding
li t1, 3

msettyp m0, t3          # Set A's datatype; m0 contains two packed bf16 squares
mls.rm m0, a0           # Load both squares of A from a

msettyp m2, t4          # Set the datatype of B square 0
msettyp m3, t4          # Set the datatype of B square 1
mls.cm m2, a1           # Load B square 0 from b
csrr t0, amenlen
mul t0, t0, t0
slli t0, t0, 2          # N * N * sizeof(i32)
add t0, a1, t0
mls.cm m3, t0           # Load B square 1 from b

msettyp m4, t4          # Set the datatype of C square 0
msettyp m5, t4          # Set the datatype of C square 1
mbcast.m.x m4, t1, t4  # Construct C square 0 with every element equal to 3
mbcast.m.x m5, t1, t4  # Construct C square 1 with every element equal to 3

msettyp m6, t4          # Set the datatype of BC square 0
msettyp m7, t4          # Set the datatype of BC square 1
madd.ew m6, m2, m4     # BC square 0 = B square 0 + C square 0
madd.ew m7, m3, m5     # BC square 1 = B square 1 + C square 1

asettyp acc0, t5        # Set acc square 0's datatype and clear its physical bits
asettyp acc1, t5        # Set acc square 1's datatype and clear its physical bits
mzero.2d.acc acc0       # Set every element of acc square 0 to i16 numeric zero
mzero.2d.acc acc1       # Set every element of acc square 1 to i16 numeric zero
mmulacc.2d acc0, m0, m6 # Both square pairs of A and BC form the inner product accumulated into acc square 0

msettyp m8, t5          # Set D's datatype; m8 contains two packed i16 squares
mmov.m.a m8, acc0      # Pack the two squares in acc0-acc1 into D
mss.rm m8, a2           # Store both squares of D to d

----

=== 12.3 UDS=16 Assembly Mapping

[source,asm]
----
# a0: a
# a1: b
# a2: d

lui t3, 0x20300
addi t3, t3, 0x110      # bf16 datatype encoding
lui t4, 0x40000
addi t4, t4, 0x20       # i32 datatype encoding
lui t5, 0x40000
addi t5, t5, 0x10       # i16 datatype encoding
li t1, 3

msettyp m0, t3          # Set the datatype of A square 0
msettyp m1, t3          # Set the datatype of A square 1
mls.rm m0, a0           # Load A square 0 from a
csrr t0, amenlen
mul t0, t0, t0
slli t0, t0, 1          # N * N * sizeof(__bf16)
add t0, a0, t0
mls.rm m1, t0           # Load A square 1 from a

msettyp m4, t4          # Set the datatype of wide B square 0
msettyp m6, t4          # Set the datatype of wide B square 1
mls.cm m4, a1           # Load B square 0 into m4-m5 from b
csrr t0, amenlen
mul t0, t0, t0
slli t0, t0, 2          # N * N * sizeof(i32)
add t0, a1, t0
mls.cm m6, t0           # Load B square 1 into m6-m7 from b

msettyp m8, t4          # Set the datatype of wide C square 0
msettyp m10, t4         # Set the datatype of wide C square 1
mbcast.m.x m8, t1, t4  # Construct C square 0 with every element equal to 3
mbcast.m.x m10, t1, t4 # Construct C square 1 with every element equal to 3

msettyp m12, t4         # Set the datatype of wide BC square 0
msettyp m14, t4         # Set the datatype of wide BC square 1
madd.ew m12, m4, m8    # BC square 0 = B square 0 + C square 0
madd.ew m14, m6, m10   # BC square 1 = B square 1 + C square 1

asettyp acc0, t5        # Set acc square 0's datatype and clear its physical bits
asettyp acc1, t5        # Set acc square 1's datatype and clear its physical bits
mzero.2d.acc acc0       # Set every element of acc square 0 to i16 numeric zero
mzero.2d.acc acc1       # Set every element of acc square 1 to i16 numeric zero
mmulacc.2d acc0, m0, m12 # Multiply A square 0 by BC square 0 and accumulate into acc square 0
mmulacc.2d acc0, m1, m14 # Multiply A square 1 by BC square 1 and accumulate into acc square 0

msettyp m16, t5         # Set the datatype of D square 0
msettyp m17, t5         # Set the datatype of D square 1
mmov.m.a m16, acc0     # Move acc square 0 to D square 0
mmov.m.a m17, acc1     # Move acc square 1 to D square 1
mss.rm m16, a2          # Store D square 0 to d
csrr t0, amenlen
mul t0, t0, t0
slli t0, t0, 1          # N * N * sizeof(i16)
add t0, a2, t0
mss.rm m17, t0          # Store D square 1 to d

----

=== 12.4 UDS=8 Assembly Mapping

[source,asm]
----
# a0: a
# a1: b
# a2: d

lui t3, 0x20300
addi t3, t3, 0x110      # bf16 datatype encoding
lui t4, 0x40000
addi t4, t4, 0x20       # i32 datatype encoding
lui t5, 0x40000
addi t5, t5, 0x10       # i16 datatype encoding
li t1, 3

msettyp m0, t3          # Set the datatype of wide A square 0
msettyp m2, t3          # Set the datatype of wide A square 1
mls.rm m0, a0           # Load A square 0 into m0-m1 from a
csrr t0, amenlen
mul t0, t0, t0
slli t0, t0, 1          # N * N * sizeof(__bf16)
add t0, a0, t0
mls.rm m2, t0           # Load A square 1 into m2-m3 from a

msettyp m4, t4          # Set the datatype of wide B square 0
msettyp m8, t4          # Set the datatype of wide B square 1
mls.cm m4, a1           # Load B square 0 into m4-m7 from b
csrr t0, amenlen
mul t0, t0, t0
slli t0, t0, 2          # N * N * sizeof(i32)
add t0, a1, t0
mls.cm m8, t0           # Load B square 1 into m8-m11 from b

msettyp m12, t4         # Set the datatype of wide C square 0
msettyp m16, t4         # Set the datatype of wide C square 1
mbcast.m.x m12, t1, t4 # Construct C square 0 with every element equal to 3
mbcast.m.x m16, t1, t4 # Construct C square 1 with every element equal to 3

msettyp m20, t4         # Set the datatype of wide BC square 0
msettyp m24, t4         # Set the datatype of wide BC square 1
madd.ew m20, m4, m12   # BC square 0 = B square 0 + C square 0
madd.ew m24, m8, m16   # BC square 1 = B square 1 + C square 1

asettyp acc0, t5        # Set acc square 0's datatype and clear its physical bits
asettyp acc1, t5        # Set acc square 1's datatype and clear its physical bits
mzero.2d.acc acc0       # Set every element of acc square 0 to i16 numeric zero
mzero.2d.acc acc1       # Set every element of acc square 1 to i16 numeric zero
mmulacc.2d acc0, m0, m20 # Multiply A square 0 by BC square 0 and accumulate into acc square 0
mmulacc.2d acc0, m2, m24 # Multiply A square 1 by BC square 1 and accumulate into acc square 0

msettyp m0, t5          # Set the datatype of wide D square 0
msettyp m2, t5          # Set the datatype of wide D square 1
mmov.m.a m0, acc0      # Move acc square 0 to D square 0
mmov.m.a m2, acc1      # Move acc square 1 to D square 1
mss.rm m0, a2           # Store D square 0 to d
csrr t0, amenlen
mul t0, t0, t0
slli t0, t0, 1          # N * N * sizeof(i16)
add t0, a2, t0
mss.rm m2, t0           # Store D square 1 to d
----
